## Summary

Interaction is the verb layer: it decides what one press of `interact` means for the lit, in-reach object the player faces, and carries that out as real changes to the ship through the Life-Support Graph's mutators, with channelled work (patch, cut, cap, crank, field check), taps, carry and throw, and the friendly-harm act gated on the actor's own sanity. Read it if you build, test or design anything the player does to the ship with their hands, any prompt or progress bar, or any system whose lies the player must be able to check: its central rule is that a true reason for a failure never reaches the player faster than the work takes, so checking costs time, light and noise and the world, not the prompt, answers. It is the verb table of record for V1 to V12, 41 numbered rules, 14 formulas (D.1b added by the 2026-10-27 user rulings), 144 acceptance rows *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: was "127", which was already stale at 128; the section now has 144 after the review's 16 added rows)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: now 148, AC-IA-143 to 146 added)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: now 153, AC-IA-147 to 151 added; AC-IA-117 to 120 neighbour-owned)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: now 156, AC-IA-152 to 154 added)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, second pass: now 157, AC-IA-18c added; 33c is an in-row sub-row)*, and it names every contract it needs from systems that are still being designed.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer (designed), Player Controller & Input (designed), Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query (designed), Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera (designed, soft), Life-Support Graph (designed), Item & Module Database (designed, soft), Suit & Equipment (designed in this run), Ship Section & Layout Data Model (designed in this run), Sanity & Hallucination Rules (undesigned), Attention (undesigned, soft)`

## Overview

Interaction is the verb layer of the game: the simulation-side system that decides what a press of the single `interact` input means for the one object the player is facing, and then carries that meaning out as a real change to the world. It has a data layer and a player-facing effect, and both are stated here. **The data layer:** for each focused object and each tool in hand it *declares* a command type and a mode (`TAP`, `HOLD_CONFIRM` or `CHANNEL`); it *registers* the command types whose effect is its own (patch, salvage cut, port cap, crank, breaker, valve, door, top-up, pickup, drop, glowstick throw, field check, the friendly-harm act, cancel) and calls the Life-Support Graph's Class A mutators M6 to M9 and M11 to M13 (M10 has no MVP verb) for the player verbs V1, V3, V4, V5, V6, V8, V9 and V12, and the Class B M16 for V7 top-up (admitted to the Graph's MVP list by the 2026-10-27 user ruling X5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; it owns channel progress, the partial-progress ledger, the FULL-repair part reservation, the loose-item table, the closed domain reason set with its immediate-versus-deferred classification, and one SIM block (`interaction.state`) with one step hook (`interaction.step`). **The player-facing effect:** the player walks into their own light until a pipe, a valve, a breaker, a door or a salvageable module is close enough to touch, a prompt plate (`E · Patch`) appears only because that object is lit and in reach, and one press starts a stationary job: a linear bar fills while the tool spits and the web listens, any step away lets go of the job, and the world — the hiss thinning, the room warming, the arc stopping — answers a beat behind the player's hands. Nothing the prompt, the bar or a rejection says ever answers whether the module is really broken.

The one-sentence version: *Interaction is how a player puts their hands on the ship, and the price of doing so — light, noise, standing still, time — is exactly the price Pillars 3 and 4 need acting to cost.* Two rules carry the whole design. **The lit-only rule:** a verb is possible only on an object that is both in reach and lit (`in_reach` from the Player Controller GDD, `lit(ref)` from the Emitter State GDD), so going dark is a trade, never a free dodge of the web's attention. **The cost rule for truth:** a reason that depends on a module's body state (its hp, state, forced mode or served power — the channel that Pillar 4 may corrupt) is never returned at the instant of the press; the command is accepted, the full job runs, and only then does it resolve to an outcome (`nothing changed`, `not repairable`, `still hot`). An instant honest rejection on a device the player merely believes is broken or fixed would be a free lie-detector (Command & Simulation Layer CR-5, row 12), and it is forbidden by construction. Reasons that depend only on geometry, light, the actor's own stock and state, or the consequence channel (out of range, not lit, no tool, busy, obstructed) are honest and immediate.

**What the game loses without it.** Without a single, declared, lit-gated verb layer there is no cost for acting, so Pillar 3 has nothing to price; there is no cheap check for a hallucination to be answered by, so Pillar 4's "checking costs time, risk or trust" degenerates into either a free oracle (an instant `INVALID_TARGET`) or no verification at all; the Graph's twelve verbs have no caller and the MVP slice (a leaking cryo bay, a SCRAMmed reactor on derelict B, a salvageable scrubber) cannot be played; and the friendly-harm act, the one place a hallucination can kill, has no gate. The Controller walks and the Emitter lights; this system is where the player's decisions touch the world.

**Index wording, reconciled.** `systems-index.md` (enumeration row 8, design order 10, Gameplay, Feature, MVP, effort M, five dependents) describes the system as "Inspect, cut, weld, open, carry, run a field check; the act-only-on-lit rule uses the light-exposure query (prompts drawn only for lit objects is the UI side, 7.0); hold-to-confirm on destructive actions; proximity focus for gamepad. Falsification checks reach Sanity as commands from here. Also owns the friendly-harm act." Read against the designed neighbours: **inspect** is a permission rule only (`inspect` is a presentation context switch; the card belongs to In-World Instruments, IA-38); **cut** is the module-salvage cut (V8), while a *section* cut is Modular Ship Assembly's; **weld** (V9) is *declared* here and *registered* by Assembly (IA-3); **open** is the door verb V4; **carry** is `pickup` and `drop` (IA-28); proximity focus and the reach sector are the Controller's, and Interaction consumes the single focus it hands over; the friendly-harm act is an ordinary tool verb under Sanity's gate, with no dedicated damage or combat system and no reward path (IA-31). `interact` is an **input action**, never a command type — the kernel text that lists it as a command is a stale token routed for refresh (Cross-References).

**Pillar position and scope.** Primary pillars P3 The Web Watches and P4 Sanity Rewrites Reality; serves P1, P2 and P5; guards the anti-pillars (not a combat game: tools repel and nothing is attacked; no intentional PvP; not a freeform builder: welding is functional and modules are pre-shaped, so Interaction only declares which button starts them). The MVP is single-seat and single-player: the friendly-harm act and the revive verb are designed, registered or declared, and testable only with stub actors; co-op contention is a Vertical Slice B seam.

**Neighbour status (Glob of `design/gdd/` on 2026-10-26: 14 files).** Designed and read: Command & Simulation Layer, Life-Support Graph, Player Controller & Input, Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera, Emitter State & Light-Exposure Query (designed in this run); also on disk although not in the index's Interaction edge list: Item & Module Database, Failure & Trail System, Save/Load & Versioned Format. **Designed in this run and read:** Suit & Equipment (SE-7, SE-13, SE-22 to SE-24, SE-40, SE-41), Ship Section & Layout Data Model (LY-9 to LY-11, LY-22, LY-23) and 2D Lighting & Occlusion (which names Interaction only for the glowstick landing tick, a Soft edge from its side); all three carry `Status: Designed` on disk (re-globbed at the revision stage), and every adopted name was grep-confirmed in them. **Still undesigned, so every contract below with them stays provisional:** Sanity & Hallucination Rules, Attention System, Modular Ship Assembly, Boarding & Salvage, In-World Instruments & Inspect View, HUD & Suit Layer, Perception Filter, Horror Entities, Derelict Authoring, Web Map, Adaptive Audio and Debug Overlay; Ship Body & Interior Physics also has no GDD, so the foot boxes Interaction reads from Body's read view are provisional (Body undesigned). Sibling GDDs were designed concurrently; the revision stage re-globbed and confirmed the three Designed statuses. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the two sentences above are historical, as of 2026-10-26. A re-glob on 2026-10-27 finds 26 GDDs on disk, all `Status: Designed`: Sanity, Attention, Modular Ship Assembly, Boarding & Salvage, In-World Instruments, HUD & Suit Layer, Perception Filter, Horror Entities, Derelict Authoring, Web Map, Chart Table, Adaptive Audio, Debug Overlay and Ship Body & Interior Physics all exist. The only neighbour still undesigned is Onboarding (Cryo Bay), systems-index row 36, Vertical Slice. Where this GDD still says "undesigned" or "provisional" for one of those systems, read it as "Designed, contract with Interaction not yet re-verified"; the Dependencies and Interactions tables carry the corrected status, and a confirming consistency run is owed.)*

**MVP slice.** MVP ships: V1 `begin_patch` (PATCH and FULL, and the self-patch), V3 `set_breaker`, V4 `set_opening` (doors), V5 `set_valve`, V6 `set_opening` (vent, HOLD_CONFIRM), V8 `begin_cut` + `begin_cap`, V9 `begin_weld`, the player seam cut `begin_seam_cut` and `install_module` (declared; Assembly registers the three; X1: weld, player seam cut and install are all MVP) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, V12 `begin_crank`, `pickup`, `drop`, `throw_glowstick`, `field_check` SCAN, `cancel_channel`; `inspect` bound and dormant unless the card ships. V7 `top_up` is MVP *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D34 / X5, V7 is admitted to the MVP verb list as a deliberate O2 TAP verb; LS-26 is amended on the Graph's side, Suit's `TOPUP_MODE` is VERB and `CELL_ENABLED` is on, the story is unblocked, and AC-IA-1 / AC-IA-41 are unconditional)*. Vertical Slice A: V2, V10, V11; HEFT and PALETTE checks. Vertical Slice B: `begin_harm` live, `begin_revive`, two-seat contention, a crewmate's channel as the human channel. Against the Graph's LS-26 slice (home-ship 5 / derelict A 4 / derelict B 5 compartments, 8 faults), this GDD's reading of which verb answers which live mode (LSG:228 lists the verbs and the live modes without pairing them) is: LEAK -> V1; SHORT/OVERLOAD -> V3; OVERHEAT/SCRAM and FREEZE -> V12; BREACH -> V4/V6 and V9; STARVE -> V5/V3; SEVERED -> V8 `begin_cap`.

## Player Fantasy

**Framing: direct.** Emitter State took an indirect framing because the player never sees "exposure". Interaction is the opposite: the player presses `interact` every minute, reads its prompt, watches its bar and hears its cues; it is the most-touched system in the game after the body. An indirect framing would hide the one moment the fantasy is made of — the hands on the object. The headline, in one sentence: *every verb is a question put to the ship with your own hands, paid for in light, noise and time, and answered by the world, never by the prompt.* (Candidate framings considered: "Reach for It" — recommended as headline, because the verb is the verification economy that Pillar 4 promises; "Spend to Act" — kept as the price layer but rejected as the headline, because it credits Emitter State's and Attention's fantasy, not this system's; "The Hands That Keep It Breathing" — kept as texture, rejected as the headline because it drifts toward a repair-progression fantasy that the Graph forbids, LS-23; "The Toolbelt" — rejected outright, because it breeds a tool tree and a weapon-tree-by-another-name.)

**The emotion, named: the honest labour of doubt.** The power is not "I can fix anything"; it is "I can find out" — the small, stubborn competence of a technician who cannot trust their eyes but can still put their hands on the thing and wait for the room to tell them. The fear is the inverse: the job takes three seconds in the light, standing still, making noise, and the dark does not wait.

> Nothing aboard is fixed by looking at it. You walk into your own light until the pipe is close enough to touch, and only then does the plate come up: `E · Patch`. It never tells you the pipe is broken. It tells you what your hands can do.
>
> You hold. You do not move, because moving lets go. The bar crawls; the welder spits; the hiss thins — not when the bar says so, but when the metal says so, a beat behind your hands. You can leave at any moment, and what you did to the pipe stays done; a half-made cut waits ten seconds for you; a patch you come back to costs a full bar for what is left. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "whatever you did stays done" was true only for the first half of a repair. The hp already raised is permanent; the progress of a cut, cap, crank or field check expires after `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`; a resumed PATCH recomputes over the full bar (D.1), so the tail costs a fresh bar. Measured in AC-IA-73b; if hide-then-return proves common in playtest, raise `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` toward 1200, Open Question 40.)*
>
> And when your eyes have started lying, this is still the way through. The gauge may say fine; the pipe may look whole. Put your hands on it and wait. The room will get warm or it will not. The work is the one question the web cannot answer for you.

**Anchor moment (solo; the MVP is single-player).** *The first patch*, in the home-ship cryo bay — the Onboarding beat "see the leak, follow it to the source, fix it, watch the gauge agree with the pipe" (`game-concept.md`).
1. Frost on the deck plates, a thin hiss at a coolant pipe. The pipe is outside the beam: no prompt. You turn; the edge of the chest lamp crosses the pipe's anchor and the pipe comes out of the black (the "honest edge" of Emitter State); the plate `E · Patch` follows after the HUD's show debounce (about 217 ms, 13 ticks; up to about 717 ms, 43 ticks, at a breach, when the honest-grid arbiter holds it, HS-45) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier figures, 150 and 650 ms, disagreed with `PROMPT_SHOW_DEBOUNCE_TICKS` 13 and the 43-tick breach figure used in the Visual/Audio table, Game Feel and AC-IA-134)* and, the other way round, goes the same render frame the pipe's `lit(ref)` is lost. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the plate no longer claims to appear "on the same frame" as the object. Hide is immediate; show may be late, and a late prompt omits a hint but never lies. Reasons: the show debounce plus the 20-tick spacing, and `lit(ref)` is a per-subject, per-tick, hysteretic bit while the object's visibility comes from the per-pixel light, so the two can differ by a frame at the rim. Latency is measured in AC-IA-134; V-3 is reworded.)* Further than `REACH_PX` away: still no prompt. One step closer, inside the `REACH_HALF_ANGLE_DEG` arc about your facing: now it prompts.
2. You hold `interact`. The acknowledgement is immediate — the hand never waits for the simulation. A linear bar with a needle-mark fills over `PATCH_CHANNEL_TICKS` (180 ticks, 3 s, [PLACEHOLDER]). The welder is a loud tool: the web is listening while you work. Your facing locks; the beam stays on the pipe because the lamp is on your chest and your hands are free (user decision, the Pandorum lamp).
3. The pipe's true hp is 3000. Every `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` the job raises it by a real increment (D.1 gives 100 bp per step). After the tenth step the true hp crosses `LEAK_HP`; the Graph reads that on its next run, so the hiss stops one Graph run later — at most 100 ms behind your hands, honestly. The frost stops spreading. The gauge on the wall agrees with the pipe.
4. The completion cue. The pipe is now patched (Graph LS-23: `hp_cap` `PATCH_CAP_BP`, permanently degraded; it carries the consequence-channel weep `27 PATCH_WEEP`, Failure & Trail FT-32, in place of WORN) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, and it will re-leak. A patch is a promise with a date on it; a FULL repair needs a part you do not have yet. The cure is deeper (Pillar 5). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the date is not `PATCH_DECAY_S`. The Graph (LS-23) holds a patch for `PATCH_DECAY_S` (240 s) and only then wears it down, 100 bp per 10 s, so a patch that ran to its 6000 cap re-leaks about 200 s after the decay starts, about 440 s after completion; a patch cancelled at the hiss (hp just above `LEAK_HP`, about a third of the bar) re-leaks about 10 s after the decay starts, about 250 s in all. The remaining two thirds of the bar buy about 190 s of extra life that no tell shows. Whether "cure now or make it last" is meant to be a visible choice is Open Question 41.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D39, default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): it IS a visible choice. The patched pipe's bolted patch plate wears a weld bead of one, two or three segments by its banked hp (D.1b; the Module-Body Presenter, In-World Instruments IW-18, owns the plate and selects the bead frame; Failure & Trail's weep is a separate static mark with no length axis) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, so a patch cancelled at the hiss wears a short bead and one run to its cap a full one: the player can read, with no number, that the longer bar bought a longer-lived patch. The patch curve is not reshaped. AC-IA-143.)*

*Stepping away.* Replay the patch, but at 1.5 s a creak overhead makes you step back. Any move intent aborts the job; the bar snaps empty with the *interrupted* cue. But the fifteen steps already applied are real: hp 4500, the leak cleared. You lost the rest of the job, not the work done. **"I can leave now" is true in every state**, and leaving is never punished retroactively. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: stated honestly, what survives is the hp already raised. Coming back to the pipe, the re-begin recomputes over the full bar (D.1), so the tail of the job costs a fresh 3 s; coming back to a half-made cut, cap, crank or field check within `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` resumes it, and after ten seconds it is gone. Leaving is never punished by taking back what was done; it does cost the time to finish.)*

*The dead reactor (derelict B; texture, rests on the verb-table reading of V12).* A SCRAMmed reactor. Derelict B's first crank succeeds as authored (Derelict Authoring's dead-reactor beat); the beat is the re-SCRAM: skip the `pb_leak` patch, let it SCRAM again, and crank it straight away. A bare-hand channel, `CRANK_CHANNEL_TICKS` (240 ticks, 4 s): stationary, loud, lit. The bar fills to the end — and the reactor does not start. The *nothing happened* cue; the Graph's physical answer, `BLOCKED(STILL_HOT)`, passed through unchanged. You spent four seconds of noise to learn the reactor is still hot — and could have learned it more cheaply from the heat shimmer and the casing's hiss, which belong to the consequence channel. The game rewards the player who reads the world before they reach for it.

*The breaker (the Pillar 3 price).* On the home-ship you close a breaker (V3, a tap). No hold, no confirm: the inrush flickers the corridor lamps (`INRUSH_PM`, `INRUSH_STEPS`, cited from the Graph) and the web saw that. The cost of the experiment is the inrush itself, not a dialog.

*Co-op beats* — a crewmate already at the object as the visible reason for `BUSY`, the revive channel on a downed crewmate, the friendly-harm act — are Vertical Slice B. The MVP fantasy rests on your own hands.

**Pillar quotes the section carries.** Pillar 4, verbatim: "No lie is unfalsifiable: the truth is always available through the physical world, a second instrument, a saner crewmate, the human channel, or time — and checking costs time, risk or trust." The deferred outcome (IA-12) is that sentence made into a mechanic: the check is *time* (the full bar), *risk* (standing still, lit, noisy) and never free. Pillar 3: "The players' light, noise, power use and restraint raise or lower its attention… Restraint and stealth are real strategies" — every channel is lit, stationary and noisy; restraint means choosing which jobs to do. Pillar 1: "A pipe may *look* fixed, but the room still gets cold, the air still thins, the pressure still drops" — every verb's result is a consequence-channel change; Interaction writes none of it itself (Failure & Trail, row 15: "no data crosses"). Pillar 2: "Every breach is preceded by a tell a careful player could have perceived" — the player's own verbs are exogenous (LS-13) and carry their own tell (a severed stub's hiss, the inrush flicker), and a verb never outruns a tell because movement always cancels. Pillar 5: "losing the home-ship or a crewmate costs ship and body, never knowledge" — a patch is free but decays, a FULL repair costs a part that comes from deeper, and nothing a verb teaches is lost on death (restart from the last autosave, Save/Load SL-7).

**Design tests.** *Primary:* when a choice makes an action cheaper to do than to be sure of, make the checking cheaper than the lie, never free; when a prompt could be drawn where `lit(ref)` is false, never draw it — a prompt must not lie by omission. *Subordinate:* (1) **"I can leave now" (P2)** — when a verb could hold the player in place versus let any move release them, release them; fails if any verb, at any sanity, rhythm or Attention level, holds the body. (2) **The prompt never answers (P4)** — when a prompt, cue or rejection could reveal module-body truth at zero time, risk or trust versus only after the full channel, after the channel; fails if a player can learn a pipe's real state by tapping and releasing, or by pressing a FULL repair, cancelling at any tick and reading the part stock, the weight cue or the reserved bucket. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: and by a one-block PATCH tap-and-cancel, whose Graph state, descriptors and noise are identical at hp 10000, 5999, 5000 and 4000 with `hp_cap` 10000, because the PATCH guard of IA-17 makes no M7 call there; AC-IA-26.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: from the first credited block on, a cancel, abort or restore consumes the part in both truth branches, so believing a lie and acting on it costs a part, which is honest under P4; only a channel that runs to its end may return the part, IA-18.)* (3) **No stat penalties from interactions (P4 secondary)** — a −10 % repair speed at low sanity versus a false gauge: the false gauge; channel durations are fixed per verb and read no sanity, Attention, rhythm or device. (4) **The hands work only in the light (P3)** — act without light versus require light: require light; exactly three listed object exceptions (actor verbs, `throw_glowstick` and the self-patch, have no object to light, IA-6) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. (5) **Physical answer, not message (P1)** — a "repaired!" toast versus the hiss stopping: the hiss; completion is a cue, the result is the world.

**How the fantasy deepens under hallucination — and must not break.** The rule of the section: *the prompt never answers; the work does.* Prompt existence is a function of the object's declared kind, the tool in hand, `lit` and `in_reach` only (IA-11), so the prompt says the same thing to a sane and to a hallucinating player. What hallucination changes is what the player *believes before* reaching, and the work is how they find out.
- **A hallucinated-broken, truly healthy pipe (false system state):** the sprite and gauge read broken. The prompt appears (it is kind-based); the bar runs the full 180 ticks, pixel- and sound-identical to a real patch; the Graph receives zero mutator calls; the job resolves `PRECONDITION(NOTHING_TO_REPAIR)` and the *nothing happened* cue. "Three seconds of noise for nothing — it wasn't broken. My eyes are going." The check cost time, noise and light; it was never free.
- **A hallucinated-fixed, truly leaking pipe (hp 2000):** looks whole, gauge green. The prompt still appears; if the player patches anyway, the real increments land against 2000. The cold is the truth (P1): the cautious player who patches a "fixed" pipe is rewarded by physics, not by a message.
- **A phantom entity:** no subject, so never lit, never focused, never prompted; `interact` with no focus is a no-op with no command and no pre-play; the only sound is the Adapter's local `cue_input_ignored`, identical for every no-focus press, so it tells a phantom from empty space no better than silence *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: conformed to IA-4 and the Controller's ignored-input cue)*. **A claim correction the section must carry:** reaching for a thing falsifies *objects*, not monsters — real HORROR subjects also take no verb at MVP, so a missing prompt is not evidence about a monster either way (true at MVP; in Vertical Slice B IA-31's precondition keeps the plate from discriminating real horrors from crewmates drawn as horrors). That symmetry is by design; the truth about monsters lives in the footprint of the world (sound, frost, hull stress), not in the hands.
- **Phantom salvage (a real registered `junk_` item):** every verb works normally, because it is a real item. The truth is the weight cue (Suit), the scanner field check (`FIELD_CHECK_TICKS`, scanner in hand) or the trip home — the cheaper check that Pillar 4(d) requires.
- **A crewmate rendered as a horror (Vertical Slice B):** for an actor whose Sanity level is below the harm threshold the prompt reads the equipped tool's ordinary verb (`E · Cut`), never a target noun; a sane actor gets no prompt and a forced attempt is rejected `NOT_ELIGIBLE`. The horror is that the hands work. (Two rulings travel with this bullet. IA-31's **blocking Vertical Slice B precondition**: the plate must not tell a crewmate drawn as a horror from a real horror, so real horrors carry a `repel` declaration with the same `verb_name_key` and the eligibility bit is the class-(c) render level. And the **self-knowledge ruling**: the plate's presence is honest knowledge of the actor's *own* sanity, which Pillar 4 allows.)

**Reference techniques — adopt / reject.**

| Reference | Technique | Verdict | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| Project Zomboid | Timed contextual actions on a linear bar, cancelled by moving; no skill-check minigame | **Adopt** | Exactly the channel model (IA-14); the designer's touchstone |
| Project Zomboid | Skill levels that shorten action time or cut failure chance | **Reject** | Progression from repair (the Graph writes none, LS-23); would break bar-length identity between a real and a hallucinated job |
| Pandorum | Chest-mounted, hands-free lamp: both hands work while lit | **Adopt** | User decision; the beam stays on the job and while carrying |
| Barotrauma | Stationary hold-to-repair where interruption is the player's choice, under pressure | **Adopt (the stationary hold)** | The breach-rhythm pressure of Pillar 2 |
| Barotrauma | Repair minigame (sliders, wiring) | **Reject** | A minigame is a QTE in disguise (no QTE anywhere) and a UI surface that could leak module-body truth |
| Generic action games | Round or dial-shaped progress and confirm indicators; verb menus | **Reject** | Art bible 3.4 / 7.3 and HR-14: the bar is linear with a needle-mark; one contextual action |
| Dead Space | Diegetic UI on the character and in the world | **Reject for prompts; adopt the spirit for results** | The prompt is explicitly meta (art bible 7.0) so it can never be corrupted into a lie; results are diegetic (hiss, cold) |
| Prompts that name the outcome ("Repair broken pipe") | Verb plus object state | **Reject** | A free oracle under Pillar 4(b); the prompt never names condition |

**Tone and language.** Use: reach, hands, hold, the bar, step away, let go, patch, cut, cap, crank, close the breaker, open the valve, throw a glowstick, check, in the light, the edge, chest lamp, beam, go dark, the hiss thins, the room warms, nothing happened. Avoid in player-facing text and in fantasy prose: attack, strike, hit, damage, kill, "target" as an on-screen noun, "failed" or "can't" as a rejection string (the kernel never displays strings), "success!", "repaired!", minigame, QTE, combo, skill, level up, XP, "health" for modules (say the pipe holds or leaks), flashlight or torch for the beam, visibility, stealth, detection, hidden. Prompt grammar is locked by the art bible: keybind plus verb, `E · Patch`, never condition, never target.

**Anti-pillar guard rails (stated as fiction as well as rule).**
1. *Not a combat game.* There is no attack input. The friendly-harm act is an ordinary tool verb on a crewmate, gated on the *actor's* authoritative Sanity level, lethal against a victim who cannot or does not respond, with the moment of death being Suit's rule (IA-31; "death is a bitch. It happens." — user), with no reward, no counter, no XP and no attribution inside Interaction: one `record_harm_event` call is made to Sanity (registry: `record_harm_event`), which attributes it to the web. Horrors take no verb at MVP; tools repel through Horror and Attention, and nothing is attacked.
2. *No intentional PvP.* A sane actor sees no prompt on a crewmate and is rejected `NOT_ELIGIBLE` if they force the attempt.
3. *Not a freeform builder.* Weld, section cut and install are pre-shaped and Assembly's; Interaction only declares which button starts them.
4. *No meter, no readout, no tooltip.* No interaction stat, no weight readout, no numeric or bar sanity readout, no "you can't do that because…" string; a rejection is a cue, never a shake. **One ruled exception (CD-GDD-ALIGN, recorded):** the actor's *own* sanity level is honest self-knowledge, which Pillar 4 allows. The concept makes the harm gate "verifiably below threshold" and gives the attacker "a visible sanity sign" (CON:252), so the presence of the friendly-harm prompt, and the immediate `NOT_ELIGIBLE` on a forced attempt, are a permitted one-bit statement about the actor's own state and about nothing in the world: no module, no figure, no hallucination class. The prompt is not a gauge of anything else (IA-31).
5. *No reward path for work.* No repair score, salvage bonus or faster hands; the reward of a patch is a warm room.

**Trade-off accepted.** Some jobs will be three seconds of noise for nothing: the deferred outcome is the price of a sanity system that is not a free lie-detector. We accept that a minority of players will call it "the game wasted my time" in exchange for the majority learning "my eyes are lying." The mitigation is diegetic — the consequence channel (cold, hiss, draught, heat shimmer) lets a careful player decide *before* reaching; reading the world is always cheaper than reaching for it. **We will know this was right if** playtesters describe a failed job as "it wasn't really broken" or "it was still too hot" rather than "the game wouldn't let me" or "bug"; no playtester asks for a "why can't I" message and, asked how they knew a pipe was really fixed, they answer with a physical sign (the cold stopped, the hiss stopped) rather than a UI element; players step away mid-channel when they hear something and do not report feeling punished; and a review finds no channel duration, reason class or prompt rule whose inputs include sanity, Attention, rhythm or device. **Pass/fail threshold:** the attribution half of these criteria is a playtest row, AC-IA-73b (at least 7 of 8 testers who receive a deferred outcome explain it with a physical cause rather than "bug" or "the game wouldn't let me"); a miss re-opens this trade-off, first by strengthening the consequence channel's legibility and never by adding a reason string. The cost to Challenge is therefore measured, not assumed. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, two additions. (1) The hands answer the world; the scanner answers Sanity. A `field_check` is the cheap way to debunk a lie about an object and is deliberately priced (a tool in hand, `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS`, TOOL noise); a hand job answers what is true of the pipe, at the price of its whole bar. The scanner therefore dominates hand-checking as a way to resolve a hallucination, and that is accepted: it resolves the lie, not the leak (Open Question 32 keeps the repair completion from debunking). (2) The Pillar 3 price of working has to be perceivable: a TOOL push with no tell is a hidden stat. The tell is Attention's (its noise-driven tell and consequence, the owner named in IA-33) voiced by Adaptive Audio; Interaction supplies only the class. AC-IA-136 is the playtest row: a consequence within about 10 s of a TOOL push, named by most testers.)*

**What this section does not claim.** The cost of light (Emitter State) and of noise (Attention); the body's honesty and the focus model (Controller); the physics of the hiss, the cold and `BLOCKED` reasons (Graph); trails (Failure & Trail); vitals, charges, the weight cue and death (Suit); what a scanner reveals, the harm threshold, scars and the victim's tell (Sanity, Perception Filter); prompt and bar pixels (HUD); cue sound design (Adaptive Audio). Interaction owns which verb a press means, when it is accepted, how long it takes, and when the answer arrives.

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

*Conventions.* Rules are numbered `IA-n` (Interaction Act); formulas `D.n` (Formulas section); invariants `I-n`; acceptance criteria `AC-IA-n`; diagnostics `IA_*`. Durations are in ticks (`TICK_RATE` 60); the one seconds-denominated knob, `FULL_JOB_GRACE_S`, converts with the kernel's `ticks_for_seconds`. "Class A" and "Class B" are the Graph's mutator classes (LS-18: Class A is command-backed and paired with a pure `check_*`; Class B is unconditional, clamps and never rejects). Every contract with a system that has no GDD on disk is tagged `(provisional — X undesigned)`; the names given for those contracts are proposals the neighbour must adopt or replace. A contract with a designed sibling is tagged `(Suit & Equipment, Designed — SE-n)` or `(Layout, Designed — LY-n)` and uses the names that GDD publishes (the owner of an interface is the system that publishes it). Where an owner GDD spells only some names of a pattern, an inferred name is tagged as inferred at its first use. Upstream constants and formulas (`in_reach`, `lit(ref)` hysteresis, `fairness_gate`, `repair_part_for`, `salvage_yield`, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` and the rest of the cited list under Tuning Knobs) are used by registry name where registered, else by the owner GDD's own identifier (Cross-References section 5 lists the 12 names not yet registered), and never restated; where this GDD needs a different value that is a CONFLICT, surfaced in Open Questions, never a silent change. Each rule names what decides it ("*Decides:*") and, where one existed, the alternative considered. Where the four specialist proposals disagreed and the advisor rulings were silent, the choice is marked "(orchestrator ruling: …)". Where a specialist proposed a departure from an advisor ruling and the checkpoint rejected it (DEP-1, DEP-3), the ruling stays in the text and the departure is recorded in Open Questions as a closed alternative with the checkpoint reason.

**Group A — Ownership, declaration, focus and the lit-only rule**

**IA-1 Interaction is the verb layer, not a doer of everything.** Interaction (a) declares, per focused object and equipped tool, which command type and mode a press of `interact` means; (b) registers the command types whose effect is its own (IA-3); (c) owns channel progress, partial-progress persistence, pickup, drop, throw, the field check, the friendly-harm act and revive; (d) is the caller of the Graph's Class A mutators M6 to M9 and M11 to M13 (M10 has no MVP verb) for the player verbs, of the Class B M16 for V7 top-up, and of Emitter State's subject registration (E6, E7, E8) and `spawn_deployable` (E3); (e) publishes exactly three Class B mutators (`sync_section`, `place_loose`, and `open_channel` for owner-registered channels, IA-35), two read views (`focus_view`, `channel_view`) and one pure derived read (`last_salvage_T()`, IA-25), and nothing else. It never draws, never subscribes to results and never calls presentation (kernel CR-10); it uses no RNG and no wall-clock (every outcome is a deterministic function of block state, true Graph state and the tick); it holds no scene-tree reference (H10). *Decides:* advisor R1, guard G1, kernel CR-10.

**IA-2 The declaration table is data.** One row per (object class, tool requirement) under `assets/data/interaction/verbs/` — the folder the Controller's lint already scans. Fields: `verb_id` (a V-id or an Interaction id); `object_class` (BREAKER_PANEL, VALVE, DOOR_FACE, VENT_HATCH, REPAIRABLE, SALVAGE_MODULE, CRANK_MACHINE, WELD_SOCKET, STUB, LOOSE_ITEM, SWITCH_FIXTURE, ACTOR, ACTOR_SELF, TOPUP_PORT, INSTRUMENT, HARDPOINT, CONSOLE (two classes, POD_CONSOLE and DOCK_CONSOLE, told apart by Layout's `console_role`, IA-40 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: ASK-CT-13 / ASK-CT-14 accepted, delegated G6, GAP)*); one interactable may carry several classes — a scrubber is both REPAIRABLE and SALVAGE_MODULE); `command_type`; `owner_system`; `mode` in {TAP, HOLD_CONFIRM, CHANNEL}; `tool_required` (a def id or NONE); `noise_class` in {QUIET, TOOL, LOUD}; `duration_key` (names a registered constant, never a literal); `reason_classes` (per reason, IMMEDIATE or DEFERRED); `verb_name_key` (a localisation key; always state-neutral, IA-11); `eligible` (an optional predicate name; at MVP: Sanity's `actor_sanity_below_harm_threshold` (registry; IA-31), Assembly's `weld_offer` and `seam_cut_offer` (registry; IA-23) and Web Map's `console_ready(role)`, with `berthed_present()` for the two DOCK_CONSOLE rows (WM-8; IA-40); an owner-supplied predicate is evaluated exactly as Interaction's own and reads no module-body field, IA-11b); `admitted_while_carrying` (bool, default false; true only on the HARDPOINT `install_module` row, the POD_CONSOLE `pod_hop` row and the door `set_opening` row, IA-28; *Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the door row added*) (registry: `admitted_while_carrying`); `destructive` (bool); `priority` (int, for IA-5); `subject_allowed` (bool, so that in-wall cable and duct edges are repaired at their visible junction, not along their length). The table takes **no** sanity, Attention, rhythm, breach, device or hold-versus-toggle input (lint, IA-4). A pull request that adds a verb is one data row, one handler and one registration line, with an empty Adapter diff (Controller acceptance stub at PCI:969; kernel velocity rule, KRN:135). *Decides:* R1, R26, PC-6.

**IA-3 One command type per verb; `interact` is an input action.** Each verb is its own command type with its own `validate`, reason subset, `max_age_ticks` 120 (`MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION`; `STALE` is unreachable from local ingress), `coalesce NONE` (edge-triggered), `actor_binding OWNED_BY_ISSUER` with `actor_source body.state` (the acting avatar's Body `actor_id`; kernel CR-7 keys the authority check `(body.state, actor)`) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: kernel CR-6 / CR-7 make `actor_source` mandatory on every `OWNED_BY_ISSUER` type, else boot fails `ACTOR_SOURCE_INVALID`; every Interaction type acts as the avatar)*, `allowed_origins {PLAYER}` and not `allowed_when_paused`. Payloads are primitives only (kernel CR-4): a registered subject ref (or a PlayerId for `begin_harm` and `begin_revive`) and, for `throw_glowstick`, `aim_angle` — **never a believed state, a direction, a verb choice or a kind** (LS-22; the one enum, `field_check`'s closed `check_kind` {SCAN}, names the instrument's check, not a subject's kind). The registry of record:

| Type | Mode | Payload | Registered by | Origins | Reason subset (immediate) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `begin_patch` (V1; also the self-patch on object class ACTOR_SELF, IA-17) | CHANNEL | `{target_ref}` | Interaction | PLAYER | `OUT_OF_RANGE, NOT_LIT, INVALID_TARGET, BUSY, NO_TOOL` (`OUT_OF_RANGE` and `NOT_LIT` apply to object targets only; the ACTOR_SELF row is `act_gate`-exempt, IA-6) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated G6, GAP: the owner adds the surface the Controller's ruled behaviour needs)* |
| `begin_cut` (module salvage, V8) | CHANNEL | `{target_ref}` | Interaction | PLAYER | same |
| `begin_cap` (port cap, V8 follow-up) | CHANNEL | `{stub_ref}` | Interaction | PLAYER | same |
| `begin_crank` (V12) | CHANNEL | `{target_ref}` | Interaction | PLAYER | same minus `NO_TOOL` |
| `cancel_channel` | TAP | `{}` | Interaction | PLAYER | none (kernel reasons only); with no channel it is **Applied as a no-op** (`IA_CANCEL_NOOP`, IA-15) |
| `set_breaker` (V3) | TAP | `{target_ref}` | Interaction | PLAYER | `OUT_OF_RANGE, NOT_LIT, INVALID_TARGET, BUSY` |
| `set_valve` (V5) | TAP | `{target_ref}` | Interaction | PLAYER | same |
| `top_up` (V7, SE-7 adopted verbatim; MVP by the 2026-10-27 user ruling X5, IA-9; *registered at boot unconditionally — Amended 2026-10-27, user rulings*) | TAP | `{node_id}` (an O2_TANK or BATTERY port subject) | Interaction | PLAYER | `OUT_OF_RANGE, NOT_LIT, INVALID_TARGET, BUSY`, plus Suit's `ALREADY_FULL` |
| `set_opening` (V4 door, V6 vent) | TAP; HOLD_CONFIRM for V6 in both directions | PLAYER: `{target_ref}`; SYSTEM: `{target_ref, area}` | Interaction | **PLAYER and SYSTEM** (IA-24) | same plus `OBSTRUCTED` |
| `pickup` | TAP | `{target_ref}` | Interaction | PLAYER | `OUT_OF_RANGE, NOT_LIT, INVALID_TARGET, BUSY`, plus Suit's `STACK_FULL` (IA-28) |
| `drop` | TAP | `{}` | Interaction | PLAYER | `OBSTRUCTED, INVALID_TARGET` |
| `throw_glowstick` | its own action (Q / R3) | `{aim_angle}` | Interaction | PLAYER | `BUSY, OUT_OF_CHARGES` |
| `field_check` | CHANNEL | `{subject_ref, check_kind}` | Interaction | PLAYER | `OUT_OF_RANGE, NOT_LIT, INVALID_TARGET, BUSY, NO_TOOL` |
| `begin_harm` | CHANNEL | `{target_player}` | Interaction | PLAYER | the channel set plus `NOT_ELIGIBLE` |
| `begin_revive` (VS-B, dormant) | CHANNEL | `{target_player}` | Interaction | PLAYER | `OUT_OF_RANGE, NOT_LIT, INVALID_TARGET, BUSY` |
| `begin_weld` (V9) | CHANNEL | `{target_ref}` (Assembly's) | **Assembly** (Modular Ship Assembly, Designed — MSA-1; registry `assembly_commands`); its apply calls `open_channel` (IA-35) | PLAYER | shared vocabulary; Assembly's `validate` (the IMMEDIATE half of `weld_offer`) |
| `begin_seam_cut` (the section cut; no V-id) | CHANNEL | `{target_ref}` (a WELD_SOCKET subject in `WELDED`) | **Assembly** (Designed — MSA-1; registry `assembly_commands`); its apply calls `open_channel` (IA-35) | PLAYER | shared vocabulary; Assembly's `validate` (the IMMEDIATE half of `seam_cut_offer`; `CUT_BLOCKED`) |
| `install_module` | TAP | `{hardpoint_ref}` | **Assembly** (Designed — MSA-26; registry `assembly_commands`) | PLAYER | Assembly's IMMEDIATE set; a body-state branch resolves in its apply as a `BLOCKED` outcome (IA-11g) |
| `set_switch`, `toggle_beam` | TAP | Emitter State's | **Emitter State** (designed; `NOT_LIT` is Interaction's precondition for `set_switch`; a PLAYER-origin `set_switch` toggles against the Emitter's true `switch_on` and the Adapter submits `{emitter_id}` with no `on` field, see the Emitter State row of Interactions) | PLAYER, SYSTEM | Emitter State's |
| `equip_tool` | — | Suit's (`{slot: 1..6}`) | **Suit** (Suit & Equipment, Designed — SE-40; Suit registers exactly this one command) | PLAYER | Suit's (`EMPTY_SLOT`, `NOT_ACTIONABLE`, `BUSY`) |
| `pod_hop` (POD_CONSOLE, carry-admitted); `berth`, `unberth` (DOCK_CONSOLE) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: ASK-CT-13 / ASK-CT-14 accepted, delegated G6, GAP)* | TAP (the console press, IA-40; noise: `berth` LOUD, `unberth` and `pod_hop` QUIET *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*) | Web Map's (`{target_id}`; `{derelict_id}`; none) | **Web Map & Transit** (Designed — WM-8, WM-9) | PLAYER | Web Map's closed reason set (WM-9) |

Interaction **declares** the rows it does not register (their `verb_name_key`, mode, tool and noise class live in the declaration table) and **registers** the rest; `set_opening` alone admits `SYSTEM` origin, for a failure cascade's door close (IA-24). *Alternative considered:* one generic `interact {target, verb}` command with an internal dispatcher — rejected: one giant `validate`, mixed reason sets, per-verb age and coalescing impossible, replay logs less legible. *Stale tokens routed:* the kernel text at KRN:85 and KRN:204 lists `interact` as a command; here it is an input action only. KRN:85 (and IDB:470) also list `use_item`, which no GDD registers: Suit registers exactly `equip_tool` and states `use_item` is not its command (SE-40, AC-SE-39), so it is removed from this table and routed to the kernel and Item DB owners as a stale token; the suppressant's effect verb (Suit's `consume_charge`) is declared here when Attention or Horror register it, and no MVP row exists (Cross-References). *Decides:* R1, R2, PC-6, LS-22, Controller acceptance at PCI:969.

**IA-4 Focus is the Controller's; Interaction re-validates; no rule branches on device.** The Adapter chooses exactly one focused object by the aim-weighted score (`FOCUS_AIM_WEIGHT`, `FOCUS_STICKY_MARGIN`; ties go to the smaller aim angle, then the smaller entity id; Controller D.9) and submits the *declared* type with that ref. The simulation never computes a score; it re-validates the chosen target with `in_reach` (`REACH_PX`, `REACH_HALF_ANGLE_DEG`, `COS2_REACH`; cited, not restated) and `lit`. There is no pick-under-cursor path on any device. A gamepad user who never touches the right stick focuses by facing (aim follows movement until the first right-stick input, PC-4/5); `interact` with no focus is an Adapter no-op (no command, no pre-play; the Adapter plays only its local `cue_input_ignored`) — except the self-target: the Controller nominates the actor's own suit as the focus when the equipped tool has an ACTOR_SELF row and no world candidate is focusable (PC-6), and the press then submits `begin_patch {own PlayerId}` (IA-17; `focus_view.self_target`, IA-41) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP: the owner adds the surface the Controller's ruled behaviour needs)*. No Interaction rule reads the input device, hold-versus-toggle or tap-to-confirm policy (Adapter policy, PC-7 and PC-13); lint: `assets/data/interaction/verbs` takes no sanity, Attention, rhythm, breach or device input. *Decides:* PC-6, R26, T8.

**IA-5 One contextual action: `select_verb`.** Given the focused object's classes and the tool in hand (Suit's `tool_in_hand`, Suit & Equipment, Designed — SE-40), the declaration row is chosen by: (1) the rows whose `tool_required` equals the tool in hand, lowest `priority` first; else (2) the bare-hand rows, lowest `priority` first; else (3) the lowest-`priority` row of the class, shown with its tool glyph and answered `NO_TOOL` on press. The choice is a function of (object classes, tool in hand, the `eligible` predicate) only — **never of hp, state, forced mode or served power** (IA-11). While the actor carries a module, the only selectable rows are `drop` placement and the declared carry-admitted rows (`admitted_while_carrying`: `install_module` on a HARDPOINT whose `compatible_defs(h)` lists the carried instance's def, `pod_hop` on the POD_CONSOLE, and V4 `set_opening` on a door face; IA-28) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; any other world target while carrying is shown no prompt at all. *Example:* a scrubber with the welder in hand selects V1 `begin_patch`; with the cutter in hand it selects V8 `begin_cut`; with bare hands row (3) selects V1 and shows the welder glyph — the press answers `NO_TOOL`. ACTOR_SELF rows are selected by tier (1) only (the exact tool in hand): tiers (2) and (3) never apply to ACTOR_SELF, so a player without the welder in hand sees no self plate and no `NEEDS_TOOL` variant for it. The Adapter evaluates this same pure data lookup (the declaration table, IA-2) with class ACTOR_SELF and Suit's `tool_in_hand` to decide whether to nominate the self-target (IA-4). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP: the owner adds the surface the Controller's ruled behaviour needs)* *Alternatives considered:* hide the verb without the tool (the player never learns it exists); auto-equip the right tool (removes a Suit decision and makes `equip_tool` pointless). *Decides:* R11, HR-14 (one contextual action), D10.

**IA-6 Act only on what is lit (`act_gate`).** `act_gate(o) = in_reach(o) AND lit(o)`, where `in_reach` is the Controller's integer predicate (itself including the lit conjunct) and `lit` is Emitter State's hysteresis bit (`LIT_ON_PM`, `LIT_OFF_PM`, no dwell). Every PLAYER-origin Interaction command whose declaration is not exempt checks `act_gate` in `validate`, against the exposure table committed at tick T−1 (EM-25). The exceptions are exactly three, plus the actor verbs that are not object verbs: `drop` of the carried module, `toggle_beam`, and the inspect card of an already-lit instrument (PC-6); `throw_glowstick` is an actor verb like `toggle_beam` and needs no lit target (IA-29); and **the self-patch is an actor verb too**: `begin_patch` on object class `ACTOR_SELF` (IA-17) targets the actor's own suit, so there is no object to light and no reach to test; its declaration row has `exempt(row) = 1`, `validate` skips both `in_reach` and `lit` for it, and an ACTOR_SELF row can produce neither `OUT_OF_RANGE` nor `NOT_LIT`. This is not a fourth object exception: the object list stays exactly three (`drop`, `toggle_beam`, the inspect card of an already-lit instrument), and the pod-console refusal below is unchanged. The dark self-patch is still priced, so it is not a free Attention dodge (Pillar 3): TOOL noise on every accrued tick (IA-33), 180 stationary ticks (`PATCH_CHANNEL_TICKS`) and the welder in hand. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated G6, GAP: the owner adds the surface the Controller's ruled behaviour needs)* **No fourth exception for the pod console (refused, stated here):** Web Map's ASK-I2 and Boarding's ASK-BS-I3 ask that `pod_hop` at the CONSOLE be exempt from the lit condition; the three exceptions are the Controller's PC-6 list (PCI:127) and IA-17 already refuses a fourth, so `pod_hop`, `berth` and `unberth` stay lit-gated (on both the POD_CONSOLE and the DOCK_CONSOLE subject, IA-40), and the hop stays available by content, not by exemption: the docked pod's dock-room prop carries a small self-powered status glow that counts as an ordinary lit fixture (not a fourth `act_gate` exception), so `pod_hop`'s lit gate is always satisfied at the console and a `NOT_LIT` at a dock console is a content defect, not a state (Open Question 31, resolved) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: X2, Web Map option (B); this refusal STANDS. The self-powered fixture class (Emitter State EM-3 / EM-11), the placement rule and lint in every dock room (Derelict Authoring), the art-director's luminance separation from the visible-unknown lamp and Web Map accepting ASK-BS-W3 are the other owners' sides; Boarding's BS-19 invariant is owned jointly: Web Map and Emitter State guarantee the glow, Authoring lints its placement)*. An unregistered ref answers exactly as an unlit one (EM-21), so a phantom and a darkened real object are indistinguishable by design. *Why the rule exists:* it makes darkness a trade, not a free dodge of the web's attention — "unlit objects can be worked on" was rejected because working in the dark would be a free Attention dodge. Pillars: **P3** (every action spends exposure) and **P4** (reaching for a phantom finds nothing). *Decides:* PC-6, EM-21/22/25, F2 (the composite is registered as a formula, D.2).

**IA-7 Mid-channel re-check.** Each channel re-reads `lit(target)` through the same query at the cadence of IA-14 (every `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS`, counted from the begin tick, inclusive) and aborts with cause UNLIT if it is false at a check; a dark gap shorter than the cadence is tolerated (at most `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS − 1` = 5 dark channel ticks, Emitter State AC-59b). The geometric conjuncts of `in_reach` are re-tested at the same cadence (OUT_OF_REACH). Neither re-check is tested for a record whose target class is ACTOR_SELF (exempt, IA-6); such a record's abort set is IA-14 rows 1-3 and 7-9. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated G6, GAP: the owner adds the surface the Controller's ruled behaviour needs)* The cadence tests are `e mod LIT_RECHECK_TICKS == 0` (IA-14 rows 4 to 6), and invariant I-1 (`GRAPH_STEP_TICKS mod LIT_RECHECK_TICKS == 0`; legal `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS` values 1, 2, 3 and 6) makes every block end a check tick, so no step is credited on an unlit or out-of-reach block end. The hook is `after: emitter.place`, so it reads tick T's table, not T−1's. *Decides:* R10, EM-25, the Controller's own flag at PCI:677 ("Interaction's rule to confirm" — confirmed).

**Group B — Modes, the verb table, reasons and the P4 lint**

**IA-8 Modes (exclusive per declaration).** TAP = V3, V4, V5, V7, `pickup`, `drop`, `set_switch` (toggle semantics, payload `{emitter_id}` only for PLAYER origin, IA-3), and the owner-registered taps declared here: Assembly's `install_module` and Web Map's `pod_hop`, `berth` and `unberth` (IA-3). HOLD_CONFIRM = V6 only at MVP, in both directions, (the irreversible loss of a room's gas, LSG:797) and any future row tagged `destructive`. CHANNEL = V1, V8 (`begin_cut`, `begin_cap`), V9, V12, `field_check`, `begin_harm`, `begin_revive`. A channel's hold is its own confirmation (HR-14 is satisfied); no verb is both. V3 closing onto a load stays TAP: the inrush (`INRUSH_PM`, `INRUSH_STEPS`) is the cost, and a confirm would punish the very experiment the game wants. `throw_glowstick` is its own action (`throw`, Q or RB; the Controller PC-2 moved the pad binding from R3 to RB, re-review 2026-10-27; provisional UX binding), not an `interact` mode. Hold-to-confirm lasts `CONFIRM_HOLD_S` on the **linear bar with needle-mark**; release drains at `CONFIRM_DRAIN_RATE`; on completion exactly one command is submitted; the simulation never sees a partial hold (Controller D.10). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: X10, hold-to-confirm gets a two-tap alternative (the Adapter-local armed state, `CONFIRM_TAP_WINDOW`) and an adjustable hold duration (`CONFIRM_HOLD_S` as a player setting, Controller- and Chart-Table-owned); the simulation still sees exactly one command either way, so no rule here changes.)* *Decides:* R8, PC-6, art bible HR-14.

**IA-9 The verb table V1 to V12 (table of record).** First written here (the Graph's own V1 to V12 list was specialist input and is not enumerated in the repo; only the eight MVP verbs are named, LSG:228). The Graph's locked MVP verbs were **V1, V3, V4, V5, V6, V8, V9 and V12** and become those plus **V7** under the 2026-10-27 user ruling X5 (the LS-26 text amendment is the Graph owner's); V7 is written in full from Suit's SE-7 and is MVP *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; the remaining rows are RESERVED, carrying the Graph's Vertical-Slice-A notes verbatim and no invented semantics.

| V | Name (Graph wording) | Mutator (LSG row) | Tool | Mode | Command (registered by) | Duration key | Noise class | MVP |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

**V7 top-up (Suit SE-7, adopted verbatim; the self-patch of IA-17 is the other Suit-driven row).** Command `top_up` (owner Interaction; origin PLAYER; `OWNED_BY_ISSUER`, `actor_source body.state` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; `coalesce NONE`; `max_age_ticks` 120; not `allowed_when_paused`); payload `{node_id}`, an O2_TANK or BATTERY port subject; bare hand; mode **TAP** (SE-7's CHANNEL option is declined: no new constant and no bar — the stock falling and the suit reading rising are the answer). `validate`, all immediate: kernel authority → `in_reach` → `lit` → `BUSY` (carrying, channelling, staggered, downed) → node kind in {O2_TANK, BATTERY} → Suit's `ALREADY_FULL` (`reserve_deficit_mgu(player_id)` is 0 for an O2_TANK port, or `cell_deficit_ticks(player_id)` is 0 for a BATTERY port — the actor's own state, so immediate and no oracle). `apply` (O2_TANK): `granted = withdraw_store(node_id, reserve_deficit_mgu)` (Graph M16, Class B) then Suit's `credit_reserve(player_id, granted)`; (BATTERY): the deficit in ticks converted to stock units by Suit's `CELL_STOCK_PER_KTICK` (rounded up), `withdraw_store`, then `credit_cell` with the grant converted back (rounded down). A top-up never credits more than the deficit. The result detail is `{}`: `granted` is never carried in the result event; the suit's own reserve reading rising (or not) is the only answer. Noise QUIET. **No `STORE_EMPTY` token and no distinct cue** (orchestrator ruling over SE-7's "new sub-code ask"): a TAP has no deferred class (IA-12), and a granted-0 top-up is Applied-and-did-nothing whose answer is the suit's own reserve reading not rising (Suit's `true_value`, the consequence channel); the tank's sight-glass is module-body and may lie. **MVP status — admitted.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D34 / D40 / D70b / X5 / Graph OQ 20. V7 `top_up` joins the MVP verb list as a deliberate O2 TAP verb; LS-26 is amended on the Graph's side to list V7, Suit's `TOPUP_MODE` is VERB and `CELL_ENABLED` is on, and the lamp cell and the "go dark" lever exist at MVP. The former routed CONFLICT with LS-26 and the passive-refill alternative (Suit's `TOPUP_MODE` PASSIVE, a hidden refill) are closed; `top_up` is registered unconditionally, the `top_up` story is unblocked, and AC-IA-1 / AC-IA-41 are unconditional. Reason: an actively chosen refill is a survival decision and a PASSIVE refill is a hidden mechanism.)*

The Item DB's tool assignment (cutter {8}, welder {1, 9}, scanner {}; bare hands for V3 to V6 and V12, IDB:242-251, confirmed here) is consistent with this table; V7 is bare-hand per Suit SE-7. *V12 reading:* "hand crank" is read as the manual restart of a SCRAMmed or FROZEN machine (M11) because the MVP slice has a SCRAMmed reactor on derelict B (LS-26) and M11 lists Interaction as its caller; the alternative reading (manual actuation of a door or valve when unpowered) goes to Open Questions. *V8 duration:* `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` is Interaction's new constant (orchestrator ruling: named `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS`, not a `CUT_*` name, so that it cannot be mistaken for Assembly's section-cut duration, which is a different channel at the same placeholder figure); the section-cut and weld durations remain Assembly's — `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 and `WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS` 360 (Modular Ship Assembly, Designed — MSA-30; registry), cited, never restated as Interaction's. *The section cut has no V-id:* Assembly's `begin_seam_cut` is declared here by its own row (IA-3, IA-23) and takes no slot in the Graph's V1 to V12 list; V10 stays RESERVED. *Decides:* R3, LS-26 (LSG:228), LS-18 (LSG:185-192), Item DB IDB-11.

**IA-10 The closed reason set and the classification rule.** Reasons are namespaced domain codes under the kernel's `VALIDATION_FAILED(domain_code)`. A reason is **IMMEDIATE** if and only if it depends only on geometry, light, the actor's own state, tool or stock, or the consequence channel. A reason is **DEFERRED** if and only if it depends on module-body state — `hp`, `state`, `forced_mode` or `served_pm` of a node or edge, the channel Pillar 4 may corrupt.

| Reason | Where it arises | Class | Meaning | Why this class |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `OUT_OF_RANGE` | validate | IMMEDIATE | the distance and arc conjuncts of Controller D.9 fail (`in_reach` without its `lit` conjunct; cited, never restated) | geometry |
| `NOT_LIT` | validate | IMMEDIATE | `lit(ref)` false, including an unregistered ref | light (the consequence channel) |
| `INVALID_TARGET` | validate | IMMEDIATE | ref not a registered subject of the declared kind, or tombstoned | the registry, not body state |
| `BUSY` | validate | IMMEDIATE | carrying (except the carry-admitted rows, IA-28), channelling, staggered, downed, in a pod, **moving (`controller.state.intent_octant != NONE`) at a `begin_*`** (PC-7: the owner's `validate` requires `intent_octant == NONE`; *Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: `validate` reads `intent_octant`, written at the `set_move_intent` apply in P2, not the P3-published `octant`, which lags a tick; the hook of IA-14 row 3 keeps reading `octant`. Resolved with the Controller's asks, Open Question 25 / OQ 47: **the reason stays `BUSY`; a separate `MOVING` reason is declined**, because the closed reason set is presentation-blind (the HUD plays one frame set for every rejection and never reads the reason, IA-41) and the new reason would buy no player-visible difference*), subject already worked by another seat, or a re-arm window still open (IA-21) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the moving case is the most-pressed reject, so `focus_view.press_blocked_moving` tells the HUD to dim the plate while the actor moves, IA-41; the press is still answered `BUSY`, nothing buffers)* | actor state; a visible crewmate; timing |
| `NO_TOOL` | validate | IMMEDIATE | required tool not in hand | actor state; the object's kind is geometry |
| `OUT_OF_CHARGES` | validate | IMMEDIATE | no glowstick in stock | actor stock |
| `OBSTRUCTED` | validate only — an immediate reject, never an attempt-expiry outcome | IMMEDIATE | a PLAYER-origin door close while a foot box overlaps the leaf (`leaf_would_overlap`, IA-24); a drop or landing cell refused; headroom exhausted (a budget reason wearing a geometry token: not body state, so immediate) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: this includes a `begin_cut` whose static worst-case yield, `max(1, scrap_count)` from the def, exceeds `subject_headroom()`, D.10 term Y)* | geometry |
| `STACK_FULL` | validate (`pickup` of a STOWED-class def) | IMMEDIATE | Suit's `can_stow` is false; **Suit-namespaced, not Interaction's** (Suit & Equipment, Designed — SE-40; read by Interaction's pickup) | actor stock |
| `ALREADY_FULL` | validate (`top_up`) | IMMEDIATE | the actor's own reserve or cell deficit is 0; **Suit-namespaced** (SE-7 / SE-40) | actor state |
| `NOT_ELIGIBLE` | validate | IMMEDIATE | `begin_harm` by an actor not below the Sanity threshold; an owner-supplied `eligible` predicate false at `validate` (Assembly's `install_module`) | the actor's own state, read on the SIM sanity level only |
| kernel: `ACTOR_NOT_OWNED`, `PAUSED`, admission reasons | kernel | IMMEDIATE | — | not Interaction's |
| `PRECONDITION(NOT_REPAIRABLE)` | **outcome** | DEFERRED | subject DESTROYED (the Graph's `NOT_REPAIRABLE`) | body state |
| `PRECONDITION(NOTHING_TO_REPAIR)` | outcome | DEFERRED | hp already at or above the job's target cap | body state |
| `PRECONDITION(NO_POWER)` | outcome | DEFERRED | a door motor's `served_pm` is 0 at the attempt's expiry | body state |
| `PRECONDITION(ALREADY_RUNNING)` | outcome | DEFERRED | crank on a node not in SCRAM or FREEZE | body state |
| `PRECONDITION(NOT_SALVAGEABLE)` | outcome | DEFERRED | node ABSENT or SALVAGED at the cut's completion | body state |
| `BLOCKED(STILL_HOT / NO_FUEL / AREA_RANGE / PORT_ABSENT / PORT_INCOMPAT / …)` | outcome | DEFERRED | the Graph's physical reason, passed through with the Graph's token unchanged (LSG:775) | the Graph's: an Applied-and-did-nothing outcome, not a reject |

**Reason precedence (one global order; orchestrator ruling: qa-lead's order adopted, compatible with the locked CR-7 order of IA-31 and the V7 chain).** When a press fails several conditions at once, `validate` returns the first of: (1) kernel authority and admission reasons (`ACTOR_NOT_OWNED`, `PAUSED`); (2) `NO_TOOL`; (3) `OUT_OF_RANGE` (the geometric conjuncts of `in_reach`); (4) `NOT_LIT`; (5) `BUSY`; (6) `INVALID_TARGET` and the declared kind check; (7) `NOT_ELIGIBLE`; (8) the stock reasons (Suit's `STACK_FULL` and `ALREADY_FULL`, and `OUT_OF_CHARGES`) and `OBSTRUCTED`. A verb that lacks a reason skips its slot (a `drop` has only `INVALID_TARGET` and `OBSTRUCTED`; `begin_crank` has no `NO_TOOL`; `throw_glowstick` has only `BUSY` then `OUT_OF_CHARGES`). Every reason in the order is IMMEDIATE, so the order itself reads no module-body field.

**One token, one terminal state (kernel CR-5):** `BLOCKED` is never an Interaction reject, because the Graph (LSG:775) and the Item DB (IDB:787, AC-89) already use it as an Applied-and-did-nothing outcome; the geometry reject is `OBSTRUCTED`. Likewise `PRECONDITION` is never a reject. The deferred nothing-to-repair token is `PRECONDITION(NOTHING_TO_REPAIR)` everywhere (orchestrator ruling: the Item DB's AC-89 and advisor R21 spell it with the `BLOCKED` family; the family name is `PRECONDITION` per R5, and the stray spelling is routed to the Item DB owner rather than absorbed). Routed to the Controller owner: PCI:650 (`BLOCKED`) and Open Question 6 (`PRECONDITION`) both become `OBSTRUCTED`; routed likewise to the Layout owner, whose LY-11 and lines 431 / 460 / 1168 spell the PLAYER verdict `BLOCKED` (copied from PCI:650). *Decides:* R5, KRN:96, LSG:775.

**IA-11 The P4 lint (a numbered rule).** (a) **No `validate` of a PLAYER-origin Interaction command reads `node()` / `edge()` `hp`, `state`, `forced_mode`, `served_pm`, the switch position (IA-20), or any `check_*` whose verdict depends on them.** Those reads occur only in `interaction.step` (and in the apply of a TAP verb, which may read a switch position because it is not a health field) and resolve as outcomes. (b) **Prompt existence is a function of (object classes, tool in hand, `lit`, `in_reach`, the `eligible` predicate) only.** A repairable-kind subject prompts regardless of its true hp; a missing prompt on a hallucinated-broken healthy pipe would be a free oracle. (c) **Channel duration and bar length are identical for a hallucinated-broken healthy pipe, a truly broken one and a DESTROYED one** — durations are fixed per verb, and PATCH versus FULL is decided by the actor's own stock (IA-18), never by hp. (d) No payload carries a believed state, a direction or a kind (including PLAYER-origin `set_switch`: `{emitter_id}` only, IA-3). (e) No simulation code (validate, apply, hook) reads a `PERCEPTION_OUT` block (CR-12). (f) The label a prompt shows is state-neutral: V3, V4 and V5 are toggles whose target position is computed from true state at apply, so their `verb_name_key` is `verb_breaker`, `verb_door`, `verb_valve` (or a neutral "use"), and a SWITCH_FIXTURE's is `verb_switch`, and V6's is `verb_vent_hatch`, never "open", "close" or "reset" — a label derived from true state would be a condition readout (art bible 7.0), and one derived from perceived state would be a believed state leaking into the payload. (g) The same lint binds Assembly's `install_module` (MSA-26): its `validate` reads no module-body field, and a body-state precondition resolves in its apply as a `BLOCKED` outcome (Modular Ship Assembly ASK I1 (ix)). **Design test for every new reason:** *could a player learn a module-body truth from this at zero time, risk or trust?* If yes, it is deferred. *Decides:* R6, KRN:96, G5, ART:745.

**IA-12 Deferred outcomes: Applied, run, resolve.** A command whose only failing precondition is module-body state is **Applied**: its apply creates the channel record (a real state change). The channel runs its full declared duration — real simulation time spent, real noise pushed, real exposure held — and at completion the record resolves to `PRECONDITION(sub)` or `BLOCKED(…)`, published as owner state change (the abort route of KRN:96: `last_result` in the seat record, read through `channel_view`); the Adapter plays the "nothing happened" cue. The result event of the Applied command carries no reason. The acknowledgement of a command that will succeed and of one that will resolve to an outcome are sound- and pixel-identical because they are the same command, the same `Applied`, the same bar. **Why this is forced, not preferred (KRN:96):** reasons are computed from true state and the result event carries `reason`; a `Rejected(PRECONDITION)` one tick later is readable by every DIAGNOSTIC subscriber and the replay log, so presentation-side deferral leaves an oracle inside the simulation. **Routed CONFLICTs (none absorbed):** the Controller text at PCI:86 and PCI:886 describes a deferred-class `Rejected` — amended to "Applied; the channel resolves to a `PRECONDITION` outcome"; the Graph text at LSG:771 says the owner's `validate` fails with `NOT_REPAIRABLE` via `check_repair_step` — here `check_repair_step` is called at each step inside the hook and a failing check skips the apply (never FAULTED, CR-9). **TAP verbs have no deferred class:** the M8 and M9 checks are kind checks, so a breaker on a FAILED junction closes and the branch stays dark — the world answers; the one TAP with a body-state precondition (door motor power) is the uniform door attempt of IA-24. Diagnostic `IA_DEFERRED_REVEAL{sub}`. *Decides:* R6, KRN:96, CON P4 cost rule.

**IA-13 Phantoms (kernel Open Question 14, closed).** A phantom entity (P4(a)) has no subject, never satisfies `lit(ref)`, never becomes the focus, so nothing is submitted and nothing is rejected; `interact` with no focus is an Adapter no-op. Phantom salvage (P4(d)) is a real registered `junk_` def and takes every verb normally; its truth is the Suit weight cue, the scanner `field_check` (IA-30) or the trip home. Negative requirements on the Perception Filter and the HUD (HUD & Suit Layer, Designed — hud-and-suit-layer.md; the Perception Filter's side is not re-verified in this pass): never register a subject, never draw a prompt where `lit(ref)` is false (Emitter State UI requirements, EM:864). The debug-only counter `IA_PHANTOM_PROBE` exists in development builds only — a release build carries none, because a probe counter would be an existence oracle. *Alternatives considered:* accept commands on phantoms and answer after a delay (needs a fake SIM id, violating the spirit of CR-12); reject phantoms with `INVALID_TARGET` (a free oracle, KRN:204). *Decides:* R7, EM-21, P4 "no lie is unfalsifiable".

**Group C — The channel**

**IA-14 Channel lifecycle, the clock and the hook's order of work.** A channel is a record in `interaction.state`, one per seat at most (PC-7): `{verb_id, owner_system, target_ref, begin_tick Tb, duration D, resume_offset, blocks_done, steps_done, hp_at_begin, cap_at_begin, t_last_block}` (the FULL-job part state lives in `full_jobs[]`, States and Transitions 5). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: `t_last_block` (the tick of the last credited block, 0 until one is credited; row 8) and `cap_at_begin` (the subject's `hp_cap` read at the first step, with `hp_at_begin`, for the PATCH guard of IA-17) are added to the persisted record. `blocks_done` is **absolute** (it includes `resume_blocks`). For a resumed job `t_last_block` is also derivable: `t_last_block = Tb + B × (blocks_done − resume_blocks)` whenever this run has credited a block, e.g. a crank resumed at `resume_blocks` 16 and cancelled at e = 20 has `blocks_done` 19 and `t_last_block = Tb + 18`. Only FULL repair uses `t_last_block` (for `grace_until`) and a FULL job never resumes through the ledger, so `resume_blocks` = 0 there.)* It is created by the verb's `apply` in P2, together with the Controller's `set_channel_ref(actor, INTERACTION, channel_id, face_octant)` (IA-36) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6/G1, CONFLICT)*, and advanced only by the hook `interaction.step`. Definitions: `Tb` is the tick whose P2 applied the begin command; elapsed `e = T − Tb`; `B = GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` (6); `D` is the declared duration, **lint: `D mod B == 0` for every `*_CHANNEL_TICKS`** (all current values satisfy it); `n = D / B` blocks. (Orchestrator ruling: the clock below is the systems-designer's "accrue at e = 1 … D" convention, not the game-designer's "accrue on the begin tick" one; the two differ by one tick. It gives the property that a cancel inside the first six ticks spends no step, no part and no ledger entry, and the lit check at e = 0 re-reads tick T after `validate` read T−1, EM-25.) On each tick, for each seat with a channel in ascending PlayerId, the hook works through this list; **the first terminal cause wins and is recorded as the cue cause**:

| Order | Test at elapsed `e` | If true |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | `cancel_channel` | handled in P2, before the hook: the record is already gone and the hook does nothing |
| 1 | `channel_ref` absent (the Controller cleared it: stagger, downed, a body transfer -- `controller.step` step 1, cap edge or body edge, Player Controller States -- or restore) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: citation only, no behaviour change)* | `ABORT(BODY)`; no accrual |
| 2 | the target is no longer a registered subject (tombstoned) | `ABORT(TARGET_GONE)` |
| 3 | `controller.state.octant != NONE` | `ABORT(MOVED)`; **abort-before-accrual** (PC-7): this tick is not accrued |
| 4 | `e mod LIT_RECHECK_TICKS == 0` and the geometric conjuncts of `in_reach` fail (cited from Controller D.9, never restated); not tested for a record whose target class is ACTOR_SELF (exempt, IA-6); such a record's abort set is rows 1-3 and 7-9 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated G6, GAP: the owner adds the surface the Controller's ruled behaviour needs)* | `ABORT(OUT_OF_REACH)` |
| 5 | `e mod LIT_RECHECK_TICKS == 0` and `lit(target)` is false (tick T's table: the hook is `after: emitter.place`); not tested for a record whose target class is ACTOR_SELF (exempt, IA-6); such a record's abort set is rows 1-3 and 7-9 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated G6, GAP: the owner adds the surface the Controller's ruled behaviour needs)* | `ABORT(UNLIT)` |
| 6 | (`begin_harm`, and every ASSEMBLY-owned record) `e mod LIT_RECHECK_TICKS == 0` and the row's `eligible` predicate is false (the Sanity gate query; Assembly's `weld_offer` or `seam_cut_offer`, so a lost `docked` aborts the channel, MSA ASK I1 (viii)) | `ABORT(NOT_ELIGIBLE)` |
| 7 | none of the above and `e ≥ 1` | accrue tick `e`; push the verb's noise (IA-33) |
| 8 | `e mod B == 0` and `e ≥ B` | block `j = resume_blocks + e / B` is credited (`resume_blocks = resume_offset / B`, 0 for a fresh job). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: **every channel credits the block, then steps.** First, for every verb and both truth branches, set `blocks_done = j` (absolute) and `t_last_block = T`; then, for incremental channels only, run the **step**: `check_*` and the Class A mutator (`begin_patch`, FULL repair, the self-patch's `patch_integrity`), or `apply_suit_damage` for `begin_harm`. FULL repair and `begin_harm` therefore write `blocks_done` like every other channel; the former wording credited only the non-incremental verbs and left them out. `blocks` in IA-18, D.7 and States and Transitions 5 **is** this `blocks_done` field, and `t_last_block` is persisted in the channel record (States and Transitions 8).)* |
| 9 | `e == D − resume_offset` | completion: resolve to `DONE` or an outcome (D.3), publish through owner state change (KRN:96), clear `channel_ref` through the Controller mutator *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: this is also the only tick on which `record_falsification` is called for a `field_check` (IA-30) and the only terminal state that may release a RESERVED FULL-job part, on `OUTCOME` (IA-18); every abort row 1 to 6, `cancel_channel` and a restore instead commit or release the reservation by the blocks-credited rule of IA-18)*; for a record whose `owner_system` is not INTERACTION (Assembly's weld and seam cut) Interaction applies nothing at DONE but the `channel_ref` clear and the ledger delete, and exposes the DONE handoff `(seat, owner_system, verb_id, target_ref)` in `channel_view` for that tick (IA-35) |

Properties (each is an acceptance row): (a) the lit check at `e = 0` re-reads tick T after `validate` read T−1, so a lamp that died in between aborts at progress 0; (b) a lamp that dies at `e = 6j + 1` lets ticks `6j + 1 … 6j + 5` accrue (**five dark ticks at most**, matching Emitter State AC-59b), and the check at `e = 6j + 6` aborts *before* block `j + 1` is credited, so no step and no ledger credit is ever earned on an unlit block end; (c) a lamp that flickers off and on again inside one block costs nothing and the block is credited; (d) a move on `e = D` leaves `blocks_done = n − 1` (PC-7: "one tick short" — the move wins); (e) steps land at `Tb + 6, Tb + 12, …`, never on `Tb`; (f) rows 4 to 6 test `e mod LIT_RECHECK_TICKS == 0`, not `e mod B`, and invariant I-1 (`GRAPH_STEP_TICKS mod LIT_RECHECK_TICKS == 0`) makes every block end a check tick, so no step is credited on an unlit or out-of-reach block end under any legal retune of `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS`. **The Graph-read lag, stated and not hidden:** the Graph's three hooks run on `T mod 6 == 0` (power), `2` (atmo) and `4` (failure) (Graph LS-8), and each runs *before* `interaction.step` on its tick (through the `emitter.place` chain). A step applied at tick `Tb + 6j` is read by the hook that consumes that mutator's effect on the first of its ticks strictly after the apply: hp changes (M7/M8/M10-class) by the failure hook (phases 8-9, `T mod 6 == 4`), requests and loads (M14, M13 valve/breaker) by the power hook (phases 0-2, `T mod 6 == 0`). The lag is the distance to the next such tick, 1 to 6 ticks in every case (≤ 100 ms); the range is unchanged. "The hiss stops when hp crosses `LEAK_HP`" therefore trails the step by up to one Graph step; this is the honest timing. *Alternative considered:* steps only on the failure-hook tick (`T mod 6 == 4`) — removes the lag variance but makes the effective duration 180 to 185 ticks and the bar length depend on the begin offset (rejected). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: the former derivation assumed the three Graph hooks share `T mod 6 == 0`; under the Graph's baseline stagger, offsets 0/2/4, they do not. Recomputed: the [1, 6] tick range is unchanged.)* A channel never locks movement; the facing locks during a channel (Controller behaviour, `FACING_HYSTERESIS`). A pause does not interrupt a channel — the tick simply does not advance (CR-16). *Decides:* R9, R10, L46 (PC-7), EM-25, R4.

**IA-15 Abort causes, cues, no buffering.** The closed set of abort causes: `MOVED`, `CANCELLED`, `UNLIT`, `OUT_OF_REACH`, `TARGET_GONE`, `BODY` (stagger, downed, body transfer, seat rebind — the sub-cause is diagnostic), `NOT_ELIGIBLE`, `RESTORE`. A channel record ends in exactly one terminal state: `DONE`, `ABORT(cause)` or `OUTCOME(sub)` (IA-12); when `cancel_channel` and an abort cause fall on the same tick, `cancel_channel` (P2) wins and the hook finds no record. A `cancel_channel` with no channel is **Applied as a no-op** (counted `IA_CANCEL_NOOP`), not rejected: the Adapter sends `cancel` on every hold release, and a reject there would play the dull "no" cue after every completed channel. Cues are specified in Visual/Audio Requirements and Game Feel; their *meaning* is fixed here: cancel (the player let go) drains the bar; abort (the world or the body ended it) snaps the bar empty with a distinct "interrupted" cue; completion fills it; a deferred outcome fills it and then plays "nothing happened"; an immediate rejection withdraws the acknowledgement within one frame with a dull cue and no shake *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the withdrawal alone is sub-perceptible, and a mashed press could flicker it faster than the 3 Hz ceiling.
