The HUD therefore holds the restored keycap in a one-step Graphite-lit state for `BAR_REJECT_HOLD_TICKS` (HUD-owned, proposed 9, safe range 6 to 18) and admits at most one reject presentation per seat per `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS`; the rejection stays a cut and a cue, never a shake)*. "Stopped", "interrupted" and "nothing happened" are three different sounds and three different shapes. `ACTION_BUFFER_TICKS` is 0: a press during a channel is submitted and answered `BUSY`; nothing queues for "after" (PCI:84). The abort cause is delivered to presentation as diagnostic data only and the HUD never reads it; the one bit the HUD needs, the player's own cancel versus every world cause, is the non-diagnostic `channel_view.ended_by` (IA-41): one drain for `PLAYER_CANCEL`, one abort frame set and one abort cue for every `WORLD` cause (HUD & Suit Layer OQ-HS-19). *Decides:* L47, L49, R10.

**IA-16 Partial progress.** *Incremental repair needs no ledger* (advisor R9): an abort leaves hp physically raised by the steps already applied, which is honest. **Non-incremental channels** (`begin_cut`, `begin_cap`, `begin_crank`, `field_check`, `begin_revive`, and Assembly's `begin_weld` and `begin_seam_cut`, whose records are Interaction's, IA-35) write a ledger entry on abort keyed `(seat, subject_ref, verb_id)` = `{blocks_done, abort_tick, n}` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: `n` added so a retuned duration discards the entry, D.6; **`begin_harm` is removed from this list, delegated G2 following the creative-director's BLOCKING reading of the D36 ruling**: a harm re-begin always restarts at `e = 0` with its own full escape window, because the cumulative damage already persists in the victim's Suit integrity and a resumed bar would put the next damage 6 ticks after the re-begin, defeating the 65-tick walking window that D36 guarantees)*. A re-begin by the same seat on the same subject and verb with `T − abort_tick ≤ PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` (inclusive) resumes: `resume_offset = blocks_done × B`, the remaining time is `(n − blocks_done) × B` ticks, and the bar continues from `blocks_done / n` (the bar is a function of declared duration and the player's own history, never of any module-body field, IA-11c). After `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` the entry is gone; it is also deleted on `DONE` or an outcome. The ledger is bounded by `MAX_PARTIAL_ENTRIES` per seat; when full, the entry with the smallest `abort_tick` is evicted (ties: the smallest `subject_ref`); expiry is evaluated every tick in the hook so snapshot contents are replay-stable. The ledger ages on global ticks whatever the subject's section is doing: an entry on a dormant derelict's subject expires `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` after its abort like any other, so a partial cut never survives a hop leg (600 ticks against a 945-tick leg; Boarding ASK-BS-I5). A `BODY` abort still credits finished blocks. Noise is pushed only for newly accrued ticks, so splitting a job does not reduce its total noise, only spreads it. *Why a short TTL:* no persistence punishes a cancel-to-peek, which is the Pillar 2 behaviour "I can leave now"; permanent persistence is a free ratchet for splitting a noise burst; proportional decay is more state for no gain. *Constraint on a neighbour:* splitting a job reduces peak Attention only if the gap is comparable to Attention's noise memory `TAU_ATT_TICKS` (1800, registry); `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS ≤ 2 × TAU_ATT_TICKS` is accepted by Attention (registry `noise_unit_relations` IR-3; Attention System, Designed — AT-3). *For incremental repair hp is the ledger:* a re-begin recomputes D.1 from the then-current hp over the full block count, so the work already applied is kept and the only loss on a one-tick-short abort is a fresh bar for the last block's basis points (a block-granular ledger for repair was proposed and rejected at the checkpoint: a second record of the same quantity could disagree with hp after M7 clamps, decay, `apply_damage` or another seat's repair). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **a restore writes no ledger entry.** `post_restore` drops the in-progress channel as `ABORT(RESTORE)` for presentation purposes only; it neither creates a partial-progress entry from the dropped record nor refreshes `abort_tick` of an existing one, so a save-restore cycle can neither start nor extend a resume window; entries that survived the snapshot keep their absolute `abort_tick` and age on global ticks as before. The one persisted record a restore does touch is the FULL-job part record, which commits or releases by the blocks-credited rule (IA-18); that is a part-stock transition, not a ledger entry. The honest cost of leaving, restated for the Player Fantasy: hp already raised stays; a cut, cap, crank or field check resumes only within `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`; a resumed PATCH recomputes over the full bar.)* *Decides:* R9, PCI Open Question 7.

**Group D — Repair, the part, and the patch**

**IA-17 Repair is real, incremental `repair_step`.** For `begin_patch` the hook, at every credited block (IA-14 row 8), calls `check_repair_step` and then M7 `repair_step(subject_kind, id, delta_bp, kind)` with `delta_bp` = `repair_step_delta` (D.1). `hp_at_begin` is read in the hook at the first step, never in `validate` (IA-11a). **Healthy-subject guard (Interaction's own, independent of the Graph's answer to the hp-clamp question):** the hook never calls M7 PATCH when `hp_at_begin ≥ LEAK_HP` **and** `cap_at_begin > PATCH_CAP_BP` (the subject has never been patched and nothing is leaking), and never calls M7 at all when `repair_step_delta ≤ 0`; such a job runs its full duration with zero mutator calls and resolves `PRECONDITION(NOTHING_TO_REPAIR)`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING, delegated G2 (the creative-director's Option B): the former threshold, `hp_at_begin` at or above the patch cap itself, left the silent band open: at true hp 4000 to 5999 a PATCH gained at most 1999 bp, permanently lowered `hp_cap` by 4000 bp and emitted the Graph's patch weep, which a healthy pipe does not, so a one-block tap-and-cancel probed a pipe's true state at the price of a block, the P4 failure Design Test 2 names. The guard is now keyed on the real harm: **both** `hp_at_begin` and `cap_at_begin` are read in the hook at the first step (the Graph's `node()` / `edge()` carry `hp_cap`, as M6's `SalvageRecord` does). A PATCH on a subject that is not leaking and has never been patched is "not a job a patch can do": silent damage is FULL work (Pillar 5), and the job runs the full bar and resolves `NOTHING_TO_REPAIR` identically at hp 10000, 5999, 5000 and 4000 with `hp_cap` 10000, with identical Graph state, descriptors and noise. Resume and top-up are kept: a half-patched subject has `hp_cap` = `PATCH_CAP_BP`, so the guard does not fire and the job continues (AC-IA-15, AC-IA-27, AC-IA-143, D.1 example 5). Leaking subjects (hp below `LEAK_HP`) are patched as before: their cold and hiss already tell the player. The Graph-side cure (M7 PATCH never lowers `hp_cap`, no weep before completion) stays open for the Graph owner (Open Question 10) and would make the guard a pure optimisation.)* A Graph answer `ABOVE_PATCH_CAP`, if M7 ever returns one, maps at the end of the bar to `PRECONDITION(NOTHING_TO_REPAIR)` as well (D.3), never to a distinct token. *Why:* otherwise a hallucinated-broken healthy pipe (hp `HP_FULL`) would have its `hp_cap` written down to `PATCH_CAP_BP` by LS-23 and go permanently DEGRADED — a hallucination physically damaging a module, breaking Pillars 1 and 4 together. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; **superseded by the re-review amendment above, which moves the guard to `LEAK_HP` and `cap_at_begin`**: the `PATCH_CAP_BP` threshold of the guard is arbitrary, and its residual is wider than "1 bp for a cap write". A hallucinated-broken pipe whose true hp lies in 4000 to 5999 is silent (no leak, because `hp ≥ LEAK_HP`), so no consequence channel warns the player, and a PATCH on it raises hp by at most 1999 bp while permanently writing `hp_cap` from 10000 down to 6000, a loss of 4000 bp of ceiling. The preferred cure is the Graph's: **M7 PATCH never lowers `hp_cap`** (it only ever raises `hp`, up to `min(hp_cap, PATCH_CAP_BP)`), which makes the guard a pure optimisation; this is routed to the Graph owner and must be answered before the repair story (Open Question 10). The interim recording of the damage range in Edge Cases B and AC-IA-90 is replaced by the guard.)* A hallucinated-fixed pipe at true hp 2000 receives real increments against 2000; its cold and its hiss persist until true hp crosses `LEAK_HP` (LS-22). A DESTROYED subject is never repairable (LS-23): the first `check_repair_step` fails, zero mutator calls, the job runs its full duration and resolves `PRECONDITION(NOT_REPAIRABLE)`. **Class A calls from a P3 hook are legal:** LSG:176 ("callable only inside a kernel-issued `WriteScope` in P2/P3") and LSG:186 (M7's caller is "Interaction (`begin_patch` channel steps)") both say so, and kernel CR-9 (KRN:104: a hook uses a published unconditional mutator or a SYSTEM-origin command) is satisfied because every step is check-then-apply: the check precedes the apply, a failing check skips the apply, and `apply` therefore stays infallible and is never FAULTED. A reader of CR-9 alone may object; both citations are given here for that reason. **The self-patch (object class ACTOR_SELF; Suit & Equipment, Designed — SE-22 / SE-40).** A declaration row of object class ACTOR_SELF (welder in hand, CHANNEL, `PATCH_CHANNEL_TICKS`, noise TOOL, payload `{target_ref}` = the actor's own PlayerId) lets the actor patch their own suit; its apply calls `set_channel_ref(actor, INTERACTION, channel_id, NONE)`: ACTOR_SELF has no target anchor, so `face_octant = NONE` and the Controller holds `facing_prev` for the channel (PCI D.8 rule 2) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6/G1, CONFLICT)*. Its incremental steps run at the IA-14 cadence and call Suit's `patch_integrity(actor, delta)` with `delta` from D.1 evaluated against `SUIT_PATCH_CAP_BP` (Suit's symbol, cited, never restated) and `integrity_bp` read **in the hook**, never in `validate`; the same healthy-subject guard applies (no call when `integrity_bp` already meets the cap; the job resolves `PRECONDITION(NOTHING_TO_REPAIR)`). There is no part and no FULL variant at MVP. The actor's own body is the honest actor channel, so reading `integrity_bp` is not an oracle question. The self-patch is an `act_gate`-exempt actor verb (IA-6) and, with the welder in hand, the Controller's focus (PC-6 self-target rule, fallback when no lit candidate is in reach and arc) makes it startable from the `interact` button with the lamp on or off; the focus never depends on `integrity_bp` (the guard above handles a healthy suit) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP: the owner adds the surface the Controller's ruled behaviour needs; IA-4, IA-5, IA-41 `self_target`)*: lamp on or off, with no other light, the press is accepted and the channel is not aborted `UNLIT`. *Resolved 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling G6 (GAP; the Controller's PC-6 option (a) is the spec; Emitter State's EM-21 rule that the owner's beam adds nothing to the owner's `act_lux_pm` stays intact, option (b) is not taken).* The self-patch story is no longer blocked. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated G6, GAP: the owner adds the surface the Controller's ruled behaviour needs. Replaces the 2026-10-27 routed acceptance condition and the design-review note that blocked the story.)* MVP if and only if Suit's integrity axis is MVP (SE-22 lists MVP damage sources, so it is). *Decides:* R4, R9, LS-18, LS-22, LS-23, Suit SE-22.

**IA-18 PATCH versus FULL, the part reservation, and the Item DB's AC-40 statements.** FULL if and only if the actor holds `parts_per_full` of `repair_part_for(subject_kind, kind_id, loop)` (`repair_part_for` cited by registry name, `parts_per_full` by the Item DB's own name; neither restated), or already holds a PAID record for the subject (a RESERVED record exists only while that seat's channel runs); otherwise PATCH. The choice is made in `validate` from the actor's own stock (the honest actor channel), so the channel's duration never depends on hp. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the stock rule above removes a decision. Whenever a part is stowed FULL is selected automatically, so a player cannot spend a cheap patch on a minor leak and keep the deep-derelict part for a real repair; that removes the Pillar 5 resource choice and fails Autonomy (SDT). **The selector is the actor's declaration, not their stock:** FULL if and only if the actor holds `parts_per_full` of the part **and has it readied** in Suit's hand rail (`part_ready(player_id, def_id)`, the actor's own selection, read in `validate`; reuses the IA-5 idea of "what is in hand"; a read of the actor's own state, never of hp, so it is no oracle), or already holds a PAID record for the subject; otherwise PATCH. A player holding the part who has not readied it completes a PATCH with the part untouched (AC-IA-135). The false claim that the bar class is "visible before the press" is withdrawn from Game Feel: `focus_view.duration_class` shows LONG pre-press only to a player who readied the part, which is their own choice told back to them, and nothing else on the plate shows the length class before the press. **Provisional on Suit:** `part_ready` and the hand-rail readying are a Suit ask (Open Question 42, cross-GDD note). Until Suit answers, the stock rule above stays in force as an interim and the autonomy defect stays open, labelled.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated G2 (creative-director: this undermines Pillar 5 more than any number): **the MVP selector is chosen, and there is one: the stock rule, with a mandatory pre-press tell.** The `part_ready` selector above is **not** an MVP rule: it rests on a Suit API that does not exist (the selector was "provisional on Suit" while IA-41, D.13 and AC-IA-91 already read it as fact), so two selectors lived in the same document. At MVP: FULL iff the actor's own stock holds `parts_per_full` of `repair_part_for(subject_kind, kind_id, loop)` or a PAID record exists, else PATCH; the commit at 100 ms is no longer an ambush because `focus_view` carries `part_tell` (IA-41), a bool true iff the focused V1 subject's declared kind has a repair part (`repair_part_for` not NONE) and the actor's own stowed count meets `parts_per_full`, drawn as a second notch on the keycap with no colour, numeral or pip (form proposed, HUD & Suit Layer owns the asset). It is a function of the declared kind and the actor's own stock only, never of hp or module-body state, so it is no oracle. A player who does not want to spend the part on this subject has the whole pre-press window to look elsewhere; a press that commits inside the first `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` costs nothing, and the Game Feel text now says exactly that. The `part_ready` / hand-rail selector stays the Suit ask of Open Question 42 as a **post-MVP upgrade** that would give the player an explicit choice; where it lands it replaces the stock rule and the tell changes meaning to "readied". Every "readied" in IA-41, D.13, AC-IA-91 and AC-IA-135 is therefore the post-MVP form, marked as such.)* The following statements discharge Item DB AC-40 and are normative:
1. `repair_part_for` returning `NONE` means **no FULL job is ever offered** on that subject (PATCH only); there is no `BLOCKED` for a missing part — FULL is simply not offered without it. (A SEAM subject returns `NONE`; a seam is restored by V9 re-weld.)
2. **A PATCH consumes no item.**
3. A FULL job issues only `kind = FULL` steps (IDB AC-38); a job never mixes kinds.
4. **A seat's part is spent when that seat's first productive step is applied** — a step that raises `hp` or raises `hp_cap` — through Suit's `consume_reserved` (Suit & Equipment, Designed — SE-41). A whole subject costs time and no part if no step is ever productive. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: restated by the commit rule below. A part is spent at the first productive step, or, if no step was productive, when the channel ends by cancel, abort or restore after at least one block was credited; it comes back only when the bar runs to its end with no productive step. "Costs time and no part" therefore holds only for a bar that runs to the end.)*
5. A FULL job interrupted after its first productive step keeps the part spent and the hp; there is no refund (IDB AC-38). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the same holds for a job interrupted after its first credited block, productive or not, so that the two truth branches cannot be told apart by what the interruption returns.)*
6. A FULL job re-issued by **the same seat on the same subject** within `FULL_JOB_GRACE_S` (Interaction's own constant, 60 s [PLACEHOLDER], carrying the Item DB's resume-allowance recommendation) is not charged again; other seats are charged. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the grace is measured from the last credited block of the interrupted job, not from the last productive step, so its length does not depend on module-body state.)*
7. A FULL job on a subject already at `hp_cap = HP_FULL` with `hp = hp_cap` consumes nothing and resolves `PRECONDITION(NOTHING_TO_REPAIR)` as an Applied-and-did-nothing outcome *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: only if the bar runs to its end; cancelled after a credited block it consumes the part exactly as it would on a damaged subject)*.
8. PATCH on a subject that is not leaking (`hp ≥ LEAK_HP`) and has never been patched (`hp_cap > PATCH_CAP_BP`): the prompt still appears (IA-11b); the job resolves `NOTHING_TO_REPAIR` unless the actor holds the part, in which case it is a FULL job — the Item DB's "offer only FULL there" reading is adopted as behaviour, and the hp-clamp question stays routed to the Graph owner. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING: was "a subject whose `hp` already exceeds `PATCH_CAP_BP`"; the guard of IA-17 is keyed on the real harm.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "holds the part" reads "has the part readied" under the declaration selector above.)*
9. DESTROYED is never repairable (IA-17): the path is salvage (scrap) then install.

**Part reservation and the commit rule (closes an oracle; orchestrator ruling, rewritten by the design review).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, BLOCKING: the former reservation released the part at `until = Tb + FULL_CHANNEL_TICKS` whatever the player did, so a player could press a FULL job, cancel at e = 7 to 10, walk away and read the pack at `until`: the part returned only if the module was truly healthy (a hallucination). That is a lie detector priced at "time and a held resource" the player pays while standing nowhere near the pipe, not lit, not loud, so its Pillar 3 and Pillar 4 price (exposure plus TOOL noise) was about 2 to 3 % of the channel's, and the former Edge Case and AC-IA-30 pinned the leak as correct. The creative-director ruling, game-designer's form, replaces it: the reservation outcome is **branch-independent** from the first moment the two truth branches could differ.)*

*Reservation.* When a FULL job is Applied, the part enters the state `RESERVED`. Suit treats a RESERVED or PAID part as having left the pack, in the stock count, in the carried mass that drives the weight cue and in its `reserved` bucket, **from the begin tick, in both truth branches** (SE-41). The record carries no timer: `until` is retired, and there is no expiry sweep.

*The commit rule.* Let `blocks` be the number of blocks the channel has credited (IA-14 row 8). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: `blocks` **is** the channel record's `blocks_done`, written by row 8 for FULL repair like every other verb, and `t_last_block` is the record's persisted field of the same name; a FULL job is never resumed through the ledger, so `blocks_done` is the run's own count.)* A block is credited in both truth branches whether or not its step was productive, so `blocks` is a branch-independent counter. The channel ends in exactly one terminal state, and the part record resolves with it:
1. **`DONE`.** The part was consumed at the first productive step (statement 4, `consume_reserved`); the record is `PAID(grace_until)` until the same hook run's completion closes it (NONE; the job is finished, so there is nothing left to resume) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: PAID is closed by the `DONE` event itself, never by a test of hp, see Re-begin below)*.
2. **`OUTCOME`** (the bar ran to its end and no step was productive: `NOTHING_TO_REPAIR`, `NOT_REPAIRABLE`). **This is the only path that returns a part:** `release_stowed`, the record becomes NONE. The `NOTHING_TO_REPAIR` outcome is the deferred reveal, paid for with the full bar.
3. **`cancel_channel`, any `ABORT(cause)` (`MOVED`, `UNLIT`, `OUT_OF_REACH`, `TARGET_GONE`, `BODY`, `NOT_ELIGIBLE`) or a restore** (a restore drops the channel as `ABORT(RESTORE)`, IA-36):
   - **`blocks = 0`** (the channel ended before its first block was credited): no step has been applied in either branch, the two branches are identical, and the part is released at once in both (`release_stowed`, record NONE).
   - **`blocks ≥ 1`**: the part is consumed **in both truth branches** (`consume_reserved` if it is still RESERVED; already consumed if a productive step made it PAID) and the record is `PAID(grace_until)` with `grace_until = t_last_block + ticks_for_seconds(FULL_JOB_GRACE_S)`, `t_last_block` the tick of the last credited block. A truly healthy module and a truly damaged one therefore leave the stock, the weight cue and the reserved bucket identical at every tick after any cancel, abort or restore. Believing a lie and acting on it costs a part, and that is honest under P4.

*What the player can still learn, and what it costs.* Pressing a FULL job and letting the bar run to its end tells the truth (a part comes back, or it does not): the whole bar, lit, stationary and loud. Cancelling before the first block (`e < GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`) tells nothing and costs nothing, because nothing has differed. Cancelling at any later tick tells nothing and costs the part. The cheap checks stay the consequence channel and the scanner (IA-30).

*Re-begin.* A re-begin by the same seat on the same subject while a PAID record exists is not charged again and issues only `kind = FULL` steps (statement 6); it reserves nothing. A RESERVED record exists only while a channel of that seat runs on the subject, so a re-begin never finds one. `grace_until` ends the record, as does the job completing or the subject being tombstoned or salvaged. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: **"completing" is the channel's `DONE` event, never a predicate sweep.** The former parenthesis, `hp = hp_cap = HP_FULL`, could be read as a per-tick test, under which a truly healthy module's PAID record would die the tick after it is created and a re-begin would charge a second part: a stock oracle (healthy and damaged modules would then differ at the re-begin). The record is deleted only by `grace_until`, by the `DONE` event of the FULL channel that closes a damaged subject, or by tombstone/salvage; it is not deleted by anything that reads hp. A re-begin on a PAID record credits blocks like any channel (IA-14 row 8) and, when it credits at least one, moves `grace_until` to its own `t_last_block + ticks_for_seconds(FULL_JOB_GRACE_S)`; one that credits none leaves it unchanged. Tombstone (`TARGET_GONE`) on a PAID record with the part already spent leaves the part consumed and the record NONE: nothing is refunded for a vanished subject.)* **Persistence.** `PAID` records persist across a restore (`grace_until` is an absolute tick, persisted like a throw's landing tick). A RESERVED record in a snapshot taken during a channel is resolved by `post_restore` from the persisted channel record's `blocks_done` by the rule above (release if 0, consume if at least 1), through Suit's Class B mutators, which are restored by then (`post_restore` runs after every block restores, CR-8); releasing a RESERVED part with `blocks ≥ 1` on restore would reintroduce the oracle (save at e = 10, restore, read the stock). Suit persists the reserved count (SE-38), so the totals stay conserved.

The reservation uses three Suit mutators, all Class B — `reserve_stowed(player, def_id, n) → bool` (false and no change if the stowed count is below `n`), `release_stowed(player, def_id, n)`, `consume_reserved(player, def_id, n)` *(Suit & Equipment, Designed — SE-41 — Suit has adopted them verbatim; Suit's `reserved` bucket is excluded from `stowed_count`, `stowed_mass_g` and the weight cue from the begin tick)*. **`validate` requires `stowed_count >= parts_per_full` with RESERVED and PAID parts excluded from the count, so `reserve_stowed` returning false inside `apply` is a defect (FAULTED, CR-9), never a path.** Diagnostics: `IA_RESERVE_RELEASE` (a part returned: `OUTCOME`, or `blocks = 0`), `IA_RESERVE_COMMIT` (a part consumed by a cancel, abort or restore with `blocks ≥ 1` and no productive step; a debug counter, never presented). Remaining leaks, accepted and stated: after the nominal end the stock tells the truth (the cost is already paid); the Graph's consequence channel is by design not an oracle to hide; and a player who runs the bar to the end learns the truth at the end. **Accepted cost (D10b):** a held part is spent on a subject a patch would have met, and the prompt cannot warn because that would read hp; the ready-part declaration above is the player's lever. **Ruled** (Open Question 34): the creative-director's branch-independent form is the user's ruling, confirmed 2026-10-27 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; qa-lead's alternative (a), which kept the former release timer and priced the early cancel with a noise debt (a TOOL push owed for the unspent remainder of the bar), is a closed alternative and is not built. *Decides:* R9, R11, IDB-19/20, AC-38/40/89, R6, design review 2026-10-27.

**IA-19 Patch decay and the fairness gate.** `PATCH_CAP_BP` and `PATCH_DECAY_S` are the Graph's registry constants (this GDD is added to their `referenced_by`): a patched subject stays DEGRADED sev 1, re-leaks after `PATCH_DECAY_STEPS` (LS-23), endogenously and gated (the gate is open because the sev-1 descriptor has been live since the patch). Interaction adds no decay and no progression value (LSG:222: nothing in the Graph rewards repair). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D39, default confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20): the patch's remaining life is made visible, not rewarded; the patch-plate bead's segment count (IW-18) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* follows the banked hp (D.1b), so "cure now or make it last" is a legible choice, and Interaction still writes no value.)* **Player verbs are EXOGENOUS and bypass the Graph's fairness gate** (LS-13, `fairness_gate`, `W_MIN_WARNING_S` per registry): the gate protects the simulation against itself, not against agency, and a player's death is Suit's rule. Consequence, stated once: salvaging the node that is running the ship — the only scrubber, the battery during a SCRAM — starves that room on that run (LSG:773); the hum of the thing being cut was the tell, and the "stupid but plausible death" is nameable afterwards. Interaction never writes the gate's number. *Decides:* R28, LS-13.

**Group E — The Graph verbs**

*Ownership clause (the Graph's LS-6, repeated verbatim as the Graph's Open Question 9 requires, LSG:74 and LSG:1158; Layout's side is LY line 136):* **Layout owns** room identity, polygon, `V` (tiles) and `exposed_tiles`; the Graph caches `V` and `exposed_tiles` as derived fields (not hashed; rebuilt in `post_restore`, which is why `restore_after: [layout.state]`). **The Graph owns** the atmosphere — `n_o2`, `n_inert`, `T`, `t_rem`, `fuel_puddle_ml` — and every opening/hatch `area` and `door_state`, every VALVE `open` and every breaker `closed`; Interaction issues the commands, the Graph publishes the mutators (R11; one writer per fact, kernel H3).

**IA-20 V3 breaker and V5 valve: TAP toggles against true position.** The payload of `set_breaker` and `set_valve` is `{target_ref}` and nothing else. `validate` runs `act_gate`, `BUSY` and the Graph's kind check (`check_set_breaker`, `check_set_valve` — names inferred from LS-18's `check_*` pattern; the Graph spells only `check_repair_step` and `check_install_module` — CABLE_JUNCTION or seam cable; VALVE or seam pipe or duct sub-edge). `apply` reads the **true** current position and calls M9 or M8 with the opposite — a payload built from the displayed position would be a believed state (LS-22), and toggling the true position makes a lying gauge cost a real physical consequence: a player who trusts a false "closed" panel and presses to "open" in fact closes it, the inrush and the lamps answer, and no reject or hint intervenes. The Graph's `read_view` (LSG:199) publishes no switch position today; Interaction therefore needs one Graph-published position read: `switch_pos` for a breaker (`closed`) and a VALVE (`open`), and `opening(edge_id) → {area, area_max, door_state}` for an OPENING *(provisional — a combined request routed to the Graph owner: Layout asks the Graph for the same read under its own name `opening(edge_id)`, Layout, Designed — LY-10, so two siblings ask for one read and the Graph owner receives one request; Interaction adopts Layout's name for V4 and V6 and keeps `switch_pos` for VALVE `open` and breaker `closed`)*. **Design-order precedence: the Graph owner's answer precedes any Interaction story for V3, V4, V5 and V6, which cannot be built without it.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the Graph has answered. LS-18 and LS-19 additions (life-support-graph.md, 2026-10-27 consistency pass) publish the pure checks `check_set_valve`, `check_set_breaker`, `check_salvage_node`, `check_cap_port`, `check_reset_node`, `check_set_opening_area`; the switch position as `node(id).switch_pos` (breaker) and `edge(id)` `open` (VALVE); `opening(edge_id) → {area, area_max, door_state}`; `open_ends(node_id)`; and confirm that the M8/M9 kind verdict is direction-neutral and that V6 is M13 on an exterior OPENING with no `vent` kind flag. The names Interaction proposed are therefore adopted as published, and the "inferred name" and "provisional" tags in this rule and in IA-24 to IA-27 are historical. Still open on the Graph side: the hp-clamp answer (Open Question 10) and V7 in LS-26 (Open Question 4).)* `apply` may read it (it runs in P2 and the position is not a health field); **`validate` must not**, and the lint of IA-11a extends to it, because a `validate` that branched on position would turn "no-op" into a reject. Closing a breaker onto a load triggers the Graph's inrush (M9 sets `energised_at_step` on the branch's loads; `INRUSH_PM`, `INRUSH_STEPS`); Interaction pushes **no** extra stimulus for it — the Graph's `power_use` is Attention's power stimulus and counting it twice would double-count. The result detail is empty (`{}`): the new state is visible only through the world. The panel's own indicator is module-body and may lie; the arc, the lamp brightness and the hum are the witnesses. The Graph's `check_*` for M8 and M9 take a state argument while `validate` wants a direction-neutral kind verdict; this is a confirmation, not a new API: LS-18's validation column for M8 ("edge kind VALVE or seam PIPE/DUCT sub-edge") and M9 ("CABLE_JUNCTION or seam CABLE sub-edge") depends on kind only, so `validate` may pass either direction and the verdict is direction-independent by the Graph's own text *(provisional — the Graph owner is asked only to confirm)*. *Alternative considered:* a payload that carries `closed`/`open` — rejected (LS-22, KRN CR-4). *Decides:* LS-18, LS-22, R16, the systems-designer's finding S2.

**IA-21 TAP verbs never reject on body state, and re-arm.** Restating IA-11 for the taps so it can be linted on its own: V3 and V5 never reject on a FAILED node or a "closed-already" display; the only TAP with a body-state precondition is the door attempt (IA-24), which is Applied and resolves as an outcome. **Re-arm guard:** a TAP on a subject actuated fewer than `TAP_REARM_TICKS` (12 [PLACEHOLDER]) ticks ago is answered immediate `BUSY` — geometry-and-timing, not module-body state, so no oracle. Its purpose is to stop 60 Hz breaker cycling used as an inrush generator and to give a door leaf time to travel; the door-travel time is the Layout's, and the guard must be at least that (ask to Layout; Layout, Designed — LY-9). The re-arm window of a door runs from the end of its attempt. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `rearm[]` is keyed by the door (`door_ref`), not by face, so a press on the far face cannot step around the guard; `TAP_REARM_TICKS` has a floor of 1, never 0, because a zero guard is no guard (Tuning Knobs). With the Graph's default of a binary `area` the door leaf's travel time is one tick (Graph OQ 21 still names the animator; a stepped slide would re-open I-8's Layout half).)*

**IA-22 V6 emergency vent (HOLD_CONFIRM, destructive).** Declared on VENT_HATCH objects (an OPENING whose far side is EXTERIOR). The player holds `interact` for `CONFIRM_HOLD_S`; one `set_opening` is then submitted. **V6 follows the IA-20 toggle rule:** `apply` reads the opening's true `area` (the Graph's `opening(edge_id)` read, provisional) and calls M13 with `area_max` if it is 0, else with 0; HOLD_CONFIRM applies in **both** directions and `verb_name_key` is the state-neutral `verb_vent_hatch`. The declaration's `vent` flag (not the payload) marks the row `destructive` and names `area_max` as the open target; it no longer fixes a direction, because a mode or label that differed by direction would read the opening's true position into the prompt (IA-11f), and LSG:797 prices refilling a vented room, so a close must exist. Paying 0.8 s on the harmless close is cheaper than a second row. The effect is the Graph's: the compartment vents through the hole (LSG:797), and venting is "the nuclear option" because the pool is finite. A vent hatch is a manual release — no motor, no power precondition, so no attempt window *(Layout, Designed — LY-9)*. One-shot LOUD noise at apply. The hold is the only guard: a confirmed vent while the actor is inside the vented compartment proceeds (the verb is exogenous; death is Suit's rule). **CONFLICT routed:** the mutator for V6 is M13 on an exterior OPENING *(provisional)*; LS-18 names neither M13 nor M10 for V6. M10's keys (`vent_pp`, `pump_throttle`) are machine setpoints, so M13 is the only mutator whose effect matches LSG:797 ("the open hole", `A_hole` in the Graph's D.7); the ask to the Graph owner is to confirm V6 = M13 and to say whether an exterior OPENING needs a `vent` kind flag. *Decides:* R3, R8.

**IA-23 V9 weld and `begin_weld` (declared here, registered by Assembly).** A WELD_SOCKET with the welder in hand declares the CHANNEL `begin_weld`, registered by Assembly *(Modular Ship Assembly, Designed — MSA-1, MSA-30; registry `assembly_commands`)*, with `eligible` = Assembly's `weld_offer` (registry). Interaction's contribution: the declaration row (tool, mode, noise class TOOL, `verb_name_key`), `act_gate` as the shared precondition vocabulary (`OUT_OF_RANGE`, `NOT_LIT`, `BUSY`, `NO_TOOL`), the shared abort set (IA-15), and the shared `channel_view`. Assembly owns `validate`, the duration (`WELD_CHANNEL_TICKS` 360, registry), `weld_join`, `can_join` and the `WELD_DP_WARN` warning (`can_join` warns, never rejects). Welding moves nothing: seam sub-edges start disconnected, and V3 closing the seam cable merges the islands (LSG:781). **The section cut and install (declared here, registered by Assembly; Modular Ship Assembly ASK I1 (iv) to (vi)).** `begin_seam_cut`: object class WELD_SOCKET in state `WELDED`, tool `tool_cutter`, CHANNEL, `SECTION_CUT_CHANNEL_TICKS` 240 (Assembly's, registry), noise TOOL per accrued tick and one LOUD one-shot at DONE (IA-33), `eligible` = Assembly's `seam_cut_offer` (registry), contention class WELD (IA-34). `install_module`: object class HARDPOINT, `tool_required` NONE, TAP, precondition: the seat carries an instance whose def is in `compatible_defs(h)` (static Item DB data, so IMMEDIATE), `admitted_while_carrying` true, owner Assembly, noise QUIET. **The channel record is Interaction's (MSA-30):** both `begin_*` applies call Interaction's Class B `open_channel` (IA-35), so the weld and the seam cut run on the IA-14 clock, abort set, ledger and noise exactly like Interaction's own verbs; Assembly runs no clock. **Stance (Modular Ship Assembly ASK L2, joint with Layout):** a WELD_SOCKET subject's anchor and `REACH_PX` leave a legal lit stance outside both hatch rectangles; the authoring lint is `SOCKET_STANCE_BAND_EMPTY` (Layout's and Derelict Authoring's to run). *Alternative considered:* Interaction registers `begin_weld` itself — rejected: a second handler for Assembly's atomic cross-block apply (CR-9) or a forwarding shell. *Decides:* R1, L33.

**IA-24 V4 doors: the foot box, the uniform attempt window and the closing retry.** A door registers **two face subjects** (EM:326; Layout places two DOOR_FACE anchors per door `ANCHOR_CLEARANCE_PX` outside the leaf span, Layout, Designed — LY-23) so either side can be worked. A PLAYER-origin `set_opening` toggles the leaf. (a) **Closing while a foot box overlaps the leaf** fails `validate` with immediate `OBSTRUCTED`, through Layout's pure query `leaf_would_overlap(door_ref, area, foot_boxes[]) → bool` (Layout, Designed — LY-11, D.5; `door_ref = (section_id, door_index)`). The foot boxes are **arguments**: `validate` passes Body's `foot_boxes(body_id)` **unfiltered** — every actor on the body, including a SEAT whose Controller row is unbound *(Ship Body & Interior Physics, Designed — BDY-18; Layout never reads `body.state`)* — with `area` 0. `validate` does not read the true aperture (IA-11a) and does not know whether the press will open or close; it always tests the would-be closed leaf (`area` 0), which is harmless on a closed door because no foot box can overlap a SOLID leaf — the test therefore bites only on a close. Items are not foot boxes. The leaf stays open. (b) A **SYSTEM-origin** close (a failure cascade) never fails `validate`: it carries an explicit `area` in its payload — the simulation is not a believer, LS-22 does not bind it, and a toggling SYSTEM close would open an already-closed door. It has **no attempt window**: its `apply` takes the P2 path of (d) at once. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **a SYSTEM close supersedes pending PLAYER attempts.** When a SYSTEM-origin close for door D is applied, every open PLAYER attempt on D (state `WINDOW` or `CLOSING_BLOCKED`) is dropped in that same apply and ends with no outcome token; otherwise a PLAYER OPEN whose window had not yet expired would re-open, one window later, a door a failure cascade had just sealed, defeating the cascade (and the Pillar 2 fairness of a sealed breach).)* (c) **The attempt window (PLAYER origin) and its completion.** Every PLAYER door command that passes `validate` is Applied and creates a door attempt in `attempts[]` — Interaction's for **both** origins, the record keeping an `origin` field and an `attempt_id`; it is **not** a channel: no `channel_ref`, no bar, no movement lock, not aborted by movement — whose `dir` is fixed at apply from the true leaf position. **M13 is never called from the P3 hook:** Layout LY-10 (ii) asks the Graph owner to confirm that no Graph phase writes `area` or `door_state` (provisional until answered); on that reading every `area` change lands in P2 and `layout.doors` (P3, `after: []`) reads same-tick state, which is why Interaction never calls M13 from its hook. At `T == Tb + DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` the hook reads the motor's `served_pm` (a P3 read, permitted by IA-11a). If it is 0 the attempt resolves `PRECONDITION(NO_POWER)` into `last_result` directly — nothing changes, so no command is needed. Otherwise the hook submits a SYSTEM-origin `set_opening {target_ref, area}` (CR-3: hooks may submit SYSTEM-origin commands, KRN:71; `area` = 0 for CLOSE, `area_max` for OPEN) whose payload stays `{target_ref, area}` (the apply finds the attempt by `door_ref` and `origin` in `attempts[]`, which holds at most one open attempt per door *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: it was per face, so presses on the two faces of one door could each hold an attempt and conflict; the key is now the door, both faces share the one record and the one `rearm[]` stamp, and a press on either face while an attempt is open is `BUSY`)*); its `validate` is kernel-only. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **completion re-reads the leaf.** The SYSTEM `set_opening` apply reads the true `area` (a P2 apply may, IA-20) and, if the leaf already matches the payload's target (`area_true == area`), skips `check_set_opening_area` and M13 and simply ends the attempt, so a completion can never toggle a leaf a cascade moved in the meantime.)* (d) **The overlap test and `CLOSING_BLOCKED`.** The SYSTEM command's `apply`, in P2 of T+1, runs for a close `leaf_would_overlap(door_ref, 0, foot_boxes)` with the current `foot_boxes(body_id)`, unfiltered (the boxes committed at the end of the previous tick): a clear leaf → `check_set_opening_area` (a name inferred from LS-18's `check_*` pattern; the Graph spells only `check_repair_step` and `check_install_module`) then M13 (the leaf is projected by `layout.doors` in the same tick, LY-10); an overlapped leaf → the attempt is marked **`CLOSING_BLOCKED`** (Layout's name, LY-11). While the attempt is `CLOSING_BLOCKED` the hook waits for a clear tick and re-submits the SYSTEM command once then, and M13 lands in the P2 after it. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: **the retry is gated by a pure read, not resubmitted blindly.** The former "re-submits the same SYSTEM command every tick" flooded the command and replay log in co-op: up to 300 SYSTEM commands for a PLAYER close and an unbounded number for a SYSTEM close. The hook now tests clearness each tick with the pure `leaf_would_overlap(door_ref, 0, foot_boxes)` read (Layout LY-11; no command) and **submits the SYSTEM `set_opening` once, on the first tick that read is clear**; if the apply in P2 finds the leaf overlapped again (a box stepped back in), the attempt returns to `CLOSING_BLOCKED` and waits for the next clear tick, so submissions are bounded by the number of clear-to-blocked cycles, not by ticks. A foot box held in the leaf for the whole 300-tick timeout therefore yields at most 2 SYSTEM commands for that door (the window-end submission and one on the first clear tick, if any), identical in every replay hash, and a 10-door cascade at most 20; AC-IA-147. The pure read in the hook is an `Interaction` P3 read of Body's committed boxes and costs no command slot.)* A SYSTEM-origin close from a cascade takes the same P2 path with no window. **No outcome token is published for a late overlap** (`OBSTRUCTED` is an immediate reject only, the initial PLAYER `validate`); the tell is the door's power-neutral latch strain (below), then the leaf that does or does not travel. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **`CLOSING_BLOCKED` now has an exit.** A PLAYER-origin attempt in `CLOSING_BLOCKED` ends after `CLOSING_BLOCKED_TIMEOUT_TICKS` (Tuning Knobs, [PLACEHOLDER] 300, range 120 to 900) with the leaf left open, no outcome token, and the door's re-arm stamp started, so a crewmate standing in a doorway cannot make the hook re-submit forever and a player who changed their mind is not held by a pending close. A SYSTEM-origin close has no timeout: its cascade owns it and re-issues it, and it is dropped only by being applied or by a restore.)* An opening command (area above 0) needs no overlap test. `attempts[]` is **not persisted**: a SYSTEM close lost at restore is re-issued by its cascade, and a PLAYER attempt lost at restore costs the player one more press. The window is **uniform for powered and unpowered doors** (orchestrator ruling): otherwise response latency would be a free power oracle. The cost is the window plus a power-neutral latch cue; the unmoved leaf is the consequence-channel answer. A TAP on a subject with an open attempt is `BUSY`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, creative-director ruling, confirmed by the user on 2026-10-27 (Open Question 43, D38): the window used to play a motor whine for `DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` on an unpowered door, which is a false world statement (a motor that is not spinning is heard spinning) and violates Pillar 1, the section's own Serves pillar; the uniform-latency rationale is sound, the cue chosen to satisfy it was not. **During the window both branches play a power-neutral mechanical cue, true in both: the latch or clutch straining, then the manual-brake release (a clack at the window's end). The motor sound plays only when the leaf actually travels**, i.e. from the tick M13 lands, in the powered branch only, and that is a consequence of the world, not of the press. The window's audio and visual event streams are identical across the power branches and contain no motor-spin character; for a player who cannot hear, the same window shows a latch-bolt strain on the door frame (a 2-frame state of the frame's latch part, not the leaf and not the status lamp, identical in both branches, held for the window), so the window is perceivable without sound and still not an oracle (AC-IA-128).)* The seam hatch is a door-kind OPENING whose pressure facts are the Graph's (`SEAL_STRAIN`, `WELD_DP_WARN`, cited by name). **Routed three-way (Graph owner, Layout owner, producer):** who animates `door_state` between an M13 call and the leaf. LY-9 derives the leaf rect from the Graph's `area` every tick and says that if `area` jumps 0 ↔ `area_max` in one tick the leaf is binary by consequence; LY-11 says the overlap check runs on every tick of a closing sweep. If the Graph sweeps `area` over ticks, the per-tick halting during the sweep is theirs and Interaction only guards the M13 call; if `area` is binary, Interaction's guard is the whole invariant. *Decides:* R17, L53, LSG:192 ("door motor power is Interaction's precondition"), LY-9, LY-11.

**IA-25 V8 salvage: the cut, the yield, the stubs.** `begin_cut` is a CHANNEL (cutter in hand, SALVAGE_MODULE object, `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS`) whose effect lands **at completion only** — the cut is one atomic salvage, resumable through the ledger, and an abort at any point leaves the module in place. At completion the hook calls `check_salvage_node` (name inferred from LS-18's `check_*` pattern; the Graph spells only `check_repair_step` and `check_install_module`), then M6 `salvage_node` → `SalvageRecord`, then `salvage_yield(rec)` (deterministic; no random draw; the loot roll is the Item DB's) to create the item instance or instances; it creates one **LOOSE_WORLD** item per yielded instance through `place_loose`, passing the instance unchanged (IA-28), at the node's `anchor_o` (adjacent-cell search per D.5) — **never auto-carried**; the player picks it up with `pickup`. It unregisters the node's OBJECT subject (E7), and for each fluid port the Graph reports as `open_end` it registers one STUB subject (E6) and records `{stub_ref, node_id, port_index}` in `stubs[]`. One-shot LOUD noise at completion. At a `DONE` completion (an outcome writes nothing) the hook also writes `last_salvage_T := T`, published as the pure derived read `last_salvage_T()` (an int64 tick, or NONE before the first salvage) so Save/Load can poll it under SL-5's gate, the same pattern as Assembly's `last_shape_change_T()` (MSA-33; Boarding ASK-BS-I4, BS-21) (registry: `last_salvage_T`). A salvage of a node that is ABSENT or SALVAGED at completion resolves `PRECONDITION(NOT_SALVAGEABLE)`. The `salvageable` flag is def data: a non-salvageable def has no declaration row, hence no prompt, and a forged submit is `INVALID_TARGET`. The cut leaves its ports `open_end` (hiss, SEVERED mode) until capped. Patch state persists through salvage and reinstall by the Item DB's launder-closing rule (IDB-18; closed at this holder by IA-28's full-instance `loose[]` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: Item DB IDB-15/16/18 and registry item_instance_to_array, G1/G6)*). Salvaging the running scrubber is legal and stated in IA-19. *Needs from the Graph:* a read for the open ports of a salvaged node, name proposed `open_ends(node_id)` *(provisional — request to the Graph owner)*. *Decides:* R3, R12, IDB-11, LSG:772.

**IA-26 `begin_cap` (V8's follow-up).** A CHANNEL: welder in hand, STUB object, `CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS`, no part, ledger-resumable. The payload is `{stub_ref}`; because a stub is a subject the player addresses it by focus like any object, and the hiss is its tell. At completion the hook maps the stub to `(node_id, port_index)` from `stubs[]`, calls `check_cap_port` (name inferred from LS-18's `check_*` pattern; the Graph spells only `check_repair_step` and `check_install_module`) then M12, unregisters the STUB subject (E7) and deletes the entry. The Graph's `PORT_ABSENT` and `PORT_INCOMPAT` pass through as outcomes. If the player never caps, the hiss persists (the consequence channel); no timer forces anything. A stub already capped by another seat is `INVALID_TARGET` (immediate). *Decides:* R3.

**IA-27 V12 hand crank.** `begin_crank` is a CHANNEL: bare hand, CRANK_MACHINE object (any node that can SCRAM or FREEZE), `CRANK_CHANNEL_TICKS`, ledger-resumable, noise TOOL. At completion the hook calls `check_reset_node` (name inferred from LS-18's `check_*` pattern; the Graph spells only `check_repair_step` and `check_install_module`) then M11 `reset_node`. Outcomes: the node is not in SCRAM or FREEZE → `PRECONDITION(ALREADY_RUNNING)`; the Graph returns `BLOCKED(STILL_HOT)` (`T_mach ≥ OVERHEAT_OFF_MC`) or `BLOCKED(NO_FUEL)` (`starve_steps > 0`) → the command succeeded and did nothing, and the player reads why from the world (LSG:775). The prompt appears on every CRANK_MACHINE regardless of its state (IA-11b). *Decides:* R3, L27.

**Group F — Carrying, throwing, falsification, harm, revive**

**IA-28 Pickup, drop, loose items.** Interaction owns `loose[]` in `interaction.state` — `{item_id, section_id, pos_px, inst}` with the position **section-local** (`section_id`, `pos_px`; Layout LY-22: "a weld or a detach moves nothing") — and registers an ITEM subject for each (EM-24: "Interaction owns the dropped-item registrations"). `inst` is the Item DB ItemInstance in its only persisted and hashed form, the six-element array from `to_array()` = `[def_id, count, condition_bp, cap_bp, patched_at_step, charge]` (IDB-15/16, registry `item_instance_to_array`); there is no separate `def_id` and no separate condition field — the def is `inst[0]`, one copy, so the two cannot diverge. `item_id` stays outside the instance (IDB-15: an instance id is not schema) and is minted from `next_item_id`, or reused from the `(instance_id, instance)` pair that `release_carried` returns. No Interaction code hand-builds an instance Dictionary. **Identity rule (lint-checked):** between `place_loose` or `release_carried` and the next `set_carried` or `add_stowed`, no Interaction code reads-then-rewrites `condition_bp`, `cap_bp`, `patched_at_step` or `charge`; a free drop and pickup returns exactly the dropped instance and spends no part, which closes the launder (IDB-18). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: Item DB IDB-15/16/18 and registry item_instance_to_array, G1/G6)* **Layout reserves no item table: the loose-item holder is Interaction's** (Layout, Designed — LY-22, "the loose-item holder is Interaction's `interaction.state`"), which closes the three-way that Suit SE-13 routed to the producer. Class B `place_loose(inst, section_id, pos_px)` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: Item DB IDB-15/16/18 and registry item_instance_to_array, G1/G6)* places one instance; its callers are (a) the yield path (IA-25), which passes each instance `salvage_yield(rec)` returns unchanged, so a patched module (`cap_bp` 6000, `patched_at_step` S) is placed with `cap_bp` 6000 and S; (b) the world-build seed path, which passes the instance the Item DB's pure `fresh_instance(def_id, count, condition_bp)` (IDB-28) builds from Derelict Authoring's `{def_id, count, condition_bp}` placement; (c) `replace_tool`'s discarded instance and any shed or hand-over (States and Transitions row 14), which pass the instance they hold. The seeding path consumes Derelict Authoring's seeded `{def_id, count, condition_bp}` placements, expanded to instances by `fresh_instance`, at the anchors of Layout's `loose_slots(section_id)` at world build, in the order `build_section` → Graph M1 → Emitter E1 → Interaction `sync_section` (LY line 456, LY-25) *(Derelict Authoring and Boarding & Salvage, Designed)*. `pickup` (TAP, lit): a CARRIED-class def needs hands free — meaning no carried instance (`carry_ref` unset); a tool in the HAND holder (Suit SE-12) does not block (Boarding ASK-BS-I2) — else `BUSY`; it moves LOOSE_WORLD → CARRIED, setting `carry_ref` through the Controller-published `set_carry_ref` (IA-36) **and** the instance through Suit's `set_carried(player_id, item_id, inst)` (SE-13, SE-40), passing the entry's `inst` unchanged — the two calls write different blocks (`controller.state` and `suit.state`), and Suit's `post_restore` asserts `controller.state.carry_ref == suit.state.carried_id` (`SUIT_RESTORE_CARRY_MISMATCH`) — and unregisters the item subject (E7). A STOWED-class def validates with Suit's `can_stow(player_id, inst[0], inst[1])` and is rejected with Suit's own immediate reason **`STACK_FULL`** (actor stock; Suit-namespaced) when the stack cannot take it; otherwise it moves LOOSE_WORLD → STOWED through Suit's `add_stowed(player_id, inst)` with the entry's `inst` unchanged, merging per IDB-15. A TOOL def picked up while the held tool's charge is 0 calls Suit's `replace_tool(player_id, instance) → discarded` (SE-40); the returned `discarded` instance is placed as a loose item by D.5 (orchestrator ruling: a pickup deletes nothing; if Suit's discard means destruction this clause is replaced). All Suit names are *(Suit & Equipment, Designed — SE-13 / SE-40)*. While carrying: gait is forced to `V_CARRY`, **every `interact` mode except `drop` and the declared carry-admitted rows is `BUSY`** — while carrying, `interact` on a HARDPOINT subject whose `compatible_defs(h)` lists the carried instance's def is admitted (Assembly's `install_module`, MSA-26), alongside `drop`, and so is V4 `set_opening` on a door face *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated G2 (creative-director: pressure should come from choices, not an unreadable lock-out): a TAP needs no free hands and the lamp is chest-mounted, so a carrier traverses a closed door without dropping; the lit rule and the uniform attempt window of IA-24 apply unchanged; the former drop, open, pick-up cycle was a flow dead-end in the dark, where pickup is lit-gated. Flag `admitted_while_carrying` on the V4 row, a Controller PC-8 carve-out is routed, Cross-References section 3)* and `pod_hop` on the POD_CONSOLE (Web Map WM-9: `carry_ref` travels with the pod; `berth` and `unberth`, the DOCK_CONSOLE rows, are not carry-admitted) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; the flag is `admitted_while_carrying` (IA-2; Modular Ship Assembly C1, Boarding ASK-BS-I1, Web Map ASK-I1; the matching PC-8 carve-out is routed to the Controller owner, Cross-References section 3); `begin_patch`, `begin_cut`, `begin_weld` and `begin_seam_cut` stay `BUSY`, the beam stays available (user decision), stagger does not drop the module, downed does (PC-8). `drop` (TAP, **exempt from the lit rule**) places by D.5 (`DROP_OFFSET_PX` in front of the foot origin, else the nearest free cell among the eight neighbouring cells one tile out, else immediate `OBSTRUCTED`), calls Suit's `release_carried(player_id) → (instance_id, instance)`, clears `carry_ref` through `clear_carry_ref`, builds the loose entry from the returned instance unchanged (`interaction.state` holds **no** `carried_item_id`) and re-registers the ITEM subject. Layout's LY-11 ask is adopted: door cells answer `NONE` from `floor_tile_at`, so D.5 never places on a door cell and the neighbour-cell search is the "snap to the nearest legal cell"; an authored item on a door cell is Layout's lint `LOOSE_ON_DOOR`, not an Interaction state. Dropping in the dark places an item that cannot be re-taken until something lights it — a deliberate Pillar 3 cost. A downed actor's carry: the Controller has already cleared `carry_ref`, and Interaction converts the module to a loose item at the foot origin on the next hook run by calling Suit's `release_carried` (SE-13: "the Controller clears `carry_ref` and Interaction converts it to a loose item next tick by calling `release_carried`"); the NONE-cap conversion is the same call. The forced drop's last fallback is the foot origin regardless of overlap; a carried module is never deleted and never refused. Interaction's `post_restore` never touches `carry_ref` or `suit.state.carried_id`. A landed glowstick is spent, never a pickup (IDB-12(c)). Weight is not speed or noise **within Interaction** (IDB-21): no weight readout and no Interaction noise push for carrying; **a stowed haul adds no carry noise from Interaction at MVP** (answers Item DB Open Question 4). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: X3 / D71 / D50 / D49: Attention adds a legible carry intake (per the ruling about 7,000 mAU per tick while a HEAVY item is carried, Attention's to recalibrate) as a stimulus the player chooses, never a hidden timer; that intake is Attention's own, computed from the carry state it reads, and is not an Interaction noise class: `noise_push` (D.9) is unchanged, Interaction supplies classes and never units, and `focus_view.carry_state` is the only carry fact it publishes (IA-41). Freezing after the cut is priced because the intake keeps running while cargo is held. The intake's trigger (carried versus stowed HEAVY), the recalibrated weights and theta, and the longer reference carry are Attention's, Boarding's and Derelict Authoring's; the Item DB's D18 ruling is that any stowed HEAVY item disables run.)* **Routed wording CONFLICT:** the Item DB instance table (IDB:443) says the CARRIED → LOOSE_WORLD move on `drop` is "Performed by: Controller"; Interaction owns `drop` (KRN:85, PCI:205, PCI:682, IDB:470) and writes the Controller's block only through its published mutators. *Decides:* R12, PC-8, R27, LY-22, SE-13.

**IA-29 Throw.** `throw_glowstick {aim_angle}` (`throw`, Q or RB; the Controller PC-2 moved the pad binding from R3 to RB, re-review 2026-10-27; provisional UX binding). `validate` order: not carrying, not channelling, not staggered, not downed → `BUSY`; stock via Suit's read → `OUT_OF_CHARGES` (both immediate). No lit requirement (an actor verb). `apply`: spend one stowed glowstick through Suit's `spend_stowed` (accepted = spent, Item DB instance-table row 10) *(Suit & Equipment, Designed — SE-40)*, compute the landing by D.4 (Layout's `clip_to_footprints(body_id, from, to)` returns the first SOLID hit cell and Interaction lands the stick on the pixel before it; door leaves are SOLID rects, LY-9, so a stick never passes a closed door), append `{land_tick = T + THROW_ARC_TICKS, land_px, owner, life_ticks = ticks_for_seconds(GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S) − THROW_ARC_TICKS}` to `throws[]`. At `land_tick` the hook calls Emitter State's E3 `spawn_deployable(kind, land_px, owner, life_ticks)`; a false return (a dormant or unknown section, or outside any section) leaves the item spent and the throw resolves silently (EM:699, `IA_THROW_VOID`). The throw does not change locomotion or lock movement; **the light is never pre-played** (it appears at landing only, like every light in this game); a landing in a closed room lights only that room; `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_LIVE` is enforced by Emitter State (the lowest-id non-evicting stick of the same kind enters a visible evict-wane, never an instant removal). A throw in flight persists across a save. One-shot LOUD noise at landing. A thrower who dies while a stick is in flight still sees it land (a world object). `THROW_RANGE_TILES` 3 (1 to 5) and `THROW_ARC_TICKS` 30 (15 to 60) are registered as Interaction's [PLACEHOLDER] at exactly the values the Controller (D.16), the Item DB (IDB:259) and Emitter State (EM:198) already cite (F1: a placeholder in a table of record becomes a fact). Chemlight `#86B896` is the stick's light colour only, never a UI colour. *Alternatives considered:* a lit-only throw (defeats throwing into the dark, which is the item's point); an arc via physics (non-deterministic); a refund on a dormant landing (a free test). *Decides:* R13, EM-15, IDB-12, the user's glowstick decision.

**IA-30 Field check.** `field_check {subject_ref, check_kind}`; the MVP `check_kind` is SCAN only (scanner in hand; Vertical Slice A adds HEFT, the lift-and-weigh, and PALETTE). A lit-only CHANNEL of `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS`, ledger-resumable. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, BLOCKING: **the call site is the hook on the completion tick, never `apply`.** In this GDD `apply` runs at the begin tick, so a call from `apply` would have Sanity DEBUNK an active lie at e = 0, a free oracle at the press (the lie ends, or visibly does not, before any time, light or noise is spent); the `outcome` argument also exists only at completion. The hook calls `record_falsification` exactly once, **on the terminal tick of the channel, whether it ends `DONE` or `OUTCOME`** (IA-14 row 9; the SCAN check has no body-state outcome at MVP, so `DONE` is the live case), and **never** at begin, at a resume-begin from the ledger, on `cancel_channel`, on any `ABORT(cause)` or on a restore.)* The hook then calls the Sanity-owned Class B mutator `record_falsification(player_id, check_kind, subject_ref, outcome)` (registry: `record_falsification`; Sanity & Hallucination Rules, Designed), which writes a **Sanity SIM block**; `outcome` is the channel result only, and Sanity derives whether a lie existed from its own ledger (Sanity C5); what the scanner reveals and whether it can itself lie is Sanity's. **No check result is ever stored in `interaction.state` as truth about a lie** (CR-12). The second-instrument check is a different gauge elsewhere on the ship, reached by walking there and `inspect` (art bible 7.0), never the inspect card of the suspect gauge; the scanner reading, and `suit.o2` / `suit.warmth` for the seat's current compartment (`suit.warmth` for the COOLANT loop's thermal quantity only), also count as rung-2 instruments (Sanity L-2I, C21); any simulation-visible falsification result is a `field_check`. The cost is time, light, a tool in hand and noise: the declared noise class is TOOL (the scanner loop, pushed per accrued tick, IA-33), so a stationary scan is never free of Attention (Sanity OQ-SN-13). Calling `record_falsification` from REPAIR or INSPECT completions is not adopted (Open Question 32). *Decides:* R14, KRN:202, IDX:25.

**IA-31 The friendly-harm act.** `begin_harm {target_player}` is a CHANNEL declared for object class ACTOR with the **cutter or welder** in hand, `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS`. The gate order in `validate` is locked (kernel CR-7): (1) kernel authority (the issuer owns the actor); (2) the immediate checks — tool (`NO_TOOL`), `act_gate` (`OUT_OF_RANGE`, `NOT_LIT`), `BUSY`; (3) the target is an ACTOR subject whose PlayerId differs from the actor's (`INVALID_TARGET`); (4) Sanity's `actor_sanity_below_harm_threshold(player_id)` (registry: `actor_sanity_below_harm_threshold`; false for an unknown PlayerId) over the SIM sanity-level block, **never `PERCEPTION_OUT`** (`NOT_ELIGIBLE`) *(Sanity & Hallucination Rules, Designed)*; (5) apply. The declaration's `eligible` predicate is the same query, so **no prompt appears for a sane actor** (no accidental harm, no intentional PvP). The prompt's `verb_name_key` is the equipped tool's ordinary verb (`verb_cut` or `verb_weld`) and **never** a target noun — a noun would make the HUD an oracle over a crewmate drawn as a monster (art bible 7.0: keybind and verb, never condition). The effect: every `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` of progress, Suit's Class B `apply_suit_damage(victim, HARM_BP_PER_STEP, source)` *(Suit & Equipment, Designed — SE-40)*, with `HARM_BP_PER_STEP` from D.8, totalling at least `HARM_TOTAL_BP` (10200 at the defaults). **What Interaction guarantees, and what is Suit's.** A completed act delivers `HARM_TOTAL_BP ≥ SUIT_HP_FULL` as EXOGENOUS damage through `apply_suit_damage(victim, HARM_BP_PER_STEP, source_ref)` (`source_ref` classed EXOGENOUS, Suit & Equipment, Designed — SE-23; the build relation `HARM_TOTAL_BP ≥ SUIT_HP_FULL` is registered on this side, Suit's symbol cited). **When death arrives is Suit's rule** (LS-13 "player death is Suit's rule"; SE-23, SE-24): `source_ref` is classed EXOGENOUS, so with `SUIT_EXO_BYPASS_LIMITER` at its default 1 (SE-23; Suit OQ-3 records it as the data form of the user's lethality decision) the integrity rung is set from the new `integrity_bp` on the run after each per-step apply, with no hold — STRAINED below `SUIT_HP_OK`, CRITICAL (the Controller's stagger on the falling edge, then walk-only) below `SUIT_LEAK_HP`, FAILED (cap NONE) below `SUIT_HP_FAIL` (Suit's symbols, cited). The victim's rung therefore tracks the damage inside the channel; a completed act leaves the victim FAILED (cap NONE, DOWNED) on the run after the last step; the victim's Pillar 2 tell is the channel itself (the tool loop and sparks, AD-7) and D.8's escape arithmetic, not the SE-17 hold floor. The first integrity band brings no stagger (the cap is FULL at STRAINED, SE-16; the stagger is CRITICAL's falling edge); the victim may self-patch (IA-17) while above `SUIT_HP_FAIL`; FAILED → `locomotion_cap` `NONE` → DOWNED → DEAD after `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` unless an axis leaves FAILED or a `begin_revive` lands (IA-32) — which is what gives revive an owner. With the knob flipped to 0 (a data flip, reversible by the user only) the uniform limiter applies to every source and FAILED arrives no sooner than the SE-17 floor (AC-SE-24). The user's decision is honoured: the act is lethal and permanent against a victim who cannot or does not respond, and Pillar 2's tell window is honoured for the victim. Completion requires the victim to remain in reach for the whole channel; a conscious victim with room to run takes only part of `HARM_TOTAL_BP` (D.8). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "room to run" has a cliff that D.8 now states. The step that drops integrity below `SUIT_LEAK_HP` (step 11 at the former 500 bp default, e = 66; step 17, e = 102, at 300 bp) sets the CRITICAL rung and the Controller's 30-tick stagger, which freezes a victim who is still inside reach; every block end inside the freeze then lands (five more steps at 300 bp, integrity at most 3400, CRITICAL; the act is lethal for a victim who stays in reach to step 26). The guaranteed escape is therefore "out of reach by the check of step `k_trap`", about 65 ticks, not the 60-tick or 20-step window the former text implied; a victim who is carrying could not make it by moving at all at 500 bp (at 300 bp a carrying victim with `t_react` of at most 29 can), and otherwise must drop the module (a tap, lit-exempt) and run. Whether that guaranteed lethality for slow victims is intended was Open Question 44; the user ruled on 2026-10-27 (D36) to lower `HARM_BP_PER_STEP` to 300 (D.8 derivation): the step that crosses `SUIT_LEAK_HP` is now step 17 (e = 102) and a walking victim of any `t_react` 0..60 from `d0` = 0 is out of reach before it; it gates Vertical Slice B, not MVP.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* The channel aborts when the victim leaves reach or goes unlit, or the Sanity query flips at a re-check (IA-14 rows 4 to 6); damage already dealt stays dealt. **No reward path:** no XP, no kill counter, no score. Interaction calls Sanity's Class B mutator `record_harm_event(actor, victim, tick)` (registry: `record_harm_event`) from the completion-tick hook at harm-channel completion (*Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "inside its apply" read as the begin-tick apply, which is wrong for the same reason as `record_falsification`; the harm event is recorded once, when the act completes*); scars, logs and the attribution to the web are Sanity's and the Codex's; the victim's tell is the Perception Filter's (the violet fringe, ART:563). The MVP is single-seat: the act is designed and testable only with stub actors; the revert-to-down-only switch (concept risk) is an Open Question, and the knob `SUIT_EXO_BYPASS_LIMITER` (default 1, SE-23) is that decision in data: a flip to 0 is the playtest revert, reversible by the user only (Open Questions 2 and 3). **Self-knowledge ruling (CD-GDD-ALIGN; closes the former known risk).** The prompt's existence, and the immediate `NOT_ELIGIBLE` on a forced attempt, are a one-bit statement about the actor's *own* sanity relative to the harm threshold. That is honest self-knowledge, which Pillar 4 allows (the concept: harm is possible only while the attacker's sanity is "verifiably below threshold" and the attacker carries "a visible sanity sign", CON:252). It reveals nothing about the world, any module, or what a figure truly is, and no sanity meter or number exists (guard rail 4 is reworded to say so). Sanity & Hallucination Rules is *recommended* (not bound) to give that state a diegetic sign, so the plate is never the first place a player learns it. The kept ruling shows no prompt for a sane actor. **Vertical Slice B precondition (BLOCKING; closes Open Question 5).** An `E · Cut` plate on a crewmate drawn as a horror would otherwise tell that figure apart from a real horror for free, because real horrors take no verb and show no plate: a class-(c) hallucination falsifiable at zero time, risk or trust, which fails the Pillar 4 design test. Therefore **no Vertical Slice B `begin_harm` story may be scheduled, marked ready or merged** until the Sanity & Hallucination Rules and Horror Entities GDDs record both: (1) the eligibility bit and the class-(c) render level are one SIM bit; and (2) every real HORROR subject carries a `repel` declaration with the same `verb_name_key` (`verb_cut` or `verb_weld`) and the same tool, `lit` and `in_reach` prompt rule, duration class, bar length, noise class and ack and cue set as `begin_harm`, so the plate, the bar and the cues cannot tell a crewmate drawn as a horror from a real horror. What `repel` does is Horror's and Attention's (tools repel; nothing is attacked). "HORROR subjects take no verb" (guard rail 1, IA-39, AC-IA-113) holds at MVP only. Until the precondition is met `begin_harm` stays stub-actor-only (AC-IA-126). At MVP (single seat, no crewmate) the leak cannot occur. *Decides:* R15, KRN CR-7, user ("death is a bitch. It happens."), anti-pillars.

**IA-32 Revive (Vertical Slice B, dormant).** `begin_revive {target_player}` is a CHANNEL on a DOWNED ACTOR subject, lit, any tool or none, `REVIVE_CHANNEL_TICKS`; completion calls Suit's `revive(victim)`, raising the locomotion cap *(Suit & Equipment, Designed — SE-40)*. Declared now because the lethality of IA-31 is coherent only if the mercy window has an owner (PCI:188 names the verb as Interaction's). Dormant at MVP (single seat). *Decides:* R24, PC-9.

**Group G — Cross-cutting rules**

**IA-33 Noise.** The declaration carries `noise_class` in {QUIET, TOOL, LOUD}. As channel owner, Interaction calls Attention's intake `push_stimulus(NOISE, source_ref, class)` once per *accrued* channel tick for TOOL-class verbs (cutter, welder, crank, scanner) and one-shot LOUD events at cut completion, seam-cut DONE (Assembly's `begin_seam_cut`, MSA-29), vent apply, throw landing and the `berth` apply (the pod console's `berth`, ruled LOUD; `unberth` and `pod_hop` stay QUIET) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)* *(Attention System, Designed — AT-3: the API and the class set {QUIET, TOOL, LOUD} confirmed)*. A tick that aborts before accrual pushes nothing. Units are Attention's; the Controller's `NOISE_PER_TICK` (walk, run, carry) is cited, never restated. A breaker close pushes nothing (the Graph's inrush is Attention's power stimulus through `power_use`); a door motor pushes nothing. Interaction states three relations Attention's units must satisfy, or the verbs become free or absurd: the TOOL-class per-tick unit lies between 2 and 6 so a 180-tick patch integrates to at least twice the walking noise of the same time and no more than 1.5 times the running noise; a LOUD one-shot is at least 30 ticks of the TOOL unit; and `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS ≤ 2 × TAU_ATT_TICKS` (IA-16) — Attention accepts all three (registry `noise_unit_relations` IR-1 to IR-3: `2 ≤ U_TOOL ≤ 6`, `U_LOUD ≥ 30 × U_TOOL`, `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS ≤ 2 × TAU_ATT_TICKS`). Attention never reads Audio (IDX:34). **Pillar 3 trace (ruled: it does not depend on sparks).** The Pillar 3 price of any channel is carried by two things that exist regardless of how Open Question 18 resolves: the target must be lit (IA-6, IA-7), so the job is done in light the web can see through Emitter State's exposure, and a dark job is impossible but for the three listed exceptions; and the tool's TOOL-class noise push on every accrued tick (above). Weld and cut sparks are non-emissive at MVP (art-director AD-6: no Light2D, not an Emitter State emitter), so the trace never rests on them. The decision is explicitly reversible: if playtest shows welding does not feel costly to the web, Emitter State may register a short-lived spark emitter at the tool-grip anchor; that only adds a cost and changes no Interaction rule, formula or acceptance row except the spark palette lint (R-7). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **the price needs a tell, and the tell has an owner.** A TOOL push that nothing answers is a hidden stat, which Pillar 3 forbids. The tell of Interaction's noise is Attention's (its noise-driven attention tell and consequence, the web's answer, AT-3 and its tell outputs) voiced by Adaptive Audio (cue keys owned there); Interaction pushes the class and nothing else, and adds no reaction of its own. The test is AC-IA-136, a playtest row: after a TOOL push a consequence is perceived within about 10 s and named by most testers. A note on the trade: the scanner dominates hand-checking as a way to resolve a hallucination, because it costs less than a whole repair bar; that is accepted and stated in Player Fantasy: the hands answer the world, the scanner answers Sanity (Open Question 32).)* *Decides:* R16, P3, PCI D.17 / Open Question 8.

**IA-34 Contention.** One in-progress job per `(subject_ref, verb class)`; the verb classes are REPAIR (V1, PATCH and FULL), SALVAGE, CAP, CRANK, CHECK, WELD and ACTOR_ACT (harm, revive). WELD covers Assembly's `begin_weld` and `begin_seam_cut`, keyed on the host socket Assembly derives (Modular Ship Assembly D.3), so the two sockets of one pair contend as one subject (MSA-37, ASK I1 (x)). A second seat gets immediate `BUSY` — the crewmate's body and beam at the object are the visible reason. Solo: a second `begin_*` during a channel is `BUSY`; nothing queues (PCI:84). The Item DB's AC-89 (two seats, one kit each, same FULL repair on the same step → exactly one kit spent, the second job answered `BLOCKED` with no part consumed) is satisfied in intent: the second seat's begin is `BUSY` at `validate`, makes no reservation, and exactly one part is spent. The token differs from AC-89's wording because commands dispatch sequentially, so the second begin is rejected before it could be Applied; routed to the Item DB owner as a wording refresh. As belt and braces for Vertical Slice B (should the exclusion ever be relaxed), the hook processes seats in ascending PlayerId, and a FULL step on a subject already at `hp = hp_cap = HP_FULL` is skipped: the job resolves `PRECONDITION(NOTHING_TO_REPAIR)` and consumes no part. Dispatch is sequential by kernel rule: CR-2's P2 row (KRN:63, "per command in order: authority check -> validate -> apply or reject") and KRN:69 ("P2 commands see earlier P2 effects in the same tick"). *Decides:* R21, IDB-20(7), PC-2.

**IA-35 Cancel across owners.** `cancel_channel` is Interaction's one cancel type (KRN:85) and cancels whatever `controller.state.channel_ref` names, stored as `(owner_system, channel_id)`. Every channel record lives in `interaction.state`, whoever registered the verb: Assembly's `begin_weld` and `begin_seam_cut` applies create theirs through Interaction's Class B `open_channel(seat, owner_system, verb_id, target_ref, duration_ticks)` (registry: `open_channel`; Modular Ship Assembly, Designed — MSA-30, C4), which writes the IA-14 record with that `owner_system` (consulting the IA-16 ledger for `resume_offset`, so an aborted weld or cut resumes inside `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`, AC-MSA-50) and changes nothing else (the caller's apply calls the Controller's 4-argument `set_channel_ref` itself, with its own `face_octant`) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6/G1, CONFLICT)*. `cancel_channel` therefore ends any record `ABORT(CANCELLED)` in P2, so no accrual happens that tick; Assembly publishes no cancel (registry `assembly_commands`: `abort_channel` absent by decision). At an ASSEMBLY-owned DONE Interaction applies nothing but the `channel_ref` clear and the ledger delete (IA-14 row 9); Assembly's `assembly.step`, ordered after `interaction.step`, reads the DONE handoff in `channel_view` and submits its own SYSTEM-origin completion. The Adapter never learns the owner (the same principle as "the Adapter never chooses the verb"). *Alternatives considered:* each owner registers its own cancel — the Adapter would have to know the owner; an Assembly-published `abort_channel(actor)` (R22's provisional route) — refused by Assembly (MSA-30) and closed. *Decides:* R22, PC-6, MSA-30.

**IA-36 The block, the Controller mutators and restore.** One block `interaction.state`: class `SIM`, `persist true`, `persist_scope WORLD`, scope `SHARED` with per-seat records keyed by **PlayerId** (never the transport peer identifier), `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY` (a crewmate's channel in progress is visible — the Pillar 1 human channel), `schema_version 1`, `restore_after [layout.state, controller.state, graph.state, body.state, emitter.state]` (`layout.state` because `sync_section` needs geometry at restore: Layout LY-22 and LY lines 442 / 992 require it; `body.state` because IA-37's `post_restore` rebuilds the registration window `W` from the restored body positions (`pos_curr`, alias `pos`, Ship Body BDY-5/BDY-6) and the predicates read them; Emitter's own `restore_after` naming `body.state` is not relied on transitively) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; contents in States and Transitions. Kernel CR-9 lets a block be written only by its owner's mutators; the Controller publishes them (PC-1 b, registry `controller_class_b_mutators`): `set_channel_ref(actor, owner_system, channel_id, face_octant)`, `clear_channel_ref(actor)`, `set_carry_ref(actor, item_id)`, `clear_carry_ref(actor)`.
