Interaction's applies call them. `face_octant = octant_of(anchor_o(target_ref) − foot)` (Controller D.8; `anchor_o` the target subject's anchor, `foot` the actor's `pos_curr`, both in the actor's body frame, the pair `in_reach` uses, Controller D.9 and D.2 here) is computed by the verb's apply at the same tick it opens the record; it is NONE for object class ACTOR_SELF and for a zero vector. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6/G1, CONFLICT: the Controller is the publisher and its 4-argument signature stands; the former unpublished-name wording and the 3-argument form are withdrawn.)* **`post_restore`:** drop every in-progress channel (the Controller clears `channel_ref` itself, PC-17; Save/Load SL:191 "a restore drops channel progress"), drop door attempts and clear `last_result` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: with the dropped channel, a RESERVED FULL-job part is resolved first by the commit rule of IA-18, from the persisted channel record's `blocks_done` and `t_last_block` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: both are written by row 8 for FULL repair and persisted, so a consumed part's `grace_until = t_last_block + ticks_for_seconds(FULL_JOB_GRACE_S)` is computed from the record, not re-derived)*: released if 0, consumed if at least 1, in both truth branches; the restore writes no partial-progress ledger entry, IA-16)*; **keep** the partial-progress ledger, the PAID FULL-job records and their grace entries, `throws[]`, `loose[]`, `stubs[]`, `last_salvage_T` and the id counters; re-register all subjects idempotently (IA-37) before the first `emitter.place` (EM-21). Each `loose[].inst` is decoded with the Item DB's `from_array(a, graph_step_no)` in `post_restore` (`graph.state` is in `restore_after`; `graph_step_no` is the restored Graph's absolute 10 Hz `step_no`); a wrong length, a wrong element type or a failed `validate_instance` fails the restore with the closed reason `INTERACTION_RESTORE_LOOSE_INSTANCE`, live state untouched, never clamped (Save/Load SL-15 items 5-6). Interaction's closed restore-failure reason list is exactly {`INTERACTION_RESTORE_LOOSE_INSTANCE`}. No `schema_version` bump (format 1 has not shipped). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: Item DB IDB-15/16/18 and registry item_instance_to_array, G1/G6)* Interaction's restore never touches `carry_ref` or `suit.state.carried_id` (Suit asserts their equality, `SUIT_RESTORE_CARRY_MISMATCH`). A save while CHANNELLING is legal (a soft gate with the `RISKY` tag, SL-5). Save/Load's block inventory (SL:276) must gain `interaction.state` — a refresh owed, not edited here. *Decides:* R25, R27, R4, SL-7, PC-17, EM-22 (the lit bit is canonical and survives).

**IA-37 Subject registration lifecycle and budgets.** Interaction exposes Class B `sync_section(section_id)`: it registers OBJECT subjects for every declared interactable of the section that lies inside a bound body's registration window (two face subjects per door) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D37, was every declared interactable of the section; the window rule is below)*, reading every `anchor_px` it registers from Layout's `subject_anchors(section_id) → [{local_key, kind_hint DOOR_FACE|HARDPOINT|SOCKET|SLOT|CONSOLE|INSTRUMENT, console_role POD|DOCK, anchor_px, room_id}]` (`console_role` present iff `kind_hint` == CONSOLE; Layout; the home airlock registers 2 console subjects, a dock room at a derelict 1, so Derelict Authoring's D.17 counts +1 subject in every set containing `home_ship` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: ASK-CT-13 / ASK-CT-14 accepted, delegated G6, GAP)*) (Layout, Designed — LY-23: "Layout places, Interaction names the kind and registers"; two DOOR_FACE anchors per door `ANCHOR_CLEARANCE_PX` outside the leaf span; per-object `anchor_o` comes from here only), ITEM subjects for the loose items placed in it, and STUB subjects for its stubs, and unregisters all of them on discard or dormancy; it marks only the subjects that changed, and creates one STUB subject and `stubs[]` entry for every uncapped Graph `open_end` a RAGGED seam cut leaves (Modular Ship Assembly ASK I1 (vii)). It is called by the section instantiate and discard atomic apply *(Layout, Designed — LY-23)*, by the dormancy apply — a hop that sets a derelict dormant and a `pod_settle` that wakes one, with no discard *(Web Map & Transit, Designed — WM-10, WM-14, WM-24; WM ASK-I1)* —, by Assembly's install, weld and seam-cut applies *(Modular Ship Assembly, Designed — MSA-26; C10)*, and by Interaction's own `post_restore`. A dormant section **must** unregister its subjects, or the subject cap is breached at the moment two sections are welded together. Interactable count is a content budget, linted below `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` with a 25 % margin: `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` 120 (fixtures plus seeded loose items plus cap-stub subjects, summed over all sections loaded at the same time) and `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE` 24 [PLACEHOLDER] together stay at or below 144, with actors at most 4 and horrors at most `HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` (2, Horror Entities, registry) (other owners' numbers quoted as a lint budget) and slack at least 42. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **salvage-yield ITEM subjects were not budgeted.** A `begin_cut` completion registers one ITEM subject per yielded instance (`salvage_yield` returns one instance of the module's def, or `scrap_count` parts for a DESTROYED node, IDB-17), created by the player's action exactly as a drop is. They are budgeted as the term `Y` of D.10 and share the player reserve, `P + Y ≤ PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE`; no new constant is added and the I-7 sum is unchanged. To keep a finished cut from leaving an unregistered, un-pickable item, `begin_cut`'s `validate` refuses with immediate `OBSTRUCTED` when `subject_headroom()` is below the def's static worst-case yield `max(1, scrap_count)` (a budget reason, not body state, as for `drop`). The S4 figure is re-checked in D.10. Which route clears the S4 WARN was Open Question 30, since ruled by the user (D37 route (a), below).)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R21: the stale owed-decision marker is closed; D37 is the decision)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: ruled, D37 route (a), see below.)* **The legal loaded sets are Derelict Authoring's** (registry `legal_loaded_sets`, confirmed by Web Map WM-24): `S_int` = F + L_seed + C is 70 / 102 / 113 / 113 / 145 for S0 to S4, and S4 `{home+B, A}` exceeds `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` by 25 (OQ-DA-20) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: Derelict Authoring L_seed B 3, one loose placement fewer)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D37, route (a) of Open Question 30 is ruled, so the former lint "ERROR above 120 on S0 to S3 and WARN with the shortfall on S4" is superseded: the lint is per registration window, below, and the S4 WARN is retired. The loaded-set figures stay as Derelict Authoring's registered upper bound. At S4 the loaded `S_total` = 145 + 24 + 4 + 2 = 175 ≤ `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` 192 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: Derelict Authoring L_seed B 3, one loose placement fewer)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*, so nothing fails dark, and no nearest-subject cull exists; "resolved by proximity" in Boarding ASK-BS-I5 (d) means focus, not registration.)* **Registration window (D37, user ruling: register subjects by body adjacency or proximity, not by loaded section).** `sync_section` still runs at the same callers and now maintains only the section's **candidate table** (declared interactables from Layout's `subject_anchors`, the loose items placed in it, its stubs: data, not Emitter subjects); a candidate becomes a subject (Emitter `register_subject`, E6) only while it is inside the **registration window** `W` of a bound body: the rooms (`room_id`, from `subject_anchors`) whose hop distance from a bound body's current room is at most `SUBJECT_WINDOW_HOPS` ([PLACEHOLDER — derive at spike], provisional 2), a hop being one door, seam hatch or dock link, counted across welded seams and docks. The hook recomputes `W` only when a bound body's `room_id` changes and on restore, section instantiate, discard and dormancy, at most once per tick, in room-id order; a candidate registers on entering `W` and unregisters when its room is beyond `SUBJECT_WINDOW_HOPS + 1` (a one-hop hysteresis, so a body standing on a threshold never flaps). Items the player creates (drops, salvage yield) sit at the player's feet and so are always in `W`. A subject outside `W` is unregistered and answers exactly as an unlit one (EM-21), which is correct because `in_reach` cannot be true for it; AC-IA-144 asserts that every candidate in reach from every walkable foot position is in `W`. `W` is a function of body positions, a SIM fact, so it is deterministic; `post_restore` rebuilds it from the restored positions, reading the restored `pos_curr` of `body.state` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, and persists no new state. **The budget is re-scoped** to the window: `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` 120 and `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE` 24 keep their values and the I-7 relation, and the lint is `W_int(p) = F + L_seed + C` summed over the rooms of `W` and its hysteresis ring (hop distance up to `SUBJECT_WINDOW_HOPS + 1`), at most `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` at every authored body position `p` of each registered loaded set (D.10). Because a window is a subset of its loaded set, `S_int` of `legal_loaded_sets` (70 / 102 / 113 / 113 / 145) is an upper bound on `W_int`: S0 to S3 pass by construction (113 ≤ 120), S4 passes iff every window excludes at least 25 of its 145 subjects, and the runtime total never exceeds the loaded sum (175 ≤ 192) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: Derelict Authoring L_seed B 3, one loose placement fewer)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* The S4 verdict depends on room-level subject counts that Derelict Authoring and Layout own, so it is measured, not assumed ([PLACEHOLDER — derive at spike]). A runtime overflow (E6 returns false and the object fails dark, which reads as a hallucination) is a content defect, counted `IA_SUBJECT_OVERFLOW`, never a gameplay state. A `drop` or `place_loose` needs a headroom read from Emitter State (provisional name `subject_headroom()`); a player `drop` with no headroom is refused `OBSTRUCTED`, so a dropped item can never be left unregistered and un-pickable. Two further content lints guard the prompt (they are consequences of IA-11b, which makes every repairable-kind subject prompt whatever its hp): `INTERACT_ANCHOR_MIN_SPACING_PX` 24 [PLACEHOLDER] (below about 8 px focus is unstable regardless of `FOCUS_STICKY_MARGIN`) and `MAX_PROMPT_CANDIDATES_IN_REACH` 3 [PLACEHOLDER] (counted on geometry, ignoring `lit`). *Decides:* R20, EM-21/24, the registered `legal_loaded_sets` (`S_int` 70 to 145, *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: Derelict Authoring L_seed B 3, one loose placement fewer)*; the earlier 156-to-190 estimate is retired) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*.

**IA-38 Inspect is a permission rule only.** `inspect` (R or RT) is a presentation-only context switch (`inspect_card`, PC-2), not a simulation command, permitted only on a lit instrument. In-World Instruments owns the card and renders the `perceived` value only (the Perception Filter's output), so the card lies exactly when the gauge lies (art bible 7.0) *(In-World Instruments & Inspect View, Designed — IW-21)*. The card ships live at MVP (IW-21; closes PCI Open Question 4 on this side and Open Question 16). The INSTRUMENT class carries the state-neutral prompt key `verb_inspect` (Instruments OQ-IW-29). *Decides:* R18, ART:747.

**IA-39 What Interaction refuses.** No interaction stat, no hidden skill, no weight readout, no sanity meter or number (the harm prompt's presence is the ruled one-bit self-knowledge of IA-31, not a readout), no reason string on screen (the kernel never displays strings), no Attention meter, no damage numbers beyond `HARM_TOTAL_BP`, no accuracy, cooldown, weapon, XP, kill counter, repair score or salvage reward. HORROR subjects take no verb at MVP (tools repel; nothing is attacked). A rejection is a cue, never a shake (HR-13). No inventory screen, no crafting recipe. *Decides:* G3, G4, anti-pillars.

**IA-40 Control-plane interactions.** *Pause:* the tick stops, no diegetic verb is admitted (`PAUSED`, never queued), channels do not advance, ledger TTLs do not age (ticks do not advance). *Body transfer* (`pod_hop`): aborts a channel (`ABORT(BODY)`); `carry_ref` travels with the pod (PCI:694). *Stagger* clears the channel; *downed* clears channel and carry. Suit's `locomotion_cap` is read by the Controller, not by Interaction. *The pod console (CONSOLE object class; Web Map & Transit, Designed — WM-8, WM-9; Chart Table, Web Map & Menus, ASK-CT-1).* Two CONSOLE subjects, told apart by Layout's `console_role` on the `subject_anchors` entry (ASK-CT-13, ANSWERED 2026-10-28), carry the declaration rows: the **POD_CONSOLE** class (role POD; every dock room) carries `pod_hop` (carry-admitted); the **DOCK_CONSOLE** class (role DOCK; the home airlock only) carries `unberth` (priority 1, `eligible` additionally requires Web Map's `berthed_present()`) and `berth` (priority 2, `eligible` additionally requires NOT `berthed_present()`), so `select_verb` (IA-5) remains a function of (class, tool, eligible) and the DOCK plate shows `unberth` while a record is berthed. All rows: bare hand, TAP, state-neutral `verb_name_key`, `eligible` = Web Map's `console_ready(role)`; noise unchanged (`berth` LOUD, one U_LOUD push at the berth apply by Interaction, `unberth` and `pod_hop` QUIET *(Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): D59, route 1)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*). Both subjects are lit-gated (IA-6). The console press is the only trigger; the Adapter injects the host-local `ChartPick` held by the Chart Table into `pod_hop` at the POD press and into `berth` at the DOCK press; a press needing a pick with none valid opens the chart (presentation; no command is submitted); `unberth` takes no payload and never opens the chart (Chart Table CT-10 R1 to R8). **Stale-pick echo (ASK-CT-14, ANSWERED 2026-10-28):** the POD and DOCK prompt plates carry a small text-free glyph slot filled from the Chart Table's host-local read `pick_glyph_key` (the node glyph the chart draws for the pick, or HOME's glyph at a derelict POD press, R1); no name, ordinal, distance or cost (IA-39 unchanged); no timer clears the pick. Refusals are Web Map's closed WM-9 reasons, shown as words on the Chart Table's register plate (WM:1029) — the Chart Table's presentation of Web Map's reasons, never an Interaction prompt or reason string (IA-39); the payload-less POD press at HOME with a derelict berthed is routed to Web Map and answers `BERTH_PENDING` (WM-9, CT-10 R6). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: ASK-CT-13 / ASK-CT-14 accepted, delegated G6, GAP)* `pod_hop` (POD_CONSOLE) is carry-admitted (IA-28); `berth` and `unberth` (DOCK_CONSOLE) are not; all three stay lit-gated (IA-6). *Decides:* CR-16, PC-9, WM-8, WM-9.

**IA-41 The two published read views, and the HUD's closed read list.** Interaction exposes read views only and never draws or calls presentation (IA-1); the HUD draws the prompt plate and the bar at `world_to_hud(anchor_o)` (the single projection of the unjolted camera pose; Pipeline C11). Both are pure, computed after the tick commits, integer, enum or key only — no strings, no node references — and keyed by PlayerId. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, BLOCKING: the HUD read contract could not be implemented. The HUD may read only `focus_view` and `channel_view`, yet the bar needs the hold-to-confirm fraction (this rule and AC-IA-64 said it is "in neither view", and the HUD GDD names it as an open ASK to the Controller, so each GDD assumed the other published it), the Applied, Rejected, Dropped and Superseded result events with their `queued_tick` (needed to withdraw an ACK), the carry state for the drop prompt, and a second prompt for HARDPOINT install versus drop; and a terminal state vanished on a re-press at T+1. **The HUD's read list is now closed at exactly four sources:** (1) `focus_view(seat)`; (2) `channel_view(seat)`; (3) **`input_view(seat)`**, the Adapter-local read `{hold_pm, armed, device_class, pressed_action}` described below, owned by the Controller (a Controller ask, which is the HUD GDD's Controller ASK (HS-6, CF-HS-16) under a new name; the HUD's provisional names `confirm_view` and `pressed(action)` are subsumed); (4) **the result-event record** `{seat, command_id, queued_tick, kind}` with `kind` in {APPLIED, REJECTED, DROPPED, SUPERSEDED}, a projection of the kernel's CR-5 result event for the local seat's own commands that **carries no reason**: the HUD plays one frame set for every rejection and never reads which reason it was. Interaction publishes (1) and (2) only (AC-IA-75 unchanged); (3) and (4) are named here so that the HUD story has a complete, closed, lintable list. The Controller must adopt `hold_pm` before any HUD story is written (Open Question 45).)*

| View | Field | Values / meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `focus_view(seat)` | `focus_ref` | 0 = none, else the single focused registered subject (Adapter-chosen, re-validated by `in_reach`); when `self_target` is true, the seat's own PlayerId tagged ACTOR_SELF (never a registered subject) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP)* |
| | `anchor_o` | world px; default = footprint centre (Controller D.9); a door's two face subjects each carry their own; passed to `world_to_hud` only; absent when `self_target` is true *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP)* |
| | `self_target` *(added 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP: the owner adds the surface the Controller's ruled behaviour needs, PC-6)* | bool: true iff the Adapter's nominated focus is the actor's own suit (object class ACTOR_SELF; IA-4, IA-5). Then `focus_ref` is the seat's own PlayerId tagged ACTOR_SELF (never a registered subject), `anchor_o` is absent (the HUD places the plate by the chest keep-out, HUD HS-39), `verb_id` is `begin_patch`, `mode` CHANNEL, `duration_class` MEDIUM (`PATCH_CHANNEL_TICKS`, no part), `tool_required` the welder, `verb_name_key` `verb_patch_self`, `glyph_action` `interact`, and `prompt_state` is ENABLED (the row is `act_gate`-exempt: no lit and no reach test, so neither HIDDEN-for-unlit nor NEEDS_TOOL can occur); a function of the declaration row and the tool in hand only, never of `integrity_bp`. `press_blocked_moving` follows the CHANNEL rule as for any CHANNEL plate |
| | `verb_id`, `mode`, `duration_class` | the IA-9 id, `TAP` / `HOLD_CONFIRM` / `CHANNEL`, and `SHORT` / `MEDIUM` / `LONG` derived from the *declared* duration (`BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS`), never from live module state; **for V1 the class follows the PATCH/FULL choice of IA-18 (per seat, from the actor's own declaration and stock): MEDIUM without the part, LONG with it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "readied"; under the interim stock rule, "without the part" / "with it")* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the MVP rule is the stock rule, "readied" being the post-MVP form, IA-18)*; never from hp**. `part_tell` *(added 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: bool, true iff `repair_part_for` of the focused V1 subject's declared kind is not NONE and the actor's own stowed count meets `parts_per_full` (the MVP form, IA-18); the HUD draws the second keycap notch from it; a function of the declared kind and the actor's own stock only, never of hp |
| | `tool_required` | def id; 0 = bare hand (for the tool glyph on a `NEEDS_TOOL` plate) |
| | `prompt_state` | `HIDDEN` (unlit, out of reach, no focus except `self_target` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6, GAP)*, a sane actor's ACTOR target, carrying with a target that is neither `drop` nor a carry-admitted row, IA-28), `ENABLED`, `NEEDS_TOOL` — **three-valued, replacing a boolean** (orchestrator ruling: one boolean cannot carry three different truths) |
| | `verb_name_key`, `glyph_action` | a state-neutral localisation key; the input action whose glyph the HUD draws on the current device |
| | `focus_seq` | monotone; increments on any change of `focus_ref` or `verb_id` (HUD debounce) |
| | `press_blocked_moving` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | bool: the actor's own locomotion has `intent_octant != NONE`, so a `begin_*` press would be answered `BUSY`. The HUD dims the plate one step (keycap and verb to Graphite lit) while it is set, so the most-pressed reject teaches "stop first" by a tell instead of by failure. A function of the actor's own state only (never of the object), so it is no oracle; it is not part of `prompt_state` and never hides the plate. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated G2 / ux-designer and ui-programmer: **(i) the field is true only on a plate whose declared `mode` is `CHANNEL` or `HOLD_CONFIRM`**, a property of the declaration row (a kind-level fact, no oracle), and **never on a TAP plate or on the drop plate**: the former "object-blind" form would have dimmed every door and breaker plate while the player walks, though TAP verbs work while moving (States and Transitions 2). **(ii) The tell is a shape change, not a tint:** the keycap is drawn hollow (outline only, the border one step lighter) and the verb text stays Paper White, because Graphite lit `#5C6670` on Ground `#1C2128` is about 2.8:1, under the 4.5:1 text rule (ART 7.6). **(iii) The tint is reserved for the OUTCOME dim** (V-9), on the keycap and border only; the reject hold (V-10) is a third, shape-only state (the keycap held one art pixel down, inset). **(iv) The moving-plus-reject frame is defined:** hollow keycap and inset together for `BAR_REJECT_HOLD_TICKS`, text unchanged; there is no "double dim". The exact pixels are HUD & Suit Layer's and the art bible's.)* |
| | `carry_state`, `drop_available`, `drop_anchor_o` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | `carry_state` in {NONE, CARRYING}; `drop_available` bool: D.5 finds a legal cell and `subject_headroom() > 0` (geometry and budget, never body state); `drop_anchor_o`: the foot origin in world px for `world_to_hud`. The drop prompt (`G . Drop`) is an **independent second plate**, shown while `carry_state` is CARRYING, so a compatible HARDPOINT (`install_module`), the POD_CONSOLE (`pod_hop`) or a lit door face (V4 `set_opening`) keeps its plate in the primary slot beside it *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; `drop_available` false dims the drop plate one step |
| `channel_view(seat)` | `owner`, `verb_id` | whose block holds the channel; selects the cue set |
| | `state` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated G2 (the creative-director sides with the specialists): **`state` is restricted to `IDLE`, `CONFIRMED`, `PROGRESS`; the terminal states `ABORT`, `DONE` and `OUTCOME` are read only from `last_result` / `prev_result`.** The former rule showed the terminal state on `channel_view.state` for one committed tick and cleared the record in the same hook run, which contradicts itself; clearing at the next hook run instead would break a re-press at T+1, because P2 applies the new begin before that hook run. `CONFIRMED` lasts the begin tick only (`e = 0`) and `PROGRESS` starts at `e = 1`.)* | `IDLE`, `CONFIRMED`, `PROGRESS` as the live state; the record's terminal results are `ABORT`, `DONE`, `OUTCOME` in `last_result` (six simulation states in all: orchestrator ruling — `OUTCOME` is a sixth member so `DONE` always means "work happened" and `OUTCOME` always means "resolved to a no-op"; the advisor's list of five omitted it while its own channel state machine named it); `ACK` is the Adapter's local pre-play between press and result event and is never a simulation state |
| | `transition_seq`, `result_seq` | monotone; increment on **every** state change; cues are edge-detected from them (a render frame may span several ticks) so a terminal transition is never skipped |
| | `progress_pm`, `length_class` | `floor(elapsed × 1000 / D)` clamped 0 to 1000 — **a function of declared duration and the player's own elapsed ticks only, never of hp** (resume offsets included, IA-16); the bar class, frozen at press |
| | `outcome` | a code, never a string; `NONE` until `state ∈ {DONE, OUTCOME}` and **unreadable, not merely unrendered, before then** |
| | `abort_cause` | diagnostic only; **never read by the HUD**; the HUD uses one abort frame set for every `WORLD` cause |
| | `ended_by` | `NONE`, `PLAYER_CANCEL`, `WORLD` — **non-diagnostic**: `PLAYER_CANCEL` iff the record ended `ABORT(CANCELLED)` by the seat's own `cancel_channel`, `WORLD` for every other abort cause; the HUD drains the bar on `PLAYER_CANCEL` and plays the one abort frame set on `WORLD` (HUD & Suit Layer OQ-HS-19, EC-HS-33). It reveals nothing the player does not know: the release was their own (registry: `ended_by`) |
| | DONE handoff | `(seat, owner_system, verb_id, target_ref)` on the tick an owner-registered record (`owner_system` not INTERACTION) reaches DONE; read by Assembly's `assembly.step` (IA-35) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: its own one-tick field, written in the hook run that completes the record and cleared in the next hook run, independent of `channel_view.state` and of `last_result`; Assembly reads this field, never a terminal `state`)* |
| | `last_result`, `prev_result` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | each `{result_seq, state, outcome, ended_by, tick}` with `state` in {DONE, ABORT, OUTCOME}: the seat's **last two terminal results, newest first**. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: these two records are now the **only** terminal source for the HUD and for tests; `channel_view.state` never shows a terminal state.)* A one-tick terminal state, or a one-deep record, would lose a DONE, ABORT or OUTCOME and its cue to a render frame or to a re-press at T+1. A result is replaced only by a newer **terminal**, never by a `begin_*`, and the depth of 2 covers a begin-and-cancel inside one render frame. `outcome` is unreadable (`NONE`) in a record whose `state` is ABORT. The HUD plays the cue of each `result_seq` it has not seen, oldest first, once. Cleared at restore |

Hold-to-confirm is **not in either simulation view**: the simulation never sees a partial hold (IA-8), so the hold bar is Adapter-local presentation state; `focus_view` supplies `mode = HOLD_CONFIRM` and `duration_class = SHORT` only. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the HUD reads the hold bar from the third source of the closed list, **`input_view(seat)`**, owned by the Controller and local to the Adapter: `hold_pm` 0..1000 (the hold fraction `b` of Controller D.10 as permille of `CONFIRM_HOLD_S`, on the render-time clock, frozen under pause); `armed` bool (the two-tap option's armed state, `CONFIRM_TAP_WINDOW`); `device_class` in the Controller's closed device-token set (classified by event type, never `device == 0`); `pressed_action` (the closed action token whose key is currently down, for the keycap drop of the ACK). It is not part of the simulation, is not hashed or replicated, and carries no strings.)*  Only the local seat's `channel_view` is drawn; in Vertical Slice B a crewmate's channel has no HUD bar — their body at the object, their beam and their tool sound are the signal (Pillar 1: no readout of a crewmate's state). The HUD may never read `interaction.state`, `node()` / `edge()` or any `PERCEPTION_OUT` block. *Decides:* R19, PIPE:299, KRN CR-10, ART:745.

**Group H — Invariants (build relations; each is lint-checked at data load and is an acceptance row)**

- **I-1** `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS mod LIT_RECHECK_TICKS == 0` (legal `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS` values 1, 2, 3 and 6). Every block end is then a check tick, so no step is credited on an unlit or out-of-reach block end (IA-7, IA-14 property (f)).
- **I-2** Every declared channel duration (`PATCH_CHANNEL_TICKS`, `FULL_CHANNEL_TICKS`, `CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS`, `CRANK_CHANNEL_TICKS`, `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS`, `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS`, `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS`, `REVIVE_CHANNEL_TICKS`) is a positive multiple of `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` (IA-14: `D mod B == 0`).
- **I-3** `THROW_RANGE_TILES > GLOWSTICK_RADIUS_TILES` (3 > 2): an unclipped throw never lights its thrower; only a first-pixel wall clip (D.4) lands the stick at the thrower's feet.
- **I-4** `THROW_ARC_TICKS < ticks_for_seconds(GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S)`: `life_ticks` at landing stays positive.
- **I-5** `DROP_OFFSET_PX < REACH_PX`: a dropped module stays re-pickable without a step, if lit.
- **I-6** `HARM_TOTAL_BP ≥ SUIT_HP_FULL` (Suit's symbol, cited): a completed harm act delivers at least a full integrity bar (IA-31).
- **I-7** `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET + PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE ≤ 144` (75 % of `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS`): the content budget of IA-37. It is a relation between the two knobs and holds at their defaults (120 + 24 = 144). The lint runs over the registration window `W_int(p)` at every authored body position of each registered loaded set (IA-37, D.10): ERROR when `W_int(p)` exceeds `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET`; the loaded-set sum `S_int` of `legal_loaded_sets` is only the upper bound, and the former S4 WARN (now 145, 25 over *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: Derelict Authoring L_seed B 3, one loose placement fewer)*) is retired *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D37)*.
- **I-8** `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS ≤ 2 × TAU_ATT_TICKS` (registry `noise_unit_relations` IR-3, accepted by Attention: 600 ≤ 3600) and `TAP_REARM_TICKS ≥` the door leaf's travel time (handed to Layout, which Interaction cannot tune alone) (IA-16, IA-21). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **the Layout half is closed under the Graph's published default.** The Graph answers that only M13 writes `area` and `door_state` and proposes a binary `area` at MVP (LS-18 additions; Graph OQ 21), and Layout derives the leaf in the same tick (LY-9, LY-11): the leaf's travel time is therefore 1 tick and `TAP_REARM_TICKS ≥ 1` (floor 1, Tuning Knobs) discharges it. If the Graph's OQ 21 is ever answered with a stepped slide, the travel time becomes the number of sweep ticks and this invariant re-opens, bound by the then-owner of the slide.)*
- **I-9** `PATCH_CHANNEL_TICKS < FULL_CHANNEL_TICKS` and `FULL_CHANNEL_TICKS ≤ BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS × 2`: a part must buy visible time, and the LONG bar class stays a bounded length (UI Requirements).

### States and Transitions

*Interaction holds no per-object state of its own beyond the records below: the objects' own states (hp, positions, stocks) belong to the Graph, Emitter State, the Item DB and Suit. Every transition here is caused by a command applied in P2, by `interaction.step` in P3, or by `post_restore`; presentation never causes one. `ACK` is not a simulation state — it exists only in the Adapter's pre-play between the press and the result event (PCI:84, 86).*

**1. The channel record, per seat (maps to `channel_view.state`).**

| State | Entered when | Exits to | Who may cause it | `channel_view.state` / notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NONE | no record | CONFIRMED on a verb's apply | — | `IDLE`; the Adapter shows `ACK` between press and result |
| CONFIRMED | the verb's `apply` (P2) created the record at `e = 0`, `progress` 0 | PROGRESS on the same tick's hook run (`e ≥ 1` accrues); ABORT at `e = 0` rows 1 to 6; `ABORT(CANCELLED)` if cancelled in P2 of a later tick | the seat's PLAYER command; the Controller mutator `set_channel_ref(..., face_octant)` runs in the same apply *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling G6/G1, CONFLICT)* | `CONFIRMED`; identical for a command that will later resolve to an outcome (IA-12) |
| PROGRESS | `e ≥ 1` accrued | PROGRESS each tick; DONE; ABORT(cause); OUTCOME(sub) | the hook only | `PROGRESS`, `progress_pm = floor(progress × 1000 / D)`; steps at every `e mod B == 0` |
| DONE | `e == D − resume_offset` and the completion resolved `OK` | NONE (same hook run) | the hook | `DONE`; ledger entry deleted; completion cue |
| ABORT(cause) | an abort row of IA-14 (1 to 6), `cancel_channel`, body transfer, restore | NONE | the hook (world and body causes), a P2 `cancel_channel` (the player), `post_restore` | `ABORT`, cause in diagnostics; the bar snaps empty (cancel drains at the cosmetic drain rate); ledger written for non-incremental verbs |
| OUTCOME(sub) | `e == D − resume_offset` and the resolve found a `PRECONDITION` or `BLOCKED` result (D.3) | NONE | the hook | `OUTCOME`, `outcome` code now readable; the bar completes normally first (ack-identical and bar-identical), then the "nothing happened" cue |

Exactly one terminal state per record; terminal → NONE happens at the end of the same hook run, after `last_result` is written *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the terminal states DONE, ABORT and OUTCOME in the table above are **record** states read through `last_result` / `prev_result` only; `channel_view.state` takes the values IDLE, CONFIRMED and PROGRESS alone, so the "`DONE`", "`ABORT`" and "`OUTCOME`" entries of its last column are the `last_result.state` value; Assembly's DONE handoff is its own one-tick field, IA-41)*; there is no PAUSED state (a pause is the absence of ticks). `last_result{result_seq, verb_id, state, outcome, ended_by, tick}` persists in the seat record until it is replaced by the next **terminal** result (not the next `begin_*`), with `prev_result` holding the one before it, so a presentation poll at render rate, or a re-press at T+1, cannot miss a cue *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the former "until the next `begin_*`" lost a DONE, ABORT or OUTCOME whenever the player pressed again on the very next tick; both are published in `channel_view`, IA-41)*.

**2. Seat availability matrix — what a press means by Controller locomotion state.**

| `loco_state` | New `begin_*` / TAP verb | `drop` | `throw_glowstick` | `cancel_channel` | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| IDLE, WALK, RUN | allowed if `act_gate`; a `begin_*` also needs `octant == NONE` at `validate` and answers `BUSY` while the actor is moving (PC-7; IA-10). A move intent that applies **after** `validate` in the same P2 is caught by the hook at `e = 0` as `ABORT(MOVED)` (Edge Cases A) | n/a (nothing carried) | allowed | Applied as a no-op if no channel (`IA_CANCEL_NOOP`) | |
| CARRY_IDLE, CARRY_WALK | `BUSY` (every mode except `drop` and the carry-admitted rows: `install_module` on a compatible HARDPOINT, `pod_hop` on the POD_CONSOLE, and V4 `set_opening` on a door face *(added 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; IA-28) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | allowed (lit-exempt) | `BUSY` | no-op | the beam stays available |
| CHANNELLING | `BUSY`; nothing queues | n/a (a channel cannot start while carrying) | `BUSY` | ends the channel | PC-7 |
| STAGGERED | `BUSY` | — | `BUSY` | Applied as a no-op (`IA_CANCEL_NOOP`; the Controller already cleared the channel) | input ignored for `STAGGER_TICKS` |
| DOWNED | `BUSY` | — | `BUSY` | Applied as a no-op (`IA_CANCEL_NOOP`) | carry dropped, channel cleared; DEAD after `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` unless the cap rises or `begin_revive` lands |
| IN_POD | `BUSY` (no focus exists) | — | `BUSY` | Applied as a no-op (`IA_CANCEL_NOOP`) | locomotion ignored |

**3. The loose-item lifecycle — the instance moves Interaction performs (Item DB instance table rows 3 to 6, 9, 10).**

| From | To | Trigger | Performed by | Effect on subject registration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| INSTALLED | LOOSE_WORLD | `begin_cut` completion (M6, `salvage_yield`, `place_loose`) | Interaction | node subject unregistered (E7); ITEM subject per yield (E6); STUB per open port |
| LOOSE_WORLD | CARRIED | `pickup` of a CARRIED-class def | Interaction, with the Controller's `set_carry_ref` and Suit's `set_carried` | ITEM subject unregistered (E7) |
| LOOSE_WORLD | STOWED | `pickup` of a STOWED-class def | Interaction, with Suit's `can_stow` (reject `STACK_FULL`) and `add_stowed` *(Suit & Equipment, Designed — SE-40)* | ITEM subject unregistered (E7) |
| CARRIED | LOOSE_WORLD | `drop`; or a downed actor's vanished `carry_ref` (next hook run) | Interaction, with Suit's `release_carried` (the loose entry is built from its return) and the Controller's `clear_carry_ref`; for a downed actor the Controller has already cleared `carry_ref`, so only `release_carried` is called | ITEM subject registered at the placement cell (E6) |
| STOWED | SPENT | the first productive FULL step (the part); an accepted `throw_glowstick` (the stick) | Interaction, with Suit's `consume_reserved` (the part) and `spend_stowed` (the stick) *(Suit & Equipment, Designed — SE-40 / SE-41)* | none (a thrown stick reappears as an Emitter State deployable, not an item) |
| LOOSE_WORLD | discarded | `discard_section` | the Layout / Assembly atomic apply, through `sync_section` | all ITEM subjects of the section unregistered; `loose[]` entries dropped |

Positions are section-local `(section_id, pos_px)` and a cell is valid iff Layout's `floor_tile_at(section_id, px)` returns a tile (D.5); the holder of the table is Interaction's (Layout LY-22).

**4. The in-flight throw.**

| State | Entered when | Exits to | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| SPENT | `throw_glowstick` applied at tick T; the stick leaves stock | IN_FLIGHT at once | accepted = spent; no refund on any later outcome |
| IN_FLIGHT | the `throws[]` entry exists, `land_tick = T + THROW_ARC_TICKS` | LANDED at `land_tick`; persists across a save | no light yet, no subject yet |
| LANDED | at `land_tick` the hook called E3 and it returned true | entry removed | Emitter State owns the deployable from here (waning, eviction, `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_LIVE`) |
| VOID | E3 returned false (dormant or unknown section, or outside any section) | entry removed | the item stays spent (EM:699); `IA_THROW_VOID` |

**5. The FULL-job part record (IA-18), per `(seat, subject_ref)`.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, BLOCKING: rewritten around the branch-independent commit rule of IA-18. `RESERVED` has no `until` and exists only while a channel of that seat runs on the subject.)*

| State | Entered when | Stock the actor sees (Suit read) | Exits to |
|---|---|---|---|
| NONE | no job | full stock | RESERVED on an Applied FULL begin with no PAID record |
| RESERVED | a FULL begin is Applied; the part moves to Suit's `reserved` bucket | stock minus `parts_per_full`, **in both truth branches** | PAID at the first productive step (`consume_reserved`); on `OUTCOME` (the bar ran to its end, no productive step): NONE, the part returns (`IA_RESERVE_RELEASE`); on `cancel_channel`, any `ABORT(cause)` or a restore with `blocks = 0`: NONE, the part returns, in both branches; with `blocks ≥ 1` and no productive step: PAID, the part is consumed, in both branches (`IA_RESERVE_COMMIT`) |
| PAID(grace_until) | the first productive step, or a cancel, abort or restore with `blocks ≥ 1` | permanently reduced | NONE at `grace_until` (`t_last_block + ticks_for_seconds(FULL_JOB_GRACE_S)`), at the channel's `DONE` event, or when the subject is tombstoned *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: "completion" was `hp = hp_cap = HP_FULL`, readable as a per-tick sweep that would kill a healthy module's PAID record at once and charge a second part on a re-begin; it is the `DONE` event only, IA-18)* |

`blocks` is the number of blocks the channel has credited, a count that is the same in both truth branches. `PAID` persists across a restore (absolute `grace_until`); a RESERVED record present in a snapshot is resolved by `post_restore` from the persisted channel record's `blocks_done` by the same rule. A re-begin by the same seat while PAID is not charged again and reserves nothing. The record count is bounded by `MAX_GRACE_ENTRIES` per seat (the smallest `grace_until` is evicted; an evicted PAID entry costs the actor a re-charge, accepted, counted `IA_GRACE_EVICT`).

**6. Other records.**

| Record | Key | Created | Expires / deleted | Resume semantics |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Partial-progress entry | `(seat, subject_ref, verb_id)` | ABORT of a non-incremental verb | `abort_tick + PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`; on DONE or outcome; oldest evicted past `MAX_PARTIAL_ENTRIES` | a re-begin within the TTL resumes at `blocks_done` (D.6) |
| Door attempt (`attempts[]`, both origins) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: keyed by door, with a timeout and supersession, IA-24)* | `(door_ref)` with `origin` and `seat` fields (one open attempt per **door**, shared by both faces); `seat` is 0 for a SYSTEM-origin (cascade) attempt | `set_opening` Applied (PLAYER: a window; SYSTEM: no window) | PLAYER: at `begin + DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` the hook reads the motor (D.12): `PRECONDITION(NO_POWER)` at once, or a SYSTEM-origin `set_opening` is submitted and applied in P2 of the next tick: M13, or state `CLOSING_BLOCKED`, re-submitted once, on the first clear tick of the hook's pure `leaf_would_overlap` read, as a SYSTEM-origin `set_opening` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was "every tick")*, applied in P2 of the next tick (M13), or, for a PLAYER attempt, until `CLOSING_BLOCKED_TIMEOUT_TICKS` have passed in `CLOSING_BLOCKED` (the attempt ends, the leaf stays open, no token); a SYSTEM close applied on the door drops every open PLAYER attempt on it; a completion that finds the leaf already at its target skips M13; SYSTEM (cascade): the same P2 path with no window and no timeout | not interruptible by the player; not a channel; not persisted — dropped on restore (a SYSTEM close lost at restore is re-issued by its cascade) |
| Re-arm stamp | subject (a door: its `door_ref`, both faces share one stamp) | a TAP actuation applied | after `TAP_REARM_TICKS` | a TAP inside the window answers `BUSY` |
| Stub | `stub_ref` | salvage completion, one per open port | `begin_cap` completion, or `discard_section` | — |

**7. Subject registration lifecycle (who registers what).**

| Event | Registers (E6) | Unregisters (E7) | Caller |
|---|---|---|---|
| Section instantiate | **candidate-table entries** for every declared interactable (two face candidates per door), anchors read from Layout's `subject_anchors(section_id)`; for the section's `loose[]` and `stubs[]` likewise; a candidate becomes an OBJECT, ITEM or STUB subject (E6) only while inside the registration window `W` (IA-37, step 0b of the hook) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, D37: was "OBJECT subjects for every declared interactable"; the rows below read the same way: `sync_section` maintains candidates, the window recompute registers and unregisters subjects)* | — | `sync_section` from the instantiate atomic apply *(Layout, Designed — LY-23)* |
| Module install | the new module's OBJECT subject | — | `sync_section` from Assembly's install apply *(Modular Ship Assembly, Designed — MSA-26)* |
| Weld or seam cut | changed subjects only; one STUB per uncapped `open_end` a RAGGED cut leaves | changed subjects only | `sync_section` from Assembly's weld and seam-cut applies (C10; ASK I1 (vii)) |
| Salvage completion | ITEM per yield; STUB per open port | the node's OBJECT subject | the Interaction hook |
| Pickup | — | the item's ITEM subject | the Interaction apply |
| Drop, or a downed actor's carry released | ITEM at the placement cell (the loose entry is built from Suit's `release_carried`) | — | the Interaction apply / hook |
| Cap completion | — | the STUB subject | the Interaction hook |
| Section discard or dormancy | — (a wake in `pod_settle` re-registers, WM-14) | every subject of the section | `sync_section` from the discard apply or from Web Map's dormancy apply (WM-10, WM-24) |
| Restore | all of the above, idempotently | — | Interaction `post_restore` |
| A declared object moves | — | — | not Interaction's: `set_subject_anchor` (E8) is called only if a declared object moves (none at MVP) |

**8. The block `interaction.state` (schema version 1; all integers, enums and arrays; no derived caches in the snapshot).**

| Field | Type | Persisted | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `schema_version` | int | yes | 1 |
| `seats[PlayerId]` | record | yes | `channel{verb_id, owner_system (INTERACTION or ASSEMBLY, IA-35), target_ref, begin_tick, duration, resume_offset, blocks_done, steps_done, hp_at_begin, cap_at_begin, t_last_block}` (persisted in the snapshot, read by `post_restore` and then dropped; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: `cap_at_begin` and `t_last_block` added, IA-14)*); `last_result{result_seq, verb_id, state, outcome, ended_by, tick}` and `prev_result` of the same shape (cleared at restore; replaced only by a newer terminal result, IA-41) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `partial[]` | array of `{seat, subject_ref, verb_id, blocks_done, abort_tick, n}` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: `n` added, D.6)* | yes | at most `MAX_PARTIAL_ENTRIES` per seat |
| `full_jobs[]` | array of `{seat, subject_ref, state, grace_until}` with `state` in {RESERVED, PAID} | yes | at most `MAX_GRACE_ENTRIES` per seat; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: `until` is retired with the release timer; a RESERVED entry in a snapshot is resolved by `post_restore`, IA-18)* |
| `attempts[]` | array of `{door_ref, seat, origin, attempt_id, begin_tick, dir, state}` with `state` in {WINDOW, CLOSING_BLOCKED}, **one open entry per `door_ref`** | no | Interaction's for both origins; seat 0 for SYSTEM origin; dropped at restore *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: keyed by door, not by face)* |
| `rearm[]` | array of `{subject_ref, until}`, where for a door `subject_ref` is its `door_ref` | no | cosmetic timing guard; dropped at restore |
| `throws[]` | array of `{owner, land_tick, land_px, life_ticks}` | yes | at most `GLOWSTICK_STACK_MAX` × seats by construction (stock-limited) |
| `loose[]` | array of `{item_id, section_id, pos_px, inst}` (`inst` = the six-element `to_array()` ItemInstance, IA-28) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: Item DB IDB-15/16/18 and registry item_instance_to_array, G1/G6)* | yes | `pos_px` section-local (LY-22); subjects re-registered at restore; no `carried_item_id` (Suit holds the carried instance) |
| `stubs[]` | array of `{stub_ref, node_id, port_index}` | yes | |
| `last_salvage_T` | int64 tick or NONE | yes | the tick of the last `begin_cut` DONE (IA-25); read through the pure `last_salvage_T()`; kept at restore; never a JSON number (two 32-bit parts, as MSA's `last_shape_change_T`) |
| `next_item_id`, `next_stub_id`, `result_seq` | ints | yes | counters live inside the block (CR-8) |

Dirty keys are marked explicitly in the mutators (a packed-array element write runs no setter). -> ADR candidate: state-block representation and the autoload-versus-injected holder for `interaction.state`.

**9. The hook contract.**

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| `hook_id` | `interaction.step` — **proposed, registered at boot; an unknown hook id fails boot (kernel CR-3)** |
| `owner`; `divisor` / `offset` | Interaction; 1 / 0 |
| `after` | `[controller.step, emitter.place]` — verbatim ids from PCI:145 and EM:913; `emitter.place` is itself `after: [body.integrate, graph.propagate.failure]`, so the chain is transitive and the Graph's three hooks (`graph.propagate.power` → `.atmo` → `.failure`) run first on a shared tick. Layout's `layout.doors` precedes `body.integrate` only after the Controller amendment LY-10 (iii) lands (PCI:143 today: `after: controller.step`); until then the order is a registration tie that CR-3 forbids relying on — hence the SYSTEM-command form of IA-24 |
| Submits | SYSTEM-origin `set_opening` for attempt completion and `CLOSING_BLOCKED` retries (CR-3: hooks may not submit PLAYER-origin commands; they may submit SYSTEM-origin ones, KRN:71) |
| Order inside one run | (0) housekeeping: convert the vanished `carry_ref` of downed seats to loose items, sweep expired ledger entries and PAID (grace) FULL-job entries — a RESERVED entry is never swept, it resolves only with its channel (IA-18, amended 2026-10-27); *(0b, added 2026-10-27 — re-review, D37: **window recompute**, once per tick and only when a bound body's `room_id` changed or a section instantiate, discard, dormancy or restore occurred this tick, in room-id order: recompute `W` (IA-37), then E6 for each candidate entering `W` and E7 for each candidate leaving the one-hop hysteresis ring, so the registrations are final before any channel row reads `lit`; its work is bounded by the candidates of the changed rooms, D.10 and AC-IA-71)* (1) per seat in ascending PlayerId, IA-14 rows 1 to 9; (2) door attempts due (the `served_pm` read and the SYSTEM-origin `set_opening` submit) and `CLOSING_BLOCKED` re-submissions; (3) throws with `land_tick == T` |
| Reads | `controller.state` (`octant`, locomotion state, `carry_ref`, foot origin); `lit` / `lux`; the Graph's `node()` / `edge()` — **the only place module-body fields are read** (and the position read of a TAP apply); Layout's `leaf_would_overlap`, `clip_to_footprints`, `floor_tile_at`, `subject_anchors`; Body's `foot_boxes(body_id)`, unfiltered (BDY-18); Sanity's gate query and the owner-supplied `eligible` predicates; Suit's stock reads |
| Writes | its own block; the Graph's M6, M7, M11, M12 (Class A, check-then-apply, from the hook); M8, M9, M13 only from P2 applies (PLAYER taps and the SYSTEM door command); M10 has no MVP verb; M16 is called from `top_up`'s P2 apply (V7 admitted at MVP, X5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; Emitter State E3, E6, E7; the Controller's `clear_channel_ref` / `clear_carry_ref`; Suit's Class B mutators; Sanity's `record_falsification` and `record_harm_event` (registry); **E6 / E7 from the window recompute (step 0b) as well as from the salvage, pickup, drop and cap paths** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, D37)* |
| Budget | at most one channel per seat; door attempts and throws in flight are few; `lit(ref)` on a registered subject is a table lookup, not a probe (EM-25), so channel re-checks cost nothing against `MAX_PROBES_PER_TICK`; far below `STEP_BUDGET_MS` by inspection (to be measured) |

### Interactions with Other Systems

*Boundary items only (a dependency table written before the consumer exists must list only what crosses the boundary, not guess a neighbour's internals). For each designed neighbour the neighbour's own Interactions row is quoted verbatim so bidirectional consistency can be checked; where this GDD differs from the quote, the difference is stated and routed. A row for an undesigned neighbour lists what Interaction needs or supplies by proposed name and is tagged provisional; the owner of an interface is the system that publishes the name.*

**Upstream — Interaction depends on these.**

| System | Data in (to Interaction) | Data out (from Interaction) | Interface owner and names fixed by the neighbour | The neighbour's own row, quoted | Consistency |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Command & Simulation Layer** (designed) | the submit → admission → authority → `validate` → `apply` pipeline; tick `T`; `ticks_for_seconds`; admission reasons; the `WriteScope` token; hook and block registries | the command types of IA-3 (primitive payloads, `coalesce NONE`, `OWNED_BY_ISSUER` with `actor_source body.state` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*); result events carrying reasons, never strings; block `interaction.state` and hook `interaction.step`; no RNG | Kernel: CR-3 hook tuple, CR-4 command anatomy, CR-5 result event, CR-7 authority, CR-8 block registry, CR-9 `WriteScope`, CR-10 subscriber classes, CR-12 `PERCEPTION_OUT` isolation, CR-16 pause, `MAX_AGE_TICKS_ACTION` | KRN:204 (row 12): "Verb commands (`begin_cut`, `begin_weld`, `interact`, field checks, friendly-harm act); as `INPUT_SURFACE` for prompts" → "Result events with honest reasons (CR-5)" → "Interaction: command types and domain reason codes". KRN:691: "Interaction System \| Hard \| Submit, domain reason codes, friendly-harm gate order \| Verb command types" | **Differs in two words:** `interact` is an input action, not a command type; `begin_weld` is registered by Assembly and only declared here. | **In-World Instruments & Inspect View** | the `inspect` permission rule (a lit instrument only); the prompt key `verb_inspect` | the card, rendering `perceived` only | Instruments | Designed (in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view.md IW-21; the card ships live at MVP) |
| **Onboarding (Cryo Bay)** | the first-verbs set: V1 patch, V5 valve, V3 breaker | — | Interaction | *(provisional — Onboarding undesigned)* |
| **HUD & Suit Layer** | `focus_view`, `channel_view` (including the non-diagnostic `ended_by`, `last_result` and `prev_result`) and, from the Controller, `input_view(seat)` and the result-event record: the HUD's closed read list (IA-41, amended 2026-10-27 — design review) | draws the prompt plate and the linear bar at `world_to_hud(anchor_o)`; **must never draw a prompt where `lit(ref)` is false**; never reads `abort_cause` or a rejection reason | HUD | Designed (hud-and-suit-layer.md; it owns the HUD-side knobs); open: the HUD must adopt `input_view` for its hold-bar ASK and the second drop plate, Open Question 45 |
| **Perception Filter and Horror Entities** | negative requirements (IA-13); HORROR subjects take no verb at MVP | the Filter never registers or moves subjects and never submits commands; Horror registers HORROR subjects (E6) | Filter / Horror | Designed (perception-filter.md, horror-entities.md; status confirmed on disk 2026-10-27, contract with Interaction not re-verified in this pass — design review) |
| **2D Lighting & Occlusion** *(2D Lighting, Designed — LO rows 422 and 933)* | the glowstick landing tick (a pool appears from the landing tick only) | — | Lighting reads Emitter State, never `interaction.state`; nothing else crosses | **Soft** edge; Lighting lists the edge Soft from its own side too (LO row 933) |
| **Adaptive Audio** | the channel cue set keyed on `channel_view` transitions (press pre-play, confirmed, progress, cancel, abort, completion, immediate-rejection, "nothing happened"); the power-neutral door latch cue and the motor cue only when the leaf travels (IA-24); the Attention tell of a TOOL push (IA-33) | — | Audio | Designed (adaptive-audio-and-tells.md; keys `ia_*` listed there, AA-32; status confirmed 2026-10-27, new door cue keys owed — design review) |
| **Debug Overlay** | the `IA_*` counters (Interaction freezes its own diagnostic names under the registry `diag_panel_contract` naming convention; the Overlay restates none); release builds carry no `IA_PHANTOM_PROBE` | — | Debug Overlay | *(Designed — debug-overlay-true-values.md; registry `diag_panel_contract`)* |

## Formulas

*Conventions.* Everything hashed or replicated is integer-only (kernel determinism rule, D.10 and D.12 of the kernel); `ceil(a / b)` is `(a + b - 1) div b` for positive `b`; the only fixed-point scale is the Controller's 1/256 direction scale. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: **`floor` in this section means `floori`, and wherever a numerator can be negative (D.1, D.1b, D.8) the operand is clamped to be non-negative first;** GDScript `/` on integers truncates toward zero, which differs from a floor for a negative numerator, and the project lints `INTEGER_DIVISION`.)* `B` is `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` (6); `Tb` is the tick whose P2 applied a begin command; `e = T - Tb`. Upstream formulas are cited by registry name (`in_reach`, `lit(ref)` hysteresis, `fairness_gate`, `repair_part_for`, `salvage_yield`), never restated. **Id index (the ids are cited by Core Rules and must not shift; "Controller D.9 / D.10" and "Pipeline" references always carry their owner's prefix):** D.1 `repair_step_delta` · D.2 `act_gate` · D.3 `channel_outcome` · D.4 `throw_landing` · D.5 `drop_placement` · D.6 `partial_resume` · D.7 `full_job_charge` · D.8 `harm_step` · D.9 `noise_push` · D.10 `subject_budget` · D.11 `channel_clock` · D.12 `door_attempt` · D.13 `length_class`. (Orchestrator ruling: D.7, D.9, D.10, D.12 and D.13 fill ids that Core Rules did not cite, so no citation moved.)

### D.1 repair_step_delta

The repair_step_delta formula is defined as:

`delta = ceil((target_cap - hp_at_begin) / n)`, applied once per credited block through `check_repair_step` then M7; **skipped (zero mutator calls)** when `hp_now >= target_cap` or `delta <= 0`.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hp_at_begin` | int bp | 0..`HP_FULL` | the Graph's true `hp`, read **in the hook** at the first step of this run (e = `B`), never in `validate` (IA-11a) | the subject's hp when this run's first step is credited |
| `target_cap` | int bp | `PATCH_CAP_BP` (PATCH), `HP_FULL` (FULL), `SUIT_PATCH_CAP_BP` (self-patch, Suit's symbol) | registry / Suit | the ceiling this job raises hp towards |
| `n` | int | 30 (PATCH, self-patch), 60 (FULL) | `D / B` with `D` = `PATCH_CHANNEL_TICKS` or `FULL_CHANNEL_TICKS` | block count over the **full** declared duration (checkpoint CQ1: hp is the ledger, so a re-begin recomputes over the full `n`) |
| `hp_now` | int bp | 0..`HP_FULL` | the Graph, read in the hook | current hp, for the skip test |
| `kind` | enum | PATCH, FULL | IA-18, decided from the actor's own stock | passed to M7; a job never mixes kinds |

**Output Range:** `delta` 1..200 for PATCH (hp 0), 1..167 for FULL (hp 0) at the default durations; never above 2 % of `HP_FULL` per block at the defaults. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: across the knob ranges `n` is 20..50 for PATCH (`PATCH_CHANNEL_TICKS` 120 to 300) and 40..100 for FULL (240 to 600), so `delta` reaches 300 for PATCH (3 %) and 250 for FULL (2.5 %); the "2 %" claim holds only at the defaults.)* M7 clamps any overshoot at `hp_cap`. An uninterrupted job gains exactly `target_cap - hp_at_begin`.

**Example:** (1) PATCH on a leaking pipe at hp 3000: `delta = ceil(3000 / 30) = 100`; thirty steps at e = 6, 12, ... 180; hp reaches `LEAK_HP` 4000 at step 10 (e = 60) and 6000 at e = 180; the Graph reads the crossing at its next run (lag 1..6 ticks, IA-14). (2) Hallucinated-fixed pipe, true hp 2000: `delta = ceil(4000 / 30) = 134`; hp at step k is 2000 + 134k, first at or above 4000 at k = 15 (4010, e = 90). (3) FULL at hp 3000: `delta = ceil(7000 / 60) = 117`, sixty steps, clamp at step 60. (4) Hallucinated-broken healthy pipe at hp 10000, PATCH: `hp_at_begin >= LEAK_HP` and `cap_at_begin` 10000 `> PATCH_CAP_BP` so zero calls, the bar runs 180 ticks and the job resolves `PRECONDITION(NOTHING_TO_REPAIR)` (D.3); `hp_cap` is untouched. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: guard moved from `PATCH_CAP_BP` to the real harm, IA-17.)* The same holds at hp 5999, 5000 and 4000 with `hp_cap` 10000: zero calls, `hp_cap` untouched, no weep, the Graph state, descriptors and noise identical to the hp 10000 run. (5) A move on e = 180 leaves 29 steps: hp 5900; the re-begin recomputes `delta = ceil(100 / 30) = 4`, hp reaches 6000 at the 25th step and the rest are skipped by the `hp_now` test; the player pays a fresh 180-tick bar for 100 bp (accepted, CQ1). *(Residual closed 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING, delegated G2: the former residual (a one-bp patch gain paid for by writing `hp_cap` down to the patch cap, accepted because no prompt could warn) and its design-review widening to the silent band hp 4000 to 5999 (a PATCH gaining 1 to 1999 bp and permanently lowering `hp_cap` by 4000 bp) no longer occur: the guard of IA-17 skips M7 PATCH whenever `hp_at_begin >= LEAK_HP` and `cap_at_begin > PATCH_CAP_BP`. Silent damage in that band is FULL work. The Graph's M7 wording fix stays routed, Open Question 10.)* The guard is evaluated only when `target_cap > hp_at_begin`, so the `ceil` numerator is never negative. (6) A resumed PATCH whose earlier steps already reached the cap: the remaining blocks hit the `hp_now >= target_cap` skip, and that skip is **never a failure** (D.3).

### D.1b bead_segments *(added 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D39, default applied confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20))*

The bead_segments formula is defined as:

`bead_segments(h) = clamp(1 + floor(2 * (h - LEAK_HP) / (PATCH_CAP_BP - LEAK_HP)), 1, 3)`, where `h` is the patched subject's true `hp`, read live by the Module-Body Presenter (In-World Instruments IW-18) through the Graph's LS-19 `node_hp(id)` (the `hp` field of `edge(id)` for an edge). For the whole `PATCH_DECAY_S` 240 s hold `h` equals the banked hp at the end of the patch, completed or cancelled; after the hold it falls with the Graph's wear (100 bp per 10 s) and the bead shortens. Interaction never reads it, never in `validate` (IA-11a), and calls no mutator. The bead is drawn on the patch plate (module-body, IW-18), not by Failure & Trail: FT-32's `27 PATCH_WEEP` is one static POINT_FIXED mark per medium with no length axis and is identical for all three bead lengths. Checks at registry defaults (`LEAK_HP` 4000, `PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000, span 2000): `h` 4000 → 1 + floor(0) = 1; 4999 → 2 × 999 / 2000 = 0.999 → 1 + 0 = 1; 5000 → 1 + floor(1) = 2; 5999 → 2 × 1999 / 2000 = 1.999 → 1 + 1 = 2; 6000 → 1 + floor(2) = 3; after the first wear tick, 5900 → 2 × 1900 / 2000 = 1.9 → 1 + 1 = 2. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `h` | int bp | 4000..6000 for a patched subject (a cancel before the first block leaves `h` unchanged) | the Graph's true `hp` | banked hp when the patch ended |
| `LEAK_HP` | int bp | 4000 | registry (cited) | hp at which the LEAK clears |
| `PATCH_CAP_BP` | int bp | 6000 | registry (cited) | the PATCH ceiling |
| `PATCH_DECAY_S` | int s | 240 | registry (cited) | the Graph's hold before wear (LS-23) |
| wear rate | bp per s | 10 (100 bp per 10 s) | Graph LS-23 (cited) | decay after the hold |

**Output Range:** 1..3. Segments 1, 2, 3 for hp 4000..4999, 5000..5999, 6000.

**Derivation (the constraint it satisfies).** Constraint: the extra bar time of "make it last" must buy a difference the player can see, with no number and no extra mechanism. Re-leak time after completion is `PATCH_DECAY_S + (h - LEAK_HP) / 10` s (the wear is 10 bp per second, so each bp above `LEAK_HP` is worth 0.1 s), so a patch banked at 4000 re-leaks in 240 s, at 5000 in 340 s, at 6000 in 440 s by the continuous form (these are the Player Fantasy figures, about 250 s and 440 s). The wear is discrete, 100 bp per 10 s, and the LEAK returns when `hp < LEAK_HP` (LS-11), so a patch banked at exactly 4000 re-leaks at the first wear tick after the hold, about 250 s; the stated figures are therefore "about" 250, 340 and 440 s *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Cutting the band `PATCH_CAP_BP - LEAK_HP` = 2000 bp at 1000 bp gives steps of 100 s of life each; the third segment is reserved for a bar run to its cap (`h` = 6000) so that finishing the job is distinguishable from stopping at five-sixths. Hence `1 + floor(2 * (h - 4000) / 2000)`.

**Example:** a PATCH from hp 3000 (D.1 example 1; `delta` 100): cancelled at e = 60 (`h` = 4000) gives 1 segment and a re-leak at about 250 s (240 s hold + one 10 s wear period) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; cancelled at e = 120 (`h` = 5000) gives 2 and about 340 s; run to e = 180 (`h` = 6000) gives 3 and about 440 s. The bar for the full job costs 180 ticks and the hiss-cancel 60: three seconds against one second buys about 200 s of life, and the bead shows it.

### D.2 act_gate

The act_gate formula is defined as:

`act_gate(o) = exempt(row) OR in_reach(o)`, where `in_reach(o)` already conjoins `lit(o)` (Controller D.9), evaluated in `validate` against the exposure table committed at T-1 (EM-25), and re-evaluated by the channel hook per IA-14 rows 4 and 5 against tick T's table.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `in_reach(o)` | bool | {0, 1} | Player Controller D.9 (`REACH_PX`, `REACH_HALF_ANGLE_DEG`, `COS2_REACH`; cited, not restated) | integer sector-and-distance predicate about the committed facing; **conjoins `lit(o)`** per Controller D.9 (the `lit` conjunct is the row below) |
| `lit(o)` | bool | {0, 1} | Emitter State hysteresis over `act_lux_pm` (`LIT_ON_PM` 150 on, `LIT_OFF_PM` 120 off, no dwell); an unregistered ref answers 0 (EM-21) | the canonical lit bit |
| `exempt(row)` | bool | {0, 1} | the declaration row | true only for `drop`, `toggle_beam`, the inspect card of an already-lit instrument, and actor verbs (`throw_glowstick`, and every declaration row whose `object_class` is `ACTOR_SELF`, the self-patch) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated G6, GAP: the owner adds the surface the Controller's ruled behaviour needs)* |

**Output Range:** boolean. A false result is the immediate `OUT_OF_RANGE` (geometry) or `NOT_LIT` (light); both are IMMEDIATE (IA-10). A false result maps to `OUT_OF_RANGE` when the distance or arc conjunct fails, else to `NOT_LIT`; the two are tested in that order (IA-10).

**Example:** a valve anchor 70 px away inside the arc, chest-lamp exposure `act_lux_pm` 140 with `lit` currently 0: 140 < `LIT_ON_PM` so `lit` stays 0 and the press answers `NOT_LIT`; the player steps closer, exposure 155 turns `lit` on (prompt appears, press accepted); a flicker to 130 keeps `lit` on (above `LIT_OFF_PM`); 110 turns it off. A phantom target is an unregistered ref, so `lit` = 0 and nothing is focused.

**Example (the self-patch):** the actor alone with a damaged suit, beam OFF, no other light, presses `begin_patch` on `ACTOR_SELF`: `exempt(row)` = 1, so `act_gate` = 1 OR `in_reach` = 1 whatever `lit` reads (it is irrelevant); the press is accepted, and no `OUT_OF_RANGE` or `NOT_LIT` can arise. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated G6, GAP: the owner adds the surface the Controller's ruled behaviour needs)*

### D.3 channel_outcome

The channel_outcome formula is defined as:

`outcome = DONE if (changed >= 1 AND no failure was recorded after the last change); else PRECONDITION(NOTHING_TO_REPAIR) if (changed == 0 AND last_failure is empty); else last_failure` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING: the middle branch is new. A job that skipped every block, whether by the PATCH guard of IA-17, by the `hp_now >= target_cap` skip on a healthy subject, or by a Graph `ABOVE_PATCH_CAP` answer, has `changed = 0` and no failure, and "else `last_failure`" was undefined for it; `NOTHING_TO_REPAIR` was prose only)*, resolved once at `e == D - resume_offset` (D.11) and published as owner state change (KRN:96). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **the at-cap skip is never a failure once `changed >= 1`.** A block skipped because `hp_now >= target_cap` (or because `delta <= 0` after the cap was reached) is a no-op, not a failed check, and is not recorded in `last_failure`; before this rule a resumed repair whose last few blocks were skipped at the cap could resolve `NOTHING_TO_REPAIR` after real changes (a successful resumed job read as a failed one). Only a failing `check_*` or a Graph physical token is a failure. With `changed = 0` and every block skipped at the cap (a healthy subject) the outcome is `NOTHING_TO_REPAIR`, as before. A restore creates no outcome and writes no ledger entry (IA-16).)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `changed` | int | 0..`n` | the hook | number of mutator calls that changed Graph or Suit state, or 1 if the verb's single completion mutator succeeded |
| `last_failure` | enum | `PRECONDITION(NOT_REPAIRABLE / NOTHING_TO_REPAIR / NO_POWER / ALREADY_RUNNING / NOT_SALVAGEABLE)`, `BLOCKED(token)` | IA-10 | the last failed `check_*`, guard, or Graph physical token (`STILL_HOT`, `NO_FUEL`, `AREA_RANGE`, `PORT_ABSENT`, `PORT_INCOMPAT`, ...) recorded in this run |
| `D`, `resume_offset` | int ticks | 60..600 (the union of the duration knob ranges: `CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS` 60 up to `FULL_CHANNEL_TICKS` 600); 0..`D - B` | D.11, D.6 | declared duration and resume point *(range corrected 2026-10-27 — design review: was 90..480)* |

**Output Range:** exactly one of `DONE`, `PRECONDITION(sub)`, `BLOCKED(token)`. **Duration, noise, bar length and the acknowledgement are independent of the outcome** (R6c; acceptance rows): the formula runs only at the end.

**Example:** crank on a SCRAMmed reactor still at `T_mach >= OVERHEAT_OFF_MC`: M11 at completion returns `BLOCKED(STILL_HOT)`, `changed` = 0, outcome = `BLOCKED(STILL_HOT)` after the full 240 ticks. PATCH on a DESTROYED pipe: every `check_repair_step` fails, `changed` = 0, outcome = `PRECONDITION(NOT_REPAIRABLE)`. PATCH on a worn pipe that reaches the cap at step 25 of 30: `changed` = 25 and nothing failed afterwards, outcome = `DONE`.

### D.4 throw_landing

The throw_landing formula is defined as:

`to = foot + round_q8(DIRQ[aim_angle] * R)`; `hit = clip_to_footprints(body, foot, to)`; `land = to` if `hit == to` (no SOLID cell on the segment), else the last pixel `s - 1` on the segment before `hit` (`foot` when the first pixel is SOLID); `land_tick = T + THROW_ARC_TICKS`; `life_ticks = ticks_for_seconds(GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S) - THROW_ARC_TICKS`, with `R = THROW_RANGE_TILES * TILE_PX` and `round_q8(v) = sign(v) * ((|v| + 128) >> 8)`.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `aim_angle` | int | 0..4095 (`AIM_UNITS`) | the throw command payload, the Controller's committed aim; zero is east and `512 * octant` steps counter-clockwise (Controller D.3 convention) | direction of the throw |
| `DIRQ[a]` | int vec2 | each component -256..256 | constant table generated offline, 1/256 scale, no runtime trigonometry | unit direction |
| `R` | int px | 144..240 (default 3 * 48 = 144) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was 48..240; I-3 requires `THROW_RANGE_TILES > GLOWSTICK_RADIUS_TILES` (2), so the legal range is 3..5 tiles)* | `THROW_RANGE_TILES` (3..5) * `TILE_PX` 48 | unclipped throw distance |
| `foot` | int vec2 px | section-local | `controller.state` foot origin | thrower's foot origin |
| `clip_to_footprints` | point | the first SOLID cell on the segment, or `to` | Layout's read (LY line 305, Designed): the first cell on the segment that lies inside a SOLID occupied span, or `to` if none; *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: **the walk is a supercover walk**, every cell the segment touches including both cells at a corner crossing, not a one-cell-per-step Bresenham line, so a diagonal throw past a wall corner cannot pass between two diagonally adjacent SOLID cells; the same walk is used by `free(p)` in D.5)* the s - 1 step back to the last clear pixel is Interaction's own derivation (Layout OQ-14 d). Door leaves are SOLID rects (LY-9), so a stick never passes a closed door | wall clipping |
| `life_ticks` | int | > 0 (invariant I-4) | `GLOWSTICK_LIFE_S` 150 -> 9000 ticks, minus `THROW_ARC_TICKS` 30 | life remaining at landing |

**Output Range:** `|land - foot|` in 0..144 px at the default (144.2 on a diagonal from rounding; up to `R` = 240 px at `THROW_RANGE_TILES` 5 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*); `land_tick` in T+15..T+60; `life_ticks` 8970 at defaults. If the first pixel is blocked, `land = foot`.

**Example:** thrower at section-local (1000, 800) facing north, `aim_angle` 1024, `DIRQ = (0, -256)`; unclipped land (1000, 656); a wall footprint occupies y <= 680, so `hit = (1000, 680)` (s = 120) and `land`, the pixel before it (s = 119), is `(1000, 681)`. Diagonal: `aim_angle` 512, `DIRQ = (181, -181)`, offset `(181 * 144 + 128) >> 8 = 102` on each axis, land (1102, 698). Apply tick T = 5000 -> `land_tick` 5030, `life_ticks` 8970; the hook calls E3 at tick 5030 (after `emitter.place`), so the first tick the stick lights the table is 5031 (one tick, below any perception threshold) and the stick's death tick is 14000 (life counts from the throw tick, EM:198). Two throws on one tick apply in `seq` order, both spent.

### D.5 drop_placement

The drop_placement formula is defined as:

`p0 = foot + round_q8(UNIT[facing] * DROP_OFFSET_PX)`; the result is `p0` if `free(p0)`, else the first `foot + STEP8[posmod(f - i, 8)] * TILE_PX` for `i = 0..7` (clockwise on screen, starting at the octant `f` nearest the facing) with `free(.)`, else `OBSTRUCTED`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: **STEP8 is now an explicit literal table**, indexed by the Controller's octant number, which counts counter-clockwise on screen from east (`aim_angle` 0 = east, 512 per octant, north = 1024; screen y points down): `STEP8[0] = (1, 0)` E, `[1] = (1, -1)` NE, `[2] = (0, -1)` N, `[3] = (-1, -1)` NW, `[4] = (-1, 0)` W, `[5] = (-1, 1)` SW, `[6] = (0, 1)` S, `[7] = (1, 1)` SE. The former `(f + i) mod 8` advanced counter-clockwise while the text said clockwise, so two implementers would have placed a blocked drop on opposite sides, a determinism break; `posmod(f - i, 8)` runs E, SE, S, SW, W, NW, N, NE for `f` = 0, which is what the example below shows. `posmod` is the non-negative modulus.)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `UNIT[facing]` | int vec2 | components -256..256 | Controller D.3 table | facing at 1/256 scale |
| `DROP_OFFSET_PX` | int px | 12..48 (< `REACH_PX`, I-5) | Interaction [PLACEHOLDER] | forward offset, default 24 |
| `STEP8[8]` | int vec2 | each component -1, 0, 1 | fixed literal table, see the formula (E, NE, N, NW, W, SW, S, SE in octant order) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | the eight neighbouring cells one tile out; diagonals use 48 px per axis |
| `free(p)` | bool | {0, 1} | `floor_tile_at(section_id, p) != NONE` (Layout, Designed — LY-22), no SOLID footprint or closed leaf at `p`, at least `INTERACT_ANCHOR_MIN_SPACING_PX` from every registered anchor, and `subject_headroom() > 0` (a player drop draws on `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE`, never refused for budget while the reserve lasts), **and no SOLID cell on the supercover segment from `foot` to `p`** *(added 2026-10-27 — re-review: without it a diagonal candidate at a wall corner could land across a SOLID seam, a placement the player cannot walk to)*. Note: Layout's LY-11 ask is adopted — door cells answer `NONE` from `floor_tile_at`, so D.5 never places on a door cell and the neighbour-cell search is the "snap to the nearest legal cell"; an authored item on a door cell is Layout's lint `LOOSE_ON_DOOR`, not an Interaction state | cell validity |

**Output Range:** a section-local point within 48 * sqrt(2) = 68 px of `foot`, or `OBSTRUCTED` (immediate). A forced drop (downed actor, the NONE-cap conversion) uses the same search with the final fallback `foot` regardless of overlap: a carried module is never deleted and never refused.

**Example:** facing east, `UNIT = (256, 0)`, foot (500, 300): `p0 = (500 + ((256 * 24 + 128) >> 8), 300) = (524, 300)`. A crate blocks `p0`: the first neighbour-cell candidate is (548, 300); if that is blocked too, i = 1 gives (548, 348) (south-east), and so on to i = 7; if all eight fail, the player `drop` is `OBSTRUCTED` and the module stays carried.

### D.6 partial_resume

The partial_resume formula is defined as:

`valid = (T - abort_tick <= PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS) AND (stored_n == D / B); resume_offset = min(blocks_done, n - 1) * B if valid else 0` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the entry stores the `n` it was written under and a re-begin whose `D / B` differs (a retuned `*_CHANNEL_TICKS`, a different tool class) discards the entry rather than resuming past the end; the clamp to `n - 1` keeps `remaining` at least `B`, so `resume_offset` stays in `0..D - B`)*; `remaining = D - resume_offset; progress_pm(e) = floor((resume_offset + e) * 1000 / D)`.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `abort_tick`, `blocks_done`, `stored_n` | int | tick; 0..`n - 1`; 1..100 | the ledger entry `(seat, subject_ref, verb_id)` of a non-incremental verb (`begin_cut`, `begin_cap`, `begin_crank`, `field_check`, `begin_revive`, and Assembly's `begin_weld` and `begin_seam_cut`; **not `begin_harm`**, *Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING, D.8*) | when the channel aborted, the blocks it had credited (absolute) and the `n` it was written under |
| `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` | int ticks | 180..1200 (default 600) | Interaction [PLACEHOLDER] | resume window, inclusive |
| `D`, `B` | int | see D.11 | declared duration, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` | |
| `progress_pm` | int | 0..1000 | `channel_view` | the bar; a function of declared duration and the player's own history only, never of hp (IA-11c) |

**Output Range:** `resume_offset` 0..`D - B`; entries bounded by `MAX_PARTIAL_ENTRIES` per seat (eviction: smallest `abort_tick`, ties smallest `subject_ref`); expiry is evaluated every tick so snapshots are replay-stable. **Incremental repair has no ledger** (hp is the ledger, D.1).

**Example:** crank (`D` 240, `n` 40) aborted by a move at e = 100: block 16 was credited at e = 96, so `blocks_done` = 16, entry `{16, Tb + 100}`. Re-begin 400 ticks later: valid, `resume_offset` 96, remaining 144 ticks, the bar starts at `progress_pm` 400. Re-begin 600 ticks later: still valid (inclusive); 601: expired, a fresh 240 ticks and a bar from 0.

### D.7 full_job_charge

The full_job_charge formula is defined as:

`offered = (repair_part_for(subject) != NONE) AND (stowed_count >= parts_per_full OR record in {RESERVED, PAID})`; on an Applied FULL begin the part is RESERVED (no timer); at the first productive step the record becomes PAID; and the record resolves with its channel: `blocks = 0` at any early end → released; `blocks ≥ 1` at any cancel, abort or restore → consumed (PAID); `OUTCOME` → released; `DONE` → the part stays spent and the record closes *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was "`DONE` → PAID"; PAID ends at the `DONE` event, never by an hp test, IA-18)*; `PAID.grace_until = t_last_block + ticks_for_seconds(FULL_JOB_GRACE_S)`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, BLOCKING: the former `until = Tb + FULL_CHANNEL_TICKS` release timer and its `max(...)` reuse rule are retired, with `t_last_productive`. `blocks` is the count of credited blocks, equal in both truth branches, so no early exit returns a part in one branch only.)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `repair_part_for` | def or NONE | registry | Item DB (cited) | NONE means no FULL job is ever offered (IA-18 statement 1) |
| `parts_per_full` | int | 1 (reactor cell 2) | Item DB's own name | parts reserved per FULL job |
| `stowed_count` | int | 0..`stack_max` | Suit, with RESERVED and PAID excluded (SE-41) | the actor's own stock |
| `record` | enum | NONE, RESERVED, PAID | `full_jobs[]` | the per-`(seat, subject)` state (States and Transitions 5) |
| `blocks` | int | 0..`n` | the hook (IA-14 row 8): **the channel record's `blocks_done`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | blocks credited by the channel; the same in both truth branches (a block is credited whether or not its step was productive) |
| `t_last_block` | int tick | >= `Tb + B` | the hook; **persisted in the channel record** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | the tick of the last credited block (branch-independent); `post_restore` reads it from the record and re-derives nothing |
| `FULL_CHANNEL_TICKS`, `FULL_JOB_GRACE_S` | int ticks / s | 240..600 (≤ 2 × `BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS`, I-9); 30..180 | Interaction [PLACEHOLDER] | hold length and the Item DB's resume allowance |

**Output Range:** parts consumed per subject-job is exactly 0 or `parts_per_full`; the stock the actor sees falls by `parts_per_full` at the begin tick **in both truth branches** and then either returns (only at `OUTCOME`, or at an early end with `blocks = 0`) or stays down (a productive step, or any cancel, abort or restore with `blocks ≥ 1`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

**Example:** Tb = 1000, `FULL_CHANNEL_TICKS` 360.
