Healthy-but-hallucinated-broken module, cancel at 1010 (`blocks` = 1, the block at 1006 was credited with no productive step): the part is consumed at 1010 and the record is PAID with `grace_until = 1006 + 3600 = 4606`; the stock was down by 1 from 1000 and stays down. Damaged module, cancel at 1010: first productive step at 1006 -> PAID, `grace_until = 1006 + 3600 = 4606`, the stock down by 1 from 1000: **the two runs are identical at every tick**. Cancel at 1003 (`blocks` = 0): the part is released at 1003 in both runs. Run to the end at 1360: healthy -> `OUTCOME`, the part returns; damaged -> `DONE`, it stays spent and the record closes. A re-begin by the same seat before 4606 after either cancel above is not charged again *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: "after either cancel", because a `DONE` closes the record)*.

### D.8 harm_step

The harm_step formula is defined as:

`n_h = ceil(HARM_TOTAL_BP / HARM_BP_PER_STEP)` and `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS = B * n_h` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D36, `HARM_BP_PER_STEP` is now the tuned input and the channel length is derived from it, so I-2 holds by construction; the former direction, `HARM_BP_PER_STEP = ceil(HARM_TOTAL_BP * B / HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS)`, is the same relation read the other way, see the derivation below)*; damage `HARM_BP_PER_STEP` is applied only at a credited block end after the geometric reach test, the lit test and the Sanity gate of the same tick have passed; `steps_landed = min(n_h, floor((t_react + floor((REACH_PX - d0) / v)) / B))` for a victim who moves straight away. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the line above is the free-running count and is **valid only while it stays below `k_trap`**. Add the stagger term: `k_trap(I0) = floori((I0 - SUIT_LEAK_HP) / HARM_BP_PER_STEP) + 1` for `I0 >= SUIT_LEAK_HP` is the first step that leaves integrity below `SUIT_LEAK_HP` (Suit's symbols, cited), and `t_trap = B * k_trap`; for `I0 < SUIT_LEAK_HP` the victim is already CRITICAL and `k_trap = 1` (`t_trap = B`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated G2: `I0` is the victim's integrity at the begin tick; the former form hard-wired `I0 = SUIT_HP_FULL`, went negative below `SUIT_LEAK_HP`, and GDScript `/` on a negative int truncates where `floori` floors. **The D36 guarantee covers a victim at `I0 = SUIT_HP_FULL` only**; a wounded victim has a shorter window, at `I0` 7000 `k_trap` 7 and `t_trap` 42 so a walking victim has `t_react_max` 5 at `d0` 0, at `I0` 5200 the first block traps. The creative-director's lean, accepted: an already-wounded crewmate being more vulnerable is honest horror under Pillar 3; it is stated here, not implicit, and recorded as a confirm item in Open Question 48.)* A victim still inside reach when that step lands is set CRITICAL, and the Controller's `STAGGER_TICKS` stagger freezes them where they stand; every later block end inside the freeze lands, so a trapped victim takes at least `min(n_h, k_trap + floor(STAGGER_TICKS / B))` steps. With `t_last = t_react + floor((REACH_PX - d0) / v)`, the last tick the fleeing victim is still in reach: **guaranteed escape iff `t_last < t_trap`** (then `steps_landed = min(k_trap - 1, floor(t_last / B))`, never CRITICAL, no stagger); **guaranteed trap iff `t_last >= t_trap + B`** (in reach at the step whatever Suit's run alignment, since the rung is set on the next `suit.vitals` run, 1 to `B` ticks later); in the band between, the outcome depends on that alignment and is never worse than the trap. The guaranteed window is stated as a maximum reaction time: `t_react_max = t_trap - 1 - floor((REACH_PX - d0) / v)`.)*

**Derivation of `HARM_BP_PER_STEP` 300 (D36, user ruling: lower `HARM_BP_PER_STEP` so a walking victim has a guaranteed escape at the defaults; creative-director preference)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Under I-6 (`HARM_TOTAL_BP` at least `SUIT_HP_FULL`, the user's lethality decision) the step size and the channel length are one lever: a smaller step needs more steps to deliver the same total, so lowering `HARM_BP_PER_STEP` and lengthening `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS` (the other option of Open Question 44) coincide, which is why the channel becomes derived. *Constraint 1 (escape):* a walking victim (`v` = 2, `REACH_PX` 72, `d0` = 0) is out of reach `floor(72 / 2)` = 36 ticks after starting to move, so `t_last = t_react + 36`; "a guaranteed escape at the defaults" is read over the whole `t_react` range the Variables table declares, 0..60, so `t_last` at most 96 must be below `t_trap`, i.e. `t_trap > 96`, i.e. `B * k_trap >= 97`, i.e. `k_trap >= 17` (6 * 17 = 102). With `k_trap = floor(5000 / step) + 1` that holds iff `floor(5000 / step) >= 16` iff `step <= 5000 / 16 = 312.5`, so `HARM_BP_PER_STEP <= 312`. *Constraint 2 (the channel stays inside its range):* `n_h = ceil(10000 / step)` at most 40 (`HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS` at most 240) requires `step >= 250`. *Constraint 3 (lethality for the stationary victim, I-6):* `n_h * step >= HARM_TOTAL_BP` holds by construction of `n_h`. So `250 <= HARM_BP_PER_STEP <= 312`. *Choice:* 300, a round value 12 below the ceiling that leaves the channel 36 ticks under its 240 ceiling for later retuning; 312 gives the same `k_trap` 17 but sits on the exact edge of the step bound (313 drops `k_trap` to 16), so a 1-bp retune of `SUIT_LEAK_HP` would break the guarantee, and 250 would sit on the knob ceiling (channel 240) and double Sanity's gap relation. *Worked check at 300:* `n_h = ceil(10000 / 300) = 34`; `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS = 6 * 34 = 204` (a multiple of `B`, I-2; bar class by D.13 is unchanged, 204 being below `BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS` 240); 34 steps deliver 10200 bp, at least `HARM_TOTAL_BP`; `k_trap = floor(5000 / 300) + 1 = 17`, `t_trap = 102`; `t_react_max` at `d0` = 0 is 102 - 1 - 36 = 65 walking (at least 60, a 5-tick margin), 102 - 1 - 24 = 77 running, 102 - 1 - 72 = 29 carrying (it was none); a walking victim with `t_react` 60 has `t_last` 96 below 102 and loses `min(k_trap - 1, floor(96 / 6))` = 16 steps = 4800 bp, integrity 5200, STRAINED, no stagger, no leak. *Trade-off, stated:* a trapped victim (`t_last >= t_trap + B` = 108) is staggered at step 17 (e = 102) and takes at least `min(n_h, 17 + floor(30 / 6))` = 22 steps = 6600 bp (integrity at most 3400, CRITICAL, walk-only, above `SUIT_HP_FAIL` 2500; one or two more steps may land after the freeze ends, depending on the Suit-run alignment and the distance the victim still has to cover), so the stagger alone no longer fails the victim; FAILED needs more than 7500 bp, i.e. step 26 (e = 156), so the act stays lethal for a victim who remains in reach, which is the user's lethality decision; guaranteed lethality for a victim who has had time to react is gone. *Cross-owner effect:* Sanity's relation `SAN_HARM_EXIT - SAN_HARM_ENTER >= ceil(HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS / 12) x max(DRIFT_MAX_PER_RUN, REC_MAX)` rises from 10 x 36 = 360 to 17 x 36 = 612 (a note for Sanity).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `HARM_TOTAL_BP` | int bp | `SUIT_HP_FULL` (I-6) up to `40 * HARM_BP_PER_STEP` (12000 at the default step; the upper bound is the `n_h <= 40` lint, *added 2026-10-27 — re-review*), default 10000 | Interaction [PLACEHOLDER], Suit's units | damage delivered by a full act |
| `HARM_BP_PER_STEP` | int bp | 250..312 (default 300) | Interaction [PLACEHOLDER], derived in the D.8 derivation *(added 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D36)* | damage per credited block end, the tuned input |
| `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS` | int ticks | 198..240 (derived: 204 at the defaults; *range corrected 2026-10-27 — re-review: 60..240 was unreachable, `n_h` is 33..40 for a step of 250..312 at `HARM_TOTAL_BP` 10000*); lint `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS < BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS`, so the harm bar stays MEDIUM as the Visual/Audio table and D.13 state | `B * n_h` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D36, was an input with default 120)* | channel length |
| `n_h` | int | 33..40 at `HARM_TOTAL_BP` 10000 and a step of 250..312; lint `n_h <= 40` *(corrected 2026-10-27 — re-review: was 10..40)* | `ceil(HARM_TOTAL_BP / HARM_BP_PER_STEP)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D36)* | steps in a full act (34 at the defaults; was 20) |
| `d0` | int px | 0..`REACH_PX` | geometry | victim distance when the act begins |
| `v` | int px/tick | 1 (`V_CARRY`), 2 (`V_WALK`), 3 (`V_RUN`) | Controller (cited) | victim speed |
| `t_react` | int ticks | 0..60 | assumption (0 = worst case for the victim) | ticks before the victim starts moving |
| `I0` | int bp | 0..`SUIT_HP_FULL`; 10000 for the D36 guarantee | Suit's `integrity_bp` of the victim at the begin tick; an analysis input for the escape arithmetic only, **not read at runtime** by Interaction *(added 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | the victim's starting integrity |
| `k_trap`, `t_trap` | int, int ticks | 17 and 102 at the defaults (`I0` = `SUIT_HP_FULL`; 11 and 66 at the former `HARM_BP_PER_STEP` 500; 21 and 126 at 250) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D36)* | `floori((I0 - SUIT_LEAK_HP) / HARM_BP_PER_STEP) + 1` for `I0 >= SUIT_LEAK_HP`, else 1 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was the `I0 = SUIT_HP_FULL` special case)*; `B * k_trap`; Suit's `SUIT_HP_FULL` 10000 and `SUIT_LEAK_HP` 5000 (registry) | the step and tick at which a victim still in reach is set CRITICAL and staggered *(added 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `STAGGER_TICKS` | int ticks | 30 | Controller (registry, cited) | how long a trapped victim is frozen in reach; 5 more block ends at `B` 6 |
| `t_last`, `t_react_max` | int ticks | see Output Range | derived | last in-reach tick of a fleeing victim; the largest reaction time that still escapes |

**Output Range:** `HARM_BP_PER_STEP` 300 at defaults *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D36, was 500)*; `steps_landed` 0..34; damage 0..10200 (clamped by Suit at integrity 0). **Stationary victim: all 34 steps land (e = 6 .. 204), 10200 bp delivered, at least `HARM_TOTAL_BP`.** The first damage lands at e = 6. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D36 lowers `HARM_BP_PER_STEP` to 300 and removes the cliff for walking and running victims; the next parenthesis restates the cliff at the new defaults (it was recorded at the former 500 bp, 120 ticks), and the former closing sentence, "These are options for the user ... the registered values stay", is superseded by the ruling. At the new defaults a victim still in reach when step 17 lands (e = 102) is staggered for 30 ticks and takes steps 18 to 22 (e = 108 to 132), at least 6600 bp in all (integrity at most 3400), CRITICAL and not FAILED by the stagger alone; FAILED needs 26 steps (e = 156).)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: **the escape is a cliff, not a slope.** At the defaults the 17th step (e = 102, integrity 4900) drops the victim below `SUIT_LEAK_HP`; a victim still in reach is staggered for 30 ticks and then takes steps 18 to 22 at e = 108 to 132, ending at integrity at most 3400, CRITICAL and above `SUIT_HP_FAIL` 2500. So the real reaction window is at most 77 ticks (the running victim's maximum, below) and a walking victim's is 65, which clears the 60 the free-running count implies; the maximum reaction times at `d0` = 0 are `t_react_max` = 65 ticks walking, 77 running and 29 carrying (a carrying victim needs 72 ticks to leave reach, so `t_react` of at most 29 still beats `t_trap` 102 by moving; a later start must `drop` and run). A victim who is out of reach by `t_trap - 1` = 101 loses at most 16 steps = 4800 bp, integrity 5200, STRAINED, no stagger, no leak. Lengthening `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS` at the former 500 bp step moved the cliff outward without changing lethality for a stationary victim; those were options for the user (Open Question 44: 180 ticks with `HARM_BP_PER_STEP` 334 and `t_trap` 90, or 240 with 250 and 126); the user ruled on 2026-10-27 (D36) for lowering `HARM_BP_PER_STEP` to 300 (see the derivation above), and the former figures (`t_react_max` 29 walking, 41 running, none carrying; `t_trap` 66; at most 10 steps = 5000 bp) are superseded.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)* **No `apply_suit_damage` call ever follows a failed reach test on the same tick** (IA-14 order: rows 4 to 6 before row 8), so none lands on a victim who is out of reach at a block end; between checks only ticks accrue, never damage. The victim escapes iff at some block end `Tb + 6j` the geometric conjuncts of `in_reach` fail — leaving the 70-degree arc about the actor's locked facing counts, not only distance. **When death follows is Suit's rule** (IA-31; SE-23: the rung is set from `integrity_bp` on the run after each apply, knob default 1; SE-24): this formula states only how much exogenous damage is delivered.

**Design intent (guard rail; anti-pillar 1, not a combat game).** This formula exists only to guarantee the victim's Pillar 2 escape window: a conscious victim who runs leaves reach and takes only part of `HARM_TOTAL_BP`. It is not a combat model and must never be tuned for lethality, damage rate or effectiveness; lethality is fixed by the user's decision through I-6 and Suit's SE-23 exogenous rule (`SUIT_EXO_BYPASS_LIMITER` default 1), not tuned here, and the reach-and-flee arithmetic below is a check that the window exists, not a balance target. Any change to `HARM_TOTAL_BP`, `HARM_BP_PER_STEP` or `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS` needs a creative-director review against the anti-pillar (the D36 change was the creative-director's stated preference). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING, delegated G2 (the creative-director's stated resolution): **every harm attempt restarts at `e = 0` with its own full escape window.** `begin_harm` is not in the partial-resume ledger (IA-16, D.6): a harmer who stops at step 16 and re-begins within `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` gets a fresh bar whose first damage lands at e = 6, not a resumed bar whose next block lands 6 ticks after the re-begin, so the 65-tick walking window that D36 guarantees is a property of every attempt, not only the first; cumulative damage already persists in the victim's Suit integrity, so nothing is lost by dropping the ledger. "Resumed harm is lethal by design" is rejected as a reading of D36.)*

**Example:** a victim at d0 = 0 who starts running (v = 3) on e = 1 reaches 72 px at tick 24 and is still in reach there (`d^2 <= REACH_PX^2`), so steps land at e = 6, 12, 18, 24 only: `floor(24 / 6) = 4` steps, 2000 bp. Walking (v = 2): `floor(36 / 6) = 6` steps, 3000 bp; ~~carrying (v = 1): 12 steps, 6000 bp~~ *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: carrying (v = 1) has `t_last` = 72 >= `t_trap` + `B` = 72, a guaranteed trap: 11 steps put integrity at 4500, the victim is staggered in reach and takes steps 12 to 16, 8000 bp in all, integrity 2000, FAILED; the 12-step, 6000 bp figure ignored the stagger and the example was lethal)*. A victim at d0 = 24 walking with `t_react` 15: `floor((15 + 24) / 6) = 6` steps, 3000 bp (`t_last` 39 < 66, escape). A cornered victim in a dead end takes the full 10000 bp.

**Example at the new defaults** *(added 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D36; the figures above use the former 500 bp step)*: running (`v` 3, `d0` 0, `t_react` 0): `floor(24 / 6)` = 4 steps, 1200 bp; walking (`v` 2): `floor(36 / 6)` = 6 steps, 1800 bp; walking with `t_react` 60: `t_last` 96 below `t_trap` 102, `floor(96 / 6)` = 16 steps, 4800 bp, integrity 5200, escapes; carrying (`v` 1) with `t_react` 29: `t_last` 101 below 102, 16 steps, 4800 bp, escapes; carrying with `t_react` 36: `t_last` 108 at least `t_trap + B`, a guaranteed trap, staggered at step 17, at least 22 steps, 6600 bp or more (integrity at most 3400); a stationary victim: 34 steps, 10200 bp, FAILED from step 26 (e = 156).

### D.9 noise_push

The noise_push formula is defined as:

`push(T) = U[TOOL]` on each tick the hook accrues a TOOL-class channel (IA-14 row 7), `U[LOUD]` once at a LOUD event (cut completion, seam-cut DONE, vent apply, throw landing, `berth` apply *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings, consistency follow-up)*), and 0 otherwise; the job total is `U[TOOL] * accrued_ticks`.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `U[class]` | int stimulus units | Attention's | Attention (Designed — AT-3; registry `noise_unit_relations`) | per-class unit; Interaction supplies the **class**, never units |
| `accrued_ticks` | int | 0..`D` | D.11 | ticks actually accrued (an aborting tick pushes nothing) |
| walk / run / carry | int | 1 / 4 / 1 | `NOISE_PER_TICK` (Controller, cited) | the reference scale |

**Output Range:** three relations Attention's units must satisfy or the verbs become free or absurd (acceptance conditions handed over, IA-33): `2 <= U[TOOL] <= 6`; `U[LOUD] >= 30 * U[TOOL]`; `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS <= 2 * TAU_ATT_TICKS` (I-8); Attention accepts all three (registry `noise_unit_relations`). A breaker close and a door motor push nothing, and **carrying pushes nothing from Interaction**: the carry intake of X3 is Attention's own, read from the carry state, never an Interaction class or unit *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Splitting a job through the ledger never reduces its total noise, only spreads it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: and it can raise it, by at most `(B - 1) * U[TOOL]` per split, because the ticks accrued since the last credited block are accrued again after the resume; exactly equal only on a block boundary)*.

**Example (illustrative; Attention owns the number):** with `U[TOOL]` = 4, a 180-tick patch integrates to 720 units — four times walking for the same 180 ticks (180) and equal to running (720), inside the 2x-to-1.5x band.

### D.10 subject_budget

The subject_budget formula is defined as:

`S_total = F + L_seed + C + P + Y + A + H <= MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS`, with the Interaction-owned lint `F + L_seed + C <= INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` and `P + Y <= PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE`, summed over every section loaded at the same time *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D37, now summed over the registration window `W` of the bound bodies and its one-hop hysteresis ring, not over every loaded section: `F`, `L_seed` and `C` count only candidates in the window; the lint is `W_int(p) = F + L_seed + C <= INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` at every authored body position `p`; `S_total` is bounded above by the loaded-set sum, so the registered figures remain the safe upper bound)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the term `Y`, the ITEM subjects created by salvage yield, was not budgeted.)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `F` | int | 0..120 | `sync_section` candidates (two faces per door) inside the registration window *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D37, was every declared interactable of every loaded section)* | fixtures |
| `SUBJECT_WINDOW_HOPS` | int | 1..4 (provisional 2) [PLACEHOLDER — derive at spike] | Interaction, new knob *(added 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D37)* | hop distance from a bound body's room within which candidates are registered; the hysteresis ring is one more |
| `L_seed`, `C`, `P` | int | 0..20, 0..18, 0..24 | seeded loose items, cap stubs (<= 6 modules x <= 3 ports), player-dropped items | |
| `Y` | int | 0..24 (shares the `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE` pool with `P`: `P + Y <= 24`) | `begin_cut` completion: one ITEM subject per yielded instance (one instance of the module's def, or `scrap_count` parts for a DESTROYED node, IDB-17) | salvage-yield items, created by the player's action like a drop *(added 2026-10-27 — design review)*. `begin_cut`'s `validate` refuses with immediate `OBSTRUCTED` when `subject_headroom()` is below `max(1, scrap_count)` of the def, so a yield is never left unregistered |
| `A`, `H` | int | <= 4, <= `HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` (2, Horror Entities, registry) | other owners' numbers quoted as a lint budget (registry `horror_subject_count`: `HORROR_SUBJECTS_MAX` + `A` <= `MAX_MOVING_SUBJECTS` 16; 16 is the moving-subject cap, not the horror count) | actors and horrors |
| `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` | int | 192 | Emitter State (cited) | hard cap; over it `register_subject` returns false and the object fails dark, which reads as a hallucination (EM-21) |

**Output Range:** a content lint, not a runtime state; `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET + PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE <= 144` (I-7), leaving at least 42 slack after `A` and `H`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: **(i) the totals below hold only at `PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE` 24**; the lint is parameterised by the reserve: the relation is `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET + PLAYER_DROP_RESERVE <= 144` for any reserve, and the S3/S4 examples are the reserve-24 instances, so a retuned reserve moves every example total by its difference from 24. **(ii) Forced drops are unbudgeted by the reserve** (a downed actor's carry and the NONE-cap conversion are never refused, D.5) and are bounded by one carried module per seat: `forced_drops <= seats` (at most 4), drawn from the slack; the runtime ceiling is therefore `S_total + seats <= 192`, 179 at the S4 figure of 175 plus 4 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: Derelict Authoring L_seed B 3, one loose placement fewer)*. **(iii) The 120 lint protects the I-7 authoring budget (the 25 % margin), not the 192 hard cap**, which is protected by the slack of at least 42.)* Per registered loaded set (`legal_loaded_sets`) and authored body position: ERROR when the window sum `W_int(p) > INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET`; the S4 WARN is retired *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D37)*, and the S4 verdict is the measured worst window ([PLACEHOLDER — derive at spike]; it must be at most 120).

**Example (registered figures, `legal_loaded_sets`; Derelict Authoring counts `F`, `L_seed` and `C`):** home ship plus derelict B welded (S3, the weld case): `S_int` 113 (at most 120) + P 24 + A 4 + H 2 = 143, 49 below the cap *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: Derelict Authoring L_seed B 3, one loose placement fewer)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* Home+B with derelict A loaded (S4): `S_int` 145, 25 over `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` (a WARN before the D37 ruling, now a window lint; OQ-DA-20, Open Question 30), + (P + Y) 24 + A 4 + H 2 = 175, still 17 below `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: Derelict Authoring L_seed B 3, one loose placement fewer)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*, so nothing fails dark *(re-checked 2026-10-27 — design review: with `Y` sharing the player reserve the S4 total is unchanged at 175 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*; a build that let `Y` exceed the reserve would reach 175 + Y_excess, and 17 is the ceiling on that excess before S4 fails dark)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: Derelict Authoring L_seed B 3, one loose placement fewer)*. A dormant section **must** unregister its subjects (`sync_section`, IA-37): unloading A from S4 lowers `S_int` by exactly A's `F` plus its uncollected `L_seed` (28 + 4 = 32). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D37. Window example: S4 `{home+B, A}` has loaded `S_int` 145 = 113 (home+B) + 32 (A) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: Derelict Authoring L_seed B 3, one loose placement fewer)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*. A body in A registers A's 32 plus only the home+B rooms inside the window beyond the dock link; the lint needs `W_int` at most 120, i.e. the home+B rooms inside the window carry at most 88 subjects, i.e. the home+B rooms outside it carry at least 25 of its 113 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: Derelict Authoring L_seed B 3, one loose placement fewer)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* The mirrored condition holds for a body in home+B. The room-level counts are Authoring's and Layout's: [PLACEHOLDER — derive at spike]. Runtime safety does not depend on it, since a window is a subset of its loaded set and `S_total` ≤ 175 ≤ 192.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*

### D.11 channel_clock

The channel_clock formula is defined as:

`e = T - Tb`; accrue tick `e` iff `e >= 1` and no abort row fires; block `j' = e / B` of this run is credited at `e = B * j'` (never at `e = 0`), which is the **absolute** block `j = resume_blocks + j'` of the job, `resume_blocks = resume_offset / B`, and sets `blocks_done = j` and `t_last_block = T` for every verb *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the former "block `j` at `e = B * j`" and IA-14 row 8's absolute `j` were two incompatible definitions; `j'` is local to the run, `j` absolute, and `e` is always local)*; lit and reach checks run at `e mod LIT_RECHECK_TICKS == 0` (including `e = 0`); completion at `e == D - resume_offset`; `dark_ticks_max = LIT_RECHECK_TICKS - 1`; `progress_pm = floor((resume_offset + e) * 1000 / D)`.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T`, `Tb` | int tick | >= 0 | kernel; the Applied begin | current tick, begin tick |
| `B` | int | 6 | `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` (cited) | block length |
| `D` | int ticks | 60..600, a multiple of `B` (I-2) | the declared `*_CHANNEL_TICKS` / `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` | duration *(range corrected 2026-10-27 — design review: it was 90..480, which excluded `CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS` 60 and `FULL_CHANNEL_TICKS` 600 that the knob ranges allow)* |
| `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS` | int ticks | {1, 2, 3, 6}, I-1 | Interaction [PLACEHOLDER] | check cadence |
| `resume_offset` | int ticks | 0..`D - B` | D.6 | resume point |
| `j'`, `j` | int | `j'` 1..`n - resume_blocks`; `j` 1..`n` | `n = D / B` | local block index of this run; absolute block index `resume_blocks + j'` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |

**Output Range:** `blocks_done` 0..`n - 1` in the ledger and in any channel that ends by cancel, abort or restore; `n` only on `DONE` (row 8 credits block `n` and row 9 completes on the same tick, and a move on that tick wins first, so an aborted job never reads `n`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: ranges now agree)*; at most `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS - 1` (5) dark ticks accrue inside a credited block (Emitter State AC-59b); a move on `e = D` leaves `blocks_done = n - 1` (the move wins, PC-7); steps land at `Tb + 6, Tb + 12, ...`, never at `Tb`.

**Example:** PATCH, `Tb` 1000, `D` 180, `n` 30. Lamp off at 1013, back on at 1016: checks at e = 12 (1012) and e = 18 (1018) pass, block 3 (e 13..18) is credited, three dark ticks accrued, no abort. Lamp off at 1013 and still off at 1018: the check at e = 18 aborts `UNLIT` before block 3 is credited, `blocks_done` 2, the hp steps stay applied. A cancel inside the first six ticks spends no step, no part and no ledger entry.

### D.12 door_attempt

The door_attempt formula is defined as:

`dir = OPEN if area_true == 0 else CLOSE` (fixed at apply); at `T == Tb + DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS`: if `served_pm == 0` -> `PRECONDITION(NO_POWER)`; else submit SYSTEM `set_opening {target_ref, area}` (`area = area_max` for OPEN, 0 for CLOSE); in P2 of T+1: if `dir == CLOSE` and `leaf_would_overlap(door_ref, 0, foot_boxes)` -> `CLOSING_BLOCKED` (the hook re-submits once, on the first clear tick of its pure read, *Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was each tick*) else M13. A SYSTEM-origin close from a cascade takes the same P2 path with no window. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: four additions. (i) The attempt is keyed by `door_ref`, one per door for both faces. (ii) In the P2 apply, **if `area_true` already equals the target `area`, skip the check and M13 and end the attempt.** (iii) A PLAYER attempt that has spent `CLOSING_BLOCKED_TIMEOUT_TICKS` in `CLOSING_BLOCKED` ends with the leaf open and no token. (iv) A SYSTEM close applied on the door drops every open PLAYER attempt on it. See IA-24.)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `area_true` | int | 0..`area_max` | the Graph's `opening(edge_id)` read (provisional), read in apply | the true leaf aperture |
| `DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` | int ticks | 18..60 (default 30) | Interaction [PLACEHOLDER] | uniform window for powered and unpowered doors (no latency oracle) |
| `served_pm` | int pm | 0..1000 | the Graph, read in the hook only | motor power |
| `foot_boxes[]` | rect list | one per actor on the body, unfiltered (BDY-18) | Body's `foot_boxes(body_id)` (Ship Body & Interior Physics, Designed — BDY-18), read in the P2 apply of the SYSTEM `set_opening` (the boxes committed at the end of the previous tick) | passed to Layout's `leaf_would_overlap` (Layout, Designed — LY-11) |

**Output Range:** exactly one of M13 applied (always in a P2 apply, never in P3), `PRECONDITION(NO_POWER)` (written by the hook, no command), or `CLOSING_BLOCKED` until clear; no outcome token exists for a late overlap. `TAP_REARM_TICKS` runs from the end of the attempt.

**Example:** a PLAYER close at Tb = 500: the window ends at 530, the motor is powered, so the hook submits the SYSTEM `set_opening` at 530 and it applies at 531; a crewmate's foot box overlaps the leaf until it clears at 541: the attempt is `CLOSING_BLOCKED` from 531, the hook's pure read is clear first at 541 and it re-submits once there *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was "re-submitted each tick")*, and M13(0) lands in P2 of 542 (42 ticks after the press, the latch strain throughout and the motor sound only from the tick the leaf travels *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: was "the motor whine throughout")*), with `layout.doors` projecting the closed leaf in that same tick. Unpowered door: at 530 the hook sees `served_pm == 0`, the outcome is `PRECONDITION(NO_POWER)` and no command is submitted; the leaf never moved.

### D.13 length_class

The length_class formula is defined as:

`length_class = SHORT if mode == HOLD_CONFIRM; else MEDIUM if D < BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS; else LONG`, computed per seat from (declaration row, tool, the actor's stock) and frozen at the press; for V1 it follows the PATCH/FULL choice of IA-18.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `D` | int ticks | 60..600 | D.11 | declared duration *(range corrected 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS` | int ticks | 180..300 (default 240) | Interaction [PLACEHOLDER] | MEDIUM/LONG boundary |

**Output Range:** SHORT, MEDIUM or LONG; never a function of hp, state, `forced_mode` or `served_pm`.

**Example:** patch (180) MEDIUM; FULL (360) LONG; crank and salvage cut (240) LONG; cap (90), field check (120), harm (204) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: derived from D36, was 120)*, revive (180) MEDIUM; V6 hold SHORT. A V1 press by an actor who holds the part is LONG, without it MEDIUM — the actor's own stock, never the pipe. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "holds the part" reads "has the part readied" under the declaration selector of IA-18, so an actor who carries a part and has not readied it gets MEDIUM and a PATCH.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: that reading is the post-MVP form; at MVP "holds the part" stands, the stock rule of IA-18, and the actor sees it before the press from `part_tell`.)*

## Edge Cases

*Grouped by theme. Each case names its diagnostic counter where one exists (`IA_*`, owned by Interaction under `diag_panel_contract`). "Exact outcome" means the state the simulation is in afterwards; a presentation cue is named only where the cue is the specified outcome.*

**A. Light, reach and the channel clock**

- **If the lamp is off at a check tick (`e mod LIT_RECHECK_TICKS == 0`) mid-channel**: the channel ends `ABORT(UNLIT)`, the bar snaps empty with the *interrupted* cue, and the block ending on that tick is not credited (steps already applied stay applied; a non-incremental verb writes its ledger entry). `IA_CHANNEL_ABORT{UNLIT}`. No step is ever earned on an unlit block end (I-1). Not for the self-patch: an ACTOR_SELF record is `act_gate`-exempt (IA-6) and IA-14 rows 4-5 do not run for it *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated G6, GAP: the owner adds the surface the Controller's ruled behaviour needs)*.
- **If the lamp flickers off and back on between two check ticks**: nothing aborts and the block is credited; at most `LIT_RECHECK_TICKS - 1` (5) dark ticks accrue. A flicker must not cancel work, and Emitter State AC-59b bounds the exposure.
- **If the lamp dies between `validate` (which read tick T-1's table) and the begin's `apply`**: the hook's check at `e = 0` re-reads tick T after `emitter.place` and aborts at progress 0 with no ledger entry. The one-tick staleness of `validate` can never start a job in the dark.
- **If a move intent is already held when `begin_*` is pressed**: `validate` answers immediate `BUSY` (PC-7: the owner requires `octant == NONE`; IA-10) and nothing is Applied. **If the move intent applies after `validate` within the same P2** (command order on one tick): the begin is Applied and the hook aborts `ABORT(MOVED)` at `e = 0` before any accrual; no step, no part use, no ledger entry (zero blocks). The move always wins (PC-7).
- **If the move intent arrives on the completion tick (`e = D`)**: abort-before-accrual leaves `blocks_done = n - 1`; a `begin_cut` module is not removed (M6 runs at completion only), a PATCH has landed 29 of 30 steps. The player re-begins; a cut resumes within `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS`, repair recomputes from hp (D.1).
- **If the focused object sits exactly on the arc or distance rim at press**: `in_reach` decides in integers: at `d^2 = REACH_PX^2` it is in reach, one pixel beyond it is `OUT_OF_RANGE` (immediate, dull cue, no shake). `IA_REJECT{OUT_OF_RANGE}`. The prompt (HUD) may lag the rim by its show debounce but never leads it.
- **If `cancel_channel` and an abort cause fall on the same tick**: `cancel_channel` (P2) wins; one terminal state, the hook finds no record. If there is no channel at all, `cancel_channel` is Applied as a no-op (`IA_CANCEL_NOOP`) — the Adapter sends it on every hold release, and a reject would play the dull cue after every completed job.
- **If the pause is entered mid-channel**: the tick does not advance, so the hook does not run, nothing accrues, no TTL ages and no verb is admitted (`PAUSED`, never queued). Resuming continues from the same tick.
- **If the zoom step `k` or the HUD scale `H` changes mid-channel**: nothing in the simulation changes — `REACH_PX` and every Interaction length are base (world) pixels; only the HUD re-projects the plate and bar (UI Requirements).
- **If the player switches input device or uses hold versus toggle for the same verb**: the simulation sees the same command and the same `channel_view`; no Interaction rule reads the device (IA-4, lint). A pad user who never touches the right stick focuses by facing.

**B. Repair, the part and the patch**

- **If a PATCH targets a hallucinated-fixed pipe (true hp 2000)**: real increments land against 2000 (D.1: 134 bp per step), hp crosses `LEAK_HP` at step 15 (e = 90) and the hiss stops about 1.5 s into the job; no message appears. The cautious player is rewarded by physics (P1).
- **If a PATCH targets a hallucinated-broken healthy pipe (hp 10000)**: the bar runs the full 180 ticks, zero M7 calls, `hp_cap` untouched, outcome `PRECONDITION(NOTHING_TO_REPAIR)` plus the *nothing happened* cue. `IA_DEFERRED_REVEAL{NOTHING_TO_REPAIR}`. The price of checking is time, noise and light, never free (P4).
- **If a PATCH targets a DESTROYED subject**: each block's `check_repair_step` fails and skips the apply (never FAULTED); the job runs 180 ticks and resolves `PRECONDITION(NOT_REPAIRABLE)`. The path is salvage (scrap) then install (LS-23).
- **If a FULL job is interrupted after its first productive step (or, branch-independently, after its first credited block)**: the record is PAID, the part stays spent, hp stays raised, and a re-begin by the same seat within `FULL_JOB_GRACE_S` is not charged again and issues only `kind = FULL` steps. `IA_FULL_RESUME`. A refund would make interruption a free probe.
- **If a FULL job is Applied on a truly healthy module and the player cancels at e = 10** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, BLOCKING: rewritten; the former text pinned a leak)*: `blocks` is 1 (the block at e = 6 was credited, productive or not), so the part is consumed in both truth branches: a healthy and a damaged module leave the actor's stock, the weight cue and Suit's reserved bucket identical at every tick from the begin tick on, through `Tb + 2 × FULL_CHANNEL_TICKS` and beyond, and the part does not come back in either. There is no oracle to read by cancelling and walking away; the only branch-dependent reading is the end of a bar that ran in full (`OUTCOME` returns the part, `DONE` does not) (IA-18, D.7, AC-IA-132).
- **If a FULL job is cancelled before its first block is credited (e < 6, or any abort at e = 0)**: `blocks` is 0, no step has differed between the branches, and the part is released at once in both (`IA_RESERVE_RELEASE`); the stock reads identically in both branches before and after.
- **If the same seat re-begins on a PAID record**: the record is reused, no second part is reserved or charged, and only `kind = FULL` steps are issued; a RESERVED record cannot be found by a re-begin because it exists only while a channel of that seat runs (IA-18).
- **If a FULL job's bar runs to its end on a module that never had a productive step**: the channel resolves `OUTCOME` (`NOTHING_TO_REPAIR` or `NOT_REPAIRABLE`) and the part returns (`IA_RESERVE_RELEASE`): the only path that returns a part after the first credited block.
- **If a record reaches the per-seat bound `MAX_GRACE_ENTRIES`**: the entry with the smallest `grace_until` is evicted; an evicted PAID entry costs the actor one re-charge (accepted, `IA_GRACE_EVICT`).
- **If two seats target one subject (Vertical Slice B)**: the second begin is immediate `BUSY` and makes no reservation; exactly one part is spent (Item DB AC-89 in intent; the token differs because dispatch is sequential, IA-34).
- **If a PATCH is chosen on a subject at hp 4000 to 5999 with `hp_cap` 10000 (the silent band, no leak)** *(rewritten 2026-10-27 — re-review, BLOCKING; the former text accepted a 1-bp gain for a permanent 4000-bp `hp_cap` loss)*: the guard of IA-17 makes zero M7 calls, `hp_cap` is untouched, no weep sounds, the bar runs the full 180 ticks and the job resolves `PRECONDITION(NOTHING_TO_REPAIR)` with the *nothing happened* cue. The Graph state, descriptors and noise are identical to a PATCH on a healthy hp 10000 pipe, so a one-block tap-and-cancel tells nothing (Design Test 2). The damage is real, silent and FULL work: the player who holds the part readied gets a FULL job there. A subject already patched (`hp_cap` = `PATCH_CAP_BP`) is topped up or resumed normally. Pinned by AC-IA-90 and AC-IA-26.
- **If a FULL job runs on a subject at hp 9950**: it is productive for 50 bp and the part is spent (the exemption covers only `hp = hp_cap = HP_FULL`). Accepted cost of the decide-from-own-stock rule (D10b): a held part may be spent where a patch would have met the need.
- **If the actor patches their own suit with the welder (the self-patch)**: steps call `patch_integrity` against `SUIT_PATCH_CAP_BP` at the IA-14 cadence; the verb is an `act_gate`-exempt actor verb (IA-6), so the press is accepted with the lamp on or off and IA-14 rows 4-5 do not run for it. *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated G6, GAP: the owner adds the surface the Controller's ruled behaviour needs)*

**C. The Graph verbs**

- **If a breaker is toggled onto a short**: the toggle runs against the true position, the Graph raises its short/overload and inrush (`INRUSH_PM`, `INRUSH_STEPS`) and the lamps answer; the verb is exogenous, so the fairness gate does not delay it (LS-13). Interaction pushes no extra stimulus for the breaker (the Graph's `power_use` is Attention's power stimulus).
- **If a TAP is repeated on one subject within `TAP_REARM_TICKS` (12)**: immediate `BUSY`. It stops 60 Hz breaker cycling used as an inrush generator and gives a door leaf time to travel. `IA_REJECT{BUSY}`.
- **If the panel gauge lies ("closed" while truly open) and the player presses "open"**: the toggle acts on the true position, so the breaker closes; the inrush and the lamps are the answer and no reject or hint intervenes (LS-22: a payload built from the displayed state would be a believed state).
- **If the running scrubber (or the battery during a SCRAM) is salvaged**: the module is removed at completion and the room starves that run (LSG:773); the hum of the thing being cut was the tell, and the exogenous cause is exempt from `fairness_gate` (R28).
- **If `begin_cut` aborts at any point before completion**: the module stays in place and intact; the effect lands at completion only, and the ledger resumes within the TTL.
- **If a salvaged module leaves open stubs and the player never caps them**: the hiss persists as the consequence-channel tell; no timer forces anything. A stub already capped by another seat is `INVALID_TARGET` (immediate).
- **If the crank (V12) targets a SCRAMmed reactor that is still hot or starved**: the channel runs the full 240 ticks and M11 returns `BLOCKED(STILL_HOT)` or `BLOCKED(NO_FUEL)`; the outcome is the Graph's token, passed through; the heat shimmer was the cheaper earlier warning. `IA_DEFERRED_REVEAL{STILL_HOT}`.
- **If the crank targets a node that is not in SCRAM or FREEZE**: the channel runs 240 ticks and resolves `PRECONDITION(ALREADY_RUNNING)`; the prompt exists on every CRANK_MACHINE regardless of state (IA-11b).
- **If a PLAYER-origin door close is pressed while a bound seat's foot box overlaps the leaf**: immediate `OBSTRUCTED` at `validate`, the leaf stays open. `IA_REJECT{OBSTRUCTED}`.
- **If a foot box overlaps the leaf when a closing attempt reaches its overlap test (the P2 apply of the SYSTEM `set_opening`: a PLAYER attempt after its window, a cascade close at once)**: the attempt enters `CLOSING_BLOCKED` and the hook re-submits the same SYSTEM command once, on the first tick its pure `leaf_would_overlap` read is clear, M13 landing in the P2 after it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was "every tick")*; no outcome token is published. A SYSTEM close lost at a restore is re-issued by its cascade.
- **If the door motor is unpowered (`served_pm` 0)**: the press is Applied, the attempt window of `DOOR_ATTEMPT_TICKS` (30) runs with the power-neutral latch strain (identical audio and visual events to a powered door's window, no motor-spin character, a frame-latch strain for the deaf player, IA-24), and the result is `PRECONDITION(NO_POWER)` with the leaf unmoved. The window is the same for powered doors, so latency is never a power oracle. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the former "motor whine" for 30 ticks on an unpowered door was a false world statement, a Pillar 1 violation; the motor sound now plays only when the leaf actually travels.)*
- **If a PLAYER close sits in `CLOSING_BLOCKED` (a crewmate or another actor holds the doorway)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the hook waits for the first clear tick (a pure read) and submits once then *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was "re-submits every tick")*, or until `CLOSING_BLOCKED_TIMEOUT_TICKS` (300) have passed, when the attempt ends with the leaf open and no token and the door's re-arm stamp starts. A SYSTEM close has no timeout.
- **If a SYSTEM close seals a door while a PLAYER open attempt on it is still in its window** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the SYSTEM apply drops the PLAYER attempt, so the player's open cannot fire a window later and defeat the cascade. If the leaf already equals an attempt's target when its completion applies, nothing is called and the attempt ends.
- **If the two faces of one door are pressed together** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `attempts[]` and `rearm[]` are keyed by the door, so the second press is `BUSY` whichever face it came from; there are never two attempts on one door.
- **If a vented hatch is pressed again**: V6 toggles against the true aperture — it closes (`area` 0) after the same `CONFIRM_HOLD_S` hold; refilling the room is the Graph's job and is priced by the Graph (LSG:797). A vent confirmed while the actor stands inside the vented compartment proceeds (exogenous; death is Suit's rule).
- **If `top_up` finds the store empty (M16 grants 0)**: Applied-and-did-nothing, no token and no cue; the suit's own reserve reading not rising is the answer. If the actor's deficit is already 0, the press is rejected with Suit's `ALREADY_FULL` (immediate, the actor's own state).

**D. Carry, drop and throw**

- **If `pickup` is pressed while carrying**: immediate `BUSY` (every mode except `drop` and the carry-admitted rows, IA-28). `IA_REJECT{BUSY}`. A tool in the HAND holder with nothing carried never makes `pickup` `BUSY`.
- **If a STOWED-class pickup would overflow the stack**: immediate Suit-namespaced `STACK_FULL` (from `can_stow`); the item stays on the floor. If a TOOL is picked up while the held tool's charge is 0, Suit's `replace_tool` runs and the discarded instance is placed as a loose item (D.5).
- **If all nine drop candidates are blocked**: immediate `OBSTRUCTED` and the module stays carried; a forced drop (downed actor, NONE cap) falls back to the foot origin regardless of overlap — a carried module is never deleted and never refused.
- **If a module is dropped in the dark**: `drop` is lit-exempt and the item is placed, but it cannot be re-taken until something lights it — a deliberate Pillar 3 cost.
- **If the actor is downed while carrying**: the Controller clears `carry_ref`; the next hook run converts the module to a loose item at the foot origin by Suit's `release_carried`; the item subject is registered and the module can be picked up again by another seat.
- **If a throw lands in a dormant or unknown section**: `spawn_deployable` returns false, the item stays spent, the throw resolves silently (`IA_THROW_VOID`, EM:699). A refund would be a free test.
- **If a throw is made while `MAX_DEPLOYABLES_LIVE` (8) sticks are live**: accepted and spent; at landing the lowest-id non-evicting stick of the same kind enters a visible evict-wane, never an instant removal (Emitter State).
- **If a throw is attempted while carrying, channelling, staggered or downed, or with no stick**: immediate `BUSY` (the first four) or `OUT_OF_CHARGES` (no stock). Nothing queues (`ACTION_BUFFER_TICKS` 0).
- **If the wall clip is the first pixel**: `land = foot`; the stick is spent and lights its thrower's feet; a closed door leaf is a SOLID rect (LY-9), so a stick never passes it. Two throws on one tick apply in `seq` order, both spent. A thrower who dies in flight still sees the stick land.

**E. Falsification, harm, phantoms**

- **If a sane actor has a crewmate in reach with a cutter or welder in hand**: no prompt, `interact` is an Adapter no-op, and a forced `begin_harm` is rejected `NOT_ELIGIBLE` (immediate). `IA_REJECT{NOT_ELIGIBLE}`. No accidental harm, no intentional PvP.
- **If the victim walks out of reach mid-act**: at the next block end the geometric test fails and the channel ends `ABORT(OUT_OF_REACH)` *before* that block's damage; damage already dealt stays dealt (D.8: a straight run from d0 = 0 at `V_RUN` costs the victim at most 1200 bp *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: 4 steps at `HARM_BP_PER_STEP` 300; was 2000 bp at the former 500)*). `IA_CHANNEL_ABORT{OUT_OF_REACH}`.
- **If the victim is still inside reach when step `k_trap` lands (17 at the defaults, e = 102)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D36; was 11 and e = 66 at 500 bp)*: integrity falls below `SUIT_LEAK_HP`, the rung goes CRITICAL on the next Suit run and the Controller staggers the victim for `STAGGER_TICKS` (30) where they stand; the block ends in the freeze land (steps 18 to 22 at e = 108 to 132, at least 6600 bp, integrity at most 3400, CRITICAL, walk-only, not FAILED by the stagger alone); a victim who stays in reach is FAILED and DOWNED at step 26 (e = 156). A walking victim of `t_react` 0..60 from `d0` = 0 is out of reach before step 17 (`t_react_max` 65); a running one up to 77; a carrying one up to 29 (otherwise drop and run). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: these windows are for a victim at full integrity (`I0` = `SUIT_HP_FULL`). A wounded victim has a shorter window, `k_trap(I0)` in D.8: at `I0` 7000 a walking victim must be moving within 5 ticks, at `I0` 5200 the first block traps; a repeat attempt on the same victim starts a fresh bar but not a fresh victim. Stated as honest horror, not hidden; Open Question 44.)* The cliff is stated in D.8 and ruled (Open Question 44, resolved); it gates Vertical Slice B, not MVP.
- **If the actor's Sanity query flips to "not below threshold" mid-act**: at the next check tick the channel ends `ABORT(NOT_ELIGIBLE)` before the damage step; at most five further ticks accrue and none of them deals damage.
- **If the victim holds a welder while the act lands**: each step sets the victim's integrity rung from `integrity_bp` on the next Suit run with no hold (SE-23, knob default 1); the victim may self-patch (IA-17) while above `SUIT_HP_FAIL` and may leave (D.8); when death arrives, and whether it does, is Suit's rule (SE-23 / SE-24) — Interaction guarantees only the exogenous damage.
- **If the actor sees the victim as a monster (class (c))**: the command still targets the real ACTOR subject and the prompt reads the equipped tool's ordinary verb (`verb_cut` / `verb_weld`), identical to a crewmate shown as human; a HORROR subject takes no verb at MVP, so the open leak (a prompt on a monster implies a crewmate) is recorded in Open Questions, not hidden. At MVP (single seat) it cannot occur.
- **If a phantom entity is under the aim or cursor**: it is not a subject, never lit, never focused; `interact` is a silent Adapter no-op (no pre-play, no cue). `IA_PHANTOM_PROBE` exists in development builds only — a release counter would be an existence oracle.
- **If a phantom salvage item (a registered `junk_` def) is targeted**: every verb works normally because it is a real item; its truth comes from the Suit weight cue, the scanner `field_check` or the trip home.
- **If a tool is missing for the focused kind**: the prompt shows the verb with the tool glyph (`NEEDS_TOOL`); the press answers immediate `NO_TOOL`, the ack is withdrawn within one frame, dull cue, no shake. `IA_REJECT{NO_TOOL}`.

**F. Save, restore, budgets and co-op seams**

- **If the game is saved while CHANNELLING and restored**: the channel and door attempts are dropped, `channel_ref` is cleared by the Controller and no cue plays; the partial-progress ledger, PAID records, in-flight throws, `loose[]` and `stubs[]` survive (absolute ticks). `IA_RESTORE_DROP`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: a RESERVED record in the snapshot does not survive as such; `post_restore` resolves it by the commit rule from the persisted channel's `blocks_done` (released if 0, consumed if at least 1, in both truth branches). Releasing a RESERVED part with `blocks ≥ 1` at restore would reopen the stock oracle (save at e = 10, restore, read stock). A restore writes no partial-progress ledger entry (IA-16). Under MVP death = restart from the last autosave, an autosave taken during a FULL channel with a credited block therefore costs the part on restore, in both branches: a stated, accepted cost of the RISKY soft gate (SL-5).)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: X7 / D14 / D17: Save/Load's package is accepted — a bound seat's `vitals_state >= CRITICAL` is a third soft-gate input (Save/Load's; capped, then RISKY, no glyph), so a seat channelling while CRITICAL is soft-gated too; the weld, cut and module-cut event autosaves are confirmed (D17), and Interaction exposes the completion-tick DONE hand-offs (`begin_cut` DONE and Assembly's weld and seam-cut DONE) that Save/Load may key them on, the trigger itself being Save/Load's, with Assembly withholding its `ASSEMBLY_CUT` event autosave until the breach is capped; the mid-FULL-channel part cost on restore stands.)*
- **If a restore happens with a throw in flight**: `land_tick` is absolute and persisted, so the stick lands on the same tick with the same `life_ticks`.
- **If `register_subject` returns false during `sync_section` or the window recompute** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, D37: candidate registration moved to the window recompute, hook step 0b)*: the object fails dark and reads as a hallucination; this is a content defect counted `IA_SUBJECT_OVERFLOW`, never a gameplay state, and the lint (D.10) exists to make it unreachable. A dormant section must unregister its subjects before two sections are welded.
- **If the crew sorties to derelict A after the weld (S4 `{home+B, A}`)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D37)*: the loaded `S_int` is 145 against `INTERACT_FIXTURE_BUDGET` 120 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix: Derelict Authoring L_seed B 3, one loose placement fewer)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)*, but only the subjects inside the bound body's registration window are registered, so the lint is on the window (at most 120, measured at the spike, [PLACEHOLDER — derive at spike]) and there is no WARN; at runtime `S_total` ≤ 175 ≤ `MAX_EXPOSURE_SUBJECTS` 192 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass after design review)* whatever the window, so nothing fails dark and no nearest-subject cull applies (Open Question 30, ruled).
- **If a derelict goes dormant with a partial cut in the ledger**: the ledger ages on global ticks, so the entry expires `PARTIAL_TTL_TICKS` (600) after its abort, inside a 945-tick hop leg; a return visit starts the cut fresh (Boarding ASK-BS-I5).
- **If a body transfer (`pod_hop`) or a seat rebind occurs mid-channel**: `ABORT(BODY)`; finished blocks are kept; `carry_ref` travels with the pod (PC-17 / PCI:694).
- **If the target is salvaged by another seat or its section is discarded mid-channel**: `ABORT(TARGET_GONE)` and its ledger entries are deleted. `IA_CHANNEL_ABORT{TARGET_GONE}`.
- **If a downed crewmate is not revived within `DOWN_TO_DEATH_TICKS` (Vertical Slice B)**: the seat is DEAD; in the solo MVP death restarts from the newest verified autosave (SL-7). `begin_revive` is dormant at MVP and tested with stub actors only.
- **If the ledger is full (`MAX_PARTIAL_ENTRIES`)**: the entry with the smallest `abort_tick` (ties: smallest `subject_ref`) is evicted deterministically. `IA_LEDGER_EVICT`.
- **If `TICK_RATE` is ever changed**: every tick-expressed knob here is a tick count, not seconds (except `FULL_JOB_GRACE_S`, converted by `ticks_for_seconds`); each must be re-derived and re-linted (I-2, I-4).

## Dependencies

*Status is the Glob of `design/gdd/` on 2026-10-26 (see Overview), re-globbed on 2026-10-27 in the design review: 26 GDDs, all Designed, only Onboarding (Cryo Bay) undesigned. "Direction" reads from this GDD's side: **depends on** = Interaction needs it; **depended on by** = it needs Interaction; **both** = each needs the other. "Hard" = the system cannot be built or played without it; "Soft" = degrades gracefully. Index edges that the systems index omits are marked and are **flagged, not fixed** — `systems-index.md` is not this stage's to edit (Cross-References carries the refresh row for `/map-systems`).*

**Bidirectional obligations (each neighbour's own contract, answered).** *Kernel:* KRN:204 (verb commands, domain reason codes, the P4 cost constraint, phantom-target Open Question 14) and KRN:691 (Hard; "Submit, domain reason codes, friendly-harm gate order") — answered by IA-3, IA-10 to IA-13 and IA-31. *Graph:* LSG:271 and LSG:861 (the verb → mutator list routed; "the commands themselves, channel timing, lit-only gating") — answered by IA-9, IA-14, IA-17 to IA-27. *Controller:* PCI:205 and PCI:725 (declaration, immediate-versus-deferred classes, abort persistence, phantom behaviour), and the Controller's stubs (`BUSY`, `BLOCKED`, `OUT_OF_CHARGES`, `CLOSING_BLOCKED`, `anchor_o` defaults, drop placement, partial-progress persistence, the 6-tick re-check) — answered by IA-4, IA-7, IA-10, IA-16, IA-24, IA-28, IA-29, IA-41. *Emitter State:* EM:326 and EM:948 (`NOT_LIT` is Interaction's reason; the throw; the landing hook; "`lit(ref)` is the only gate on prompts and verbs, including phantom targets") — answered by IA-6, IA-13, IA-29, IA-37. *Item DB:* IDB:470, IDB:864, IDB-11, IDB-20, AC-38 / 40 / 89 — answered by IA-18, IA-34. *Pipeline:* PIPE:299 — answered by IA-41. *Save/Load:* SL-7, SL:191 — answered by IA-36. *Failure & Trail:* FT:316 — answered by IA-1 and IA-39 (no data crosses). Reciprocal rows owed in the neighbours are listed in Cross-References.

**Engine dependencies and gaps (Godot 4.7.2; facts only from `docs/engine-reference/godot/`; no ADR exists yet).** The reference has `VERSION.md`, `breaking-changes.md` and `modules/` (animation, audio, input, navigation, networking, physics, rendering, ui); **there is no `scripting.md`, `core.md` or `gameplay.md`**, so no GDScript language behaviour is asserted by this GDD and nothing below describes a class, signal or node in a rule (rules stay behavioural). Post-cutoff items this design touches, each tagged -> ADR candidate: (1) 4.7 typed-return overrides — an override of a method with a typed return inherits the type and needs an explicit `return` on every path, which bites any abstract handler base with typed `validate()` / `apply()`; (2) 4.7 packed-array element writes no longer call the property setter, so dirty-key marking in `interaction.state` must be explicit in the mutators, never in setters; (3) 4.7 `InputEvent.device` is `DEVICE_ID_MOUSE` / `DEVICE_ID_KEYBOARD`, no longer `0` — the Adapter's glyph and last-device rule must classify by event type (not a simulation concern, flagged for the prompt glyph); (4) 4.6 dual focus — a prompt plate or bar built from focusable controls must be non-focusable, because the in-world focus model is custom; (5) 4.7 `CanvasItem` line drawing adds no antialiasing feather, so a bar or needle drawn with line primitives must be widened or drawn as filled rectangles on the art-pixel grid (art bible HR-12 stroke floor); (6) `duplicate()` is shallow — snapshots of the nested ledgers need a deep copy. **Not sourceable in the pinned reference** (flagged, not used): `Input.get_vector` dead-zone semantics, character-body motion calls, `_process` versus the kernel tick ordering, autoload behaviour, `Resource` versus `RefCounted` serialisation, the progress-bar widget choice. ADRs needed before build: the input-adapter ADR, the kernel tick-source ADR, the state-block representation ADR, the handler-base ADR (typed returns, `@abstract`), the Layout integer-geometry query ADR (throw clipping, door overlap, drop placement), the integer direction table for throws (no runtime trigonometry), and the render-time clock source for the hold bar under pause. Determinism: every Interaction predicate and formula is integer-only and reads `body.state.pos` and the Emitter State table; Godot Physics 2D makes no determinism claim (CR-13), so no physics query is permitted anywhere in this system.

## Tuning Knobs

*Every Interaction knob is [PLACEHOLDER] until a playtest spike confirms it, lives in data under `assets/data/interaction/` (never hardcoded; gameplay values are data-driven), is a tick count unless the unit says otherwise (changing `TICK_RATE` invalidates every tick-expressed knob), and reads no sanity, Attention, rhythm, breach or device input (IA-4 lint). **★ = registry candidate** (a cross-system fact other GDDs cite or must satisfy); an unstarred knob is Interaction-internal and stays in this GDD's data. Knobs another GDD owns are cited with a pointer, never duplicated. Build relations among knobs are the invariants I-1 to I-9 (Core Rules, Group H); ranges are jointly linted through I-1 to I-9, so a value legal alone may be refused with the others at their defaults; and the registry-candidates stage reads the final text.*

**Interaction-owned knobs**

| Knob | Default | Unit | Safe range | Too high | Too low | Interacts with |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ★ `PATCH_CHANNEL_TICKS` | 180 | ticks (3 s) | 120-300, multiple of 6 (I-2), and < `FULL_CHANNEL_TICKS` (I-9) | patching is avoided; fixing feels punishing | a patch is risk-free noise-wise, no reason to cancel-to-peek | D.1, D.11, D.9; `PATCH_DECAY_S` 240 (a 3 s fix of a 4 min hold, *amended 2026-10-27 — design review:* the real re-leak is about 440 s after a full bar and about 250 s after a patch cancelled at the hiss, because the Graph's wear starts after `PATCH_DECAY_S` and takes about 200 s to cross `LEAK_HP`; Open Question 41); `FULL_CHANNEL_TICKS` (I-9); Controller OQ 7 cites 180 |
| `FULL_CHANNEL_TICKS` | 360 | ticks (6 s) | 240-600, multiple of 6, and ≤ 2 × `BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS` (480 at the default; 600 only with `BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS` 300) (I-9) | tedious; the held part is tied up long | the part cost is not matched by time; hold shorter than the oracle window | D.7 (the commit rule's `blocks`; `until` is retired, *amended 2026-10-27 — design review*), I-9, `BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS` |
| `CAP_CHANNEL_TICKS` | 90 | ticks | 60-180 | salvage feels punitive | stub hiss is trivial to cap | V8 loop; D.11 (the 60-tick floor is the lowest `D` in the document, D.3 / D.11 ranges) *(row amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the stray "D.1 not used" is removed)* |
| `CRANK_CHANNEL_TICKS` | 240 | ticks (4 s) | 120-480 | restart noise burst too long | SCRAM reset is trivial | D.3 `STILL_HOT` reveal cost, `BAR_LONG_FROM_TICKS` |
| ★ `SALVAGE_CHANNEL_TICKS` | 240 | ticks (4 s) | 180-480 | salvage is grinding | salvage is a tap; a free faucet risk | LOUD completion, Assembly's section-cut figure (the Controller's "cut 240" is ambiguous between the two; Assembly's stays Assembly's) |
| ★ `FIELD_CHECK_TICKS` | 120 | ticks (2 s) | **90-240** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: was 60-240, below the registered `FIELD_CHECK_FLOOR_TICKS` 90; the lower bound is now that floor, and a registry request corrects the registered range)* | nobody checks | a check is nearly free (breaks the P4 cost rule) | Sanity's scanner economics (floor rationale `FIELD_CHECK_FLOOR_TICKS` 90, registry, Sanity OQ-SN-13), D.9 (noise class TOOL) |
| ★ `HARM_BP_PER_STEP` | 300 | bp per block end (Suit units) | 250-312 (D.8 derivation) | the escape cliff returns: above 312 a walking victim with `t_react` up to 60 can no longer beat `t_trap` | the channel passes 240 ticks (`n_h` over 40) and Sanity's gap relation tightens | D.8, I-6, Suit SE-23, Sanity's gap relation; any change needs creative-director review (anti-pillar 1) *(added 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D36)* |
| ★ `HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS` | 204 (derived: `B * ceil(HARM_TOTAL_BP / HARM_BP_PER_STEP)`) | ticks (3.4 s) | 198-240 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: was 60-240, unreachable at `HARM_TOTAL_BP` 10000; D.8)* | never lands on anyone who can move | a stab: combat-like, violates PC-12 | D.8, Suit limiter holds (SE-23), I-6; no longer tuned directly; any change needs creative-director review (anti-pillar 1) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: D36, was 120 and an input; Sanity's `SAN_HARM_EXIT - SAN_HARM_ENTER >= ceil(HARM_CHANNEL_TICKS / 12) x max(...)` now needs 17 x 36 = 612 where it needed 10 x 36 = 360)* |
| ★ `HARM_TOTAL_BP` | 10000 | bp (Suit units) | `SUIT_HP_FULL` (I-6) to `40 * HARM_BP_PER_STEP` (the `n_h <= 40` lint; 12000 at the default step) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: upper bound added)* | n/a (clamped by Suit) | the act cannot kill: breaks the user's lethal-friendly-fire decision | D.8, Suit SE-22 / SE-23, `HARM_BP_PER_STEP` derived; any change needs creative-director review (anti-pillar 1) |

## Visual/Audio Requirements

*Lane.* Interaction draws and sounds nothing itself (IA-1, kernel CR-10): every pixel below is drawn by the HUD, the character and tool renderer, or the neighbour that Interaction's commands cause; this section states constraints on those drawers, not pixel art, shaders or sound design (technical-artist, audio-director). Source: the art-director consult (`AD-n` rulings, `V-n` events, `R-n` review checks, `F-n` flags), integrated with the ui-programmer's proposal (`UI-n`). Art bible citations are `ART x.y` and `HR-n` (7.7). Pixel ownership of the prompt and bar is the HUD GDD (undesigned): values marked *proposed* need its sign-off.

**Art-director rulings (outcomes only; the reasoning is in the stage spec file).** (1) AD-1: the NEEDS_TOOL plate stays opaque Ground `#1C2128` in all three `prompt_state` values; NEEDS_TOOL is a notched chamfer plus an appended 16x16 tool glyph on a taller plate (about 18 art px against 12). (2) AD-4: the abort break-notch is held for `BAR_HOLD_DONE_TICKS` 12. (3) AD-5: the first plate candidate is beyond the subject on the line from the player through the anchor, then above, below, right, left; the chest keep-out is never overlapped. (4) AD-2: the bar lengths below are the fill-able interior length.

### Events, visual feedback, audio class and priority

Priority: **P** = required for the MVP slice, **V** = Vertical Slice, **A** = Alpha.

| Event or state | Visual feedback | Audio class (existence and sync only) | Pri |
|---|---|---|---|
| `prompt_state` ENABLED (V-1) | Opaque Ground plate, Graphite-lit `#5C6670` chamfered border, keycap plus state-neutral verb (`verb_cut`, `verb_weld`, `verb_breaker`, `verb_valve`, `verb_door`, `verb_vent_hatch`), one 4x3 art px anchor tab on the edge nearest the anchor to say which of up to `MAX_PROMPT_CANDIDATES_IN_REACH` is focused. Cut in after the HUD debounce; no fade, slide, bounce or scale. No outline or glow on the world object. Never names condition or target | none | P |
| NEEDS_TOOL (V-2) | AD-1 plate; tool glyph Paper White (Tungsten marks only the *equipped* tool, ART 7.2); depends only on object kind and the actor's tool, so it is not an oracle | none | P |
| HIDDEN (V-3) | Nothing drawn; hide is immediate, same render frame, no dwell. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: reworded. The plate **hides within one render frame of a committed `lit(ref)` false** and **shows after the HUD debounce**; it does not appear "exactly with the object". `lit(ref)` is a per-subject, per-tick, hysteretic bit (`LIT_ON_PM` 150 on, `LIT_OFF_PM` 120 off) while the object's visibility comes from the per-pixel light, so the object can be seen a frame before the plate and the plate can outlast the object's last lit pixel by the hysteresis band; a late plate omits a hint and never lies, and a hide is never late. Measured by AC-IA-134 (focus-to-plate latency at breach time) and AC-IA-137 (hide-immediately).)* | none | P |
| Press, ACK pre-play (V-4) | Keycap face drops 1 art px; bar track appears at fill 0 at the class length; for a CHANNEL the body shows only the first work-loop pose (tool raised), reversible by a cut in one render frame | `ia_start` for a CHANNEL with a short attack ramp so a withdrawal fades, never clicks; a TAP plays nothing at the press | P |
| CONFIRMED, PROGRESS (V-5) | Stepped fill `fill_px = floor(progress_pm x L / 1000)`, needle-mark on the leading edge, 6-frame two-handed work loop **not scrubbed by progress**, tool `use` frames; never eased | `ia_loop` (tool loop) | P |
| Cancel, hold released (V-6; `ended_by` = `PLAYER_CANCEL`, IA-41) | Bar drains right to left at `CONFIRM_DRAIN_RATE` x fill speed (cosmetic: the sim channel already ended); a new ACK cuts it; body returns to idle by a cut. Reduced motion: snap to empty | `ia_cancel` ("stopped") | P |
| Abort, any world cause (V-7; `ended_by` = `WORLD`; never keyed on `abort_cause`) | Fill snaps empty, needle removed, break-notch (2x2 art px Ground cut in the track's upper border) held 12 ticks then cut. One frame set for every cause; a stagger or down abort additionally shows the Controller's stagger or down frames. A restore plays no abort frame | `ia_abort` ("interrupted") | P |
| DONE (V-8) | Bar fills, holds 12 ticks, is cut; work loop ends by a cut. **No checkmark, flash, sparkle or "repaired" text** | `ia_done` | P |
| OUTCOME, deferred reveal (V-9) | Bar fills exactly as DONE (identical frames to the terminal tick), then is removed at once; the plate dims one step (keycap and verb to Graphite lit) for 12 ticks *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the tint applies to the keycap fill and the border only; the verb text stays Paper White, at least 4.5:1, and the tint is now used by the OUTCOME dim alone, see `press_blocked_moving`, IA-41)*; no text says why | `ia_nothing` ("nothing happened") | P |
| Immediate reject (V-10) | Ack withdrawn within one render frame: bar removed, keycap restored, plate to its prior state. No shake, flash, red or text; the same frame set for every reason. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the restored keycap is held in the one-step Graphite-lit state for `BAR_REJECT_HOLD_TICKS` so the reject is perceivable, with at most one reject presentation per seat per `PROMPT_MIN_CYCLE_TICKS`; while the actor is moving, `press_blocked_moving` already dims the plate before the press.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the reject hold is the inset keycap shape, not a tint; the tint belongs to V-9 alone; a reject while moving shows the hollow and inset shapes together, IA-41.)* | `ia_no` (dull) | P |
| Hold-to-confirm, V6 (V-11) | Same component at class SHORT, in both directions; destructive is signalled by the hold, not by hue (no Alarm Red, no warning mark, no dialog). Frozen under pause (AD-9). Tap-to-confirm "armed" (AD-10): needle parked at the right end and the plate bezel thickened 1 art px; expiry removes both by a cut | `ia_start` / `ia_cancel` as a channel; LOUD one-shot at apply | P |
| Pause (V-12) | Prompt and bar freeze; the world is not dimmed, blurred or desaturated (ART 7.4) | none | P |
| Carry state (V-13) | Shared carried-module sprite held below or overhead, never across the chest lamp; the drop prompt is `G . Drop` anchored at the foot origin under the AD-5 keep-outs; a compatible HARDPOINT (`install_module`) and the POD_CONSOLE (`pod_hop`) keep their ordinary plates (the carry-admitted rows, IA-28), and a lit door face keeps its ordinary V4 plate *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; every other world target shows no prompt. The beam stays on | none | P |

The lamp is module-body and may lie but must not change on the press. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: a power-neutral latch-bolt strain on the door frame (a 2-frame state of the frame's latch, not the leaf and not the lamp) is shown for the whole window in both branches, so a player who cannot hear still perceives that the press registered; it carries no information about power; confirmed 2026-10-27 as part of the user's D38 ruling.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* | ~~motor whine through the window~~ **a latch or clutch strain through the window, then the manual-brake release clack at its end, identical for both branches; the motor sound plays only when the leaf actually travels (Open Question 43)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review, Pillar 1: an unpowered door must not be heard spinning a motor)* | P |
